# Mutation Port Dossiers — implementation reference

> Generated by a 4-agent inventory sweep (2026-08-14), ~900k tokens,
> 358 tool calls. Companion to `MUTATIONS-TO-SKILLS-MIGRATION.md`.
> Purpose: M1/M2 can be implemented from these dossiers without
> re-reading the original mutation sources.
>
> **Honesty bound:** these are agent-produced and were spot-checked, not
> exhaustively re-verified line by line. Treat file:line citations as
> high-confidence but confirm before relying on an exact number.



======================================================================
# Part 1
======================================================================

> **NOTE:** FALSE-PREMISE CORRECTION vs the plan (F5). The plan says 'MP is a dead currency. +1 granted per level.' The grant line exists (LevelingSystem.cs:65) but it is a NO-OP: `Entity.GainMP` (Entity.cs:139-149) only works when the entity already has an MP stat, and the Player blueprint has none. Verified three ways — `grep -rn '"Name": "MP"' Assets/Resources/` returns 0 hits; the Player's Stats array (Objects.json:894-936) lists Hitpoints/Str/Agi/Tou/Level/Experience/SP/DV/Ego; and the inheritance chain Player→Creature→PhysicalObject was walked programmatically with no MP at any level. The observable consequence is that the sidebar renders `MP -` today (SidebarStateBuilder.cs:46 passes fallback "-"). MP is not just unspendable — it is never granted and never displayed as a number. The plan's conclusion (retire it) is right; its reasoning understates the case.

> **NOTE:** 🔴 THE SINGLE BIGGEST MIGRATION HAZARD IS NOT MENTIONED IN THE PLAN. `InventoryUI.cs:3443` filters the grimoire picker with `GrimoireTooltipData.IsGrimoireMutation(ab.SourceMutationClass)`, and `SkillsPart.cs:134` registers every skill ability with `sourceMutationClass: ""`. Delete mutations without re-keying and the picker becomes permanently empty — the Enter/Space/number-key binding flow on the inventory Abilities tab (InventoryUI.cs:3182-3207) silently does nothing. Five more readers (InventoryUI 3300/3334/3655, InputHandler 3871, HotbarStateBuilder 24) degrade to name-only rows, losing the 21 hand-written flavor/mechanics/signature records. Cheapest fix: rename the field to `SourceClass`, have SkillsPart pass `skill.GetType().Name`, and re-key GrimoireTooltipData's 21 entries to the new skill class names. Add this to M3 or M4 explicitly.

> **NOTE:** 🔴 PLAN DECISION D2's `Cost: -1` SENTINEL IS AN SP FAUCET. `BuySkillAction.cs:172` executes `spStat.BaseValue -= cost` with no lower guard, so a negative Cost GRANTS SP. The default `Cost = -999` (SkillData.cs:62 / PowerData.cs:28) is worse: one Enter press on a Cost-less row grants +999 SP. `SkillRegistry.LoadFromJson` (SkillRegistry.cs:163-210) validates only `Class` — the 'the registry will warn' claim in SkillData.cs:59-61 is doc-vs-impl drift with zero implementation. All shipped content is safe today (12 trees + 67 powers, every Cost present and >0: 71×1, 4×50, 3×100, 1×25), so this is latent, not live. Fix independent of the migration: add the missing Cost warning to LoadFromJson, and a `if (cost < 0)` reject in both BuySkillAction.Execute and SkillsScreenStateBuilder.EvaluateRowState.

> **NOTE:** TEACH-ONLY NODES: DEFINITIVELY YES in the current schema and UI, with zero code changes — but not the way the plan proposes. Use `"Requires": "<sentinel that is no class>"` + `"Flags": 2` (FLAG_OBFUSCATED). The row renders '???' in dark gray, is unbuyable (MissingPrereq), and correctly flips to the real name + 'owned' once a grimoire teaches it, because EvaluateRowState checks isOwned first (SkillsScreenStateBuilder.cs:124). This works because `SkillsPart.AddSkill` (SkillsPart.cs:88-143) consults the registry for NOTHING — it checks only duplicate-by-Type. The one rough edge is the failure message 'You must learn <sentinel> first.' A ~10-line `TeachOnly` bool on SkillData/PowerData gets the right message and is the better answer; I'd recommend it over the sentinel.

> **NOTE:** PRE-EXISTING BUG in the purchase flow, unrelated to the migration but in scope of what I was asked to trace: `SkillsScreenUI.FormatFailure` case InsufficientSP (SkillsScreenUI.cs:209-210) prints `r.CostPaid`, which is only assigned on the success path (BuySkillAction.cs:174). The player sees 'You need 0sp to learn Whirlwind (3 available).' Fix: add a `Cost` field to BuySkillAction.Result set right after registry resolution, or have the UI use `row.Cost`.

> **NOTE:** PRE-EXISTING GAMEPLAY HOLE: `GrimoireChargePart.Refill()` and `ChargesPerVial` have ZERO production callers (exhaustive grep). There is no way to re-ink a grimoire — every rite book is a hard 10-cast consumable, directly contradicting its own docstring's 'cheap re-inking' (GrimoireChargePart.cs:20-22). Compounding the confusion, `InkVialPart` (Items/InkVialPart.cs:40) refills `RentalSystem`'s Ink WALLET (Entity.IntProps["Ink"], the weapon-rental currency), not book charges. Two unrelated systems share the word 'ink'. M2 is the natural place to close this, but it is a scope decision for the user, not an assumption.

> **NOTE:** PRE-EXISTING COVERAGE GAP, measured: 27 grimoire blueprints teach a mutation but GrimoireTooltipData has only 21 records. Six taught mutations — ChillDraftMutation, ConjureWaterMutation, DryingBreezeMutation, HearthwarmMutation, KindleFlameMutation, WardGleamMutation — are ALREADY invisible in the grimoire picker today. GrimoireTooltipData.cs:48-55 documents exactly this hazard and :134-139 records that the 11 rites were retro-fixed after a cold-eye audit; these six utility spells were missed. The migration is the moment to close it or delete the picker.

> **NOTE:** SCOPE CORRECTION on plan F1/F2: the 14 'unreachable' classes are unreachable in SHIPPING play, but six of them (FireBolt, IceShard, PoisonSpit, PrismaticBeam, FrostNova, ChainLightning) ARE granted in DevMode via GameBootstrap.GrantShowcaseSpellMutations (GameBootstrap.cs:1033-1069, called at :331 behind `if (DevMode.Enabled)`). Deleting them removes the dev sandbox's only projectile-FX showcase. Trivial to re-point at skill classes, but don't be surprised when the dev path goes quiet.

> **NOTE:** SIZING WARNING for M1/M2: the skills screen already renders 79 rows (12 trees + 67 powers) into a 14-row viewport (SkillsScreenUI.cs:51) with single-row-step navigation only — no PageUp/PageDown, no home/end, no collapse (HandleInput, :134-174). Adding 29 ported powers plus a Rites tree takes it to ~108 rows, i.e. ~8 screenfuls of one-row-at-a-time scrolling. This is a UX cliff the plan doesn't budget for. Add paging or per-tree collapse in the same milestone.

> **NOTE:** OPPORTUNITY, not a risk: `ActivatedAbility.Class` has exactly one reader — AbilityManagerStateBuilder.cs:61/74-81, which sorts by it and shows it as a 12-char column (AbilityManagerUI.cs:336, TruncateClass :392-399). Post-migration every value collapses to "Skills" (except Persuasion's two) and the sort becomes a no-op. Setting `ActivatedAbilitySpec.Class` to the TREE name instead ('Pyromancy', 'Cryomancy', 'Rites', ...) is a one-line-per-skill change, needs no UI edit, fits the 12-char budget (longest tree name is 'ShortBlades' at 11), and gives better grouping than mutations ever did.

> **NOTE:** `GrimoireInk` (Gameplay/Mutations/GrimoireInk.cs) is the one file in the Mutations folder with zero mutation dependencies — it takes an Entity and returns a GrimoireChargePart. Move it to Gameplay/Magic/ with `git mv`, don't rewrite it. Conversely `ConsumingRiteBase` carries two invariants that MUST survive the port to a skill base: ink is checked AFTER target gathering so a whiffed rite never burns a charge (ConsumingRiteBase.cs:129-137), and `OnCastResolved` fires unconditionally for caster-side rewards because `ApplyPayoff` is gated on target survival (:56-71 — the Bloodletter's-Ledger heal bug is documented right there).

> **NOTE:** OBSERVABILITY GAP to close during the port: GrimoirePart.DoRead emits no diag record on any branch (taught / already-known / no-part / unknown-class). Per CLAUDE.md's 'every gate that can reject emits a record' rule this should have one. The natural shape is `category="skill" kind="Added" source="grimoire"` for success (SkillsPart.AddSkill already emits `skill/Added` with a `source` field, so passing `source: "grimoire"` gets it free) plus a `skill/GrimoireRejected` with a `reason` for the three failure branches.

> **NOTE:** HONESTY BOUNDS. (a) Everything above is from reading actual source; every file:line was opened, not inferred, and the blueprint/JSON counts (32 grimoire blueprints, 27 with MutationClassName, 11 with GrimoireCharge, 12 trees + 67 powers, 21 tooltip records, Cost distribution) were computed by parsing the JSON, not by eye. (b) I did NOT run the EditMode suite or enter Play mode — the claim 'the sidebar renders MP -' is derived from source (no MP stat + FindStat fallback at SidebarStateBuilder.cs:46), not observed on screen; it is the one assertion worth confirming live in 10 seconds. (c) I did not audit the 47 mutation test files individually — the census is filename-level (`grep -rln`), so the per-file port cost in plan risk #2 is still unmeasured. (d) I did not verify runtime reachability of the 27 grimoire blueprints in worldgen (TradeStockBuilder / VillagePopulationBuilder reference GrimoirePart but I did not trace their spawn tables).


## Character creation — there is none (no chargen step for mutations OR skills)

VERDICT: **the game has no character-creation flow at all.** No mutation step, no skill step, no attribute allocation, no stat-point buy. There is no `CharacterCreation*`/`Chargen*` type anywhere in `Assets/Scripts/` (exhaustive grep returned zero hits). The only pre-play menu is `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/UI/BootMenuController.cs:25-95` — a two-key modal ([C]ontinue / [N]ew game, `BootMenuController.cs:27-28`) that only appears when a save exists (`TryActivate`, :38-46). Pressing N just sets `IsActive=false` and logs 'Starting a new game.' (:82-85).

What actually substitutes for chargen, in order, all inside `GameBootstrap.SetupPlayer` (`Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Bootstrap/GameBootstrap.cs:312-352`):
1. `_factory.CreateEntity("Player")` (:315) — the Player blueprint at `Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json` supplies ALL starting character state: parts `ActivatedAbilities` (:860-862), `Skills` with **empty Params** (:864-867), `Mutations` with `StartingMutations = "FlamingHandsMutation:1,CalmMutation:1"` (:868-876); stats Hitpoints 40, Str/Agi/Tou 18, Level 1, Experience 0, **SP 0 (Min 0, Max 999)** (:925-931), DV 0, Ego 16; IntProps `Drams=50`, `Ink=50`.
2. `if (DevMode.Enabled)` → `GrantShowcaseSpellMutations()` (:331) else `NewGameLoadout.Grant(...)` (:339).
3. `GivePlayerFarmingStarterKit()` (:343) — includes `WateringGrimoire`.
4. `GivePlayerStartingSpells()` (:344) → `StartingSpellKit.GrantAll` (:1130).

So the *skill* half of 'chargen' is `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/StartingSpellKit.cs:48-54`, which hard-codes four class names — `Pyromancy_EmberSpit`, `Hydromancy_JetBlast`, `Galvanism_GroundSurge`, `Cryomancy_RimeGrip` — and grants them via `skills.AddSkill(SpellClasses[i], source: "starting-kit")` (:75). It early-returns 0 if the actor has no `SkillsPart` (:69-70). Runs on BOTH dev and normal paths.

The *mutation* half is two-pronged: (a) `MutationsPart.StartingMutations` parsed in `MutationsPart.cs:46-57` (`Initialize` splits on ',' then ':'), granting FlamingHands + Calm; (b) dev-only `GameBootstrap.GrantShowcaseSpellMutations()` (:1033-1069), which grants FireBolt/IceShard/PoisonSpit/PrismaticBeam/FrostNova/ChainLightning — **these six are the ONLY runtime acquisition path for 6 of the 14 'unreachable' classes, and it is DevMode-gated**, so the audit's '14 have no acquisition path' is correct for shipping play but the dev sandbox does reach 6 of them.

Presentation-layer registry grep (as requested): `MutationRegistry` appears in Presentation exactly once — `GameBootstrap.cs:187` (`MutationRegistry.EnsureInitialized` inside `PerformanceDiagnostics.MeasureStartupPhase("InitializeMutations", PerformanceMarkers.Bootstrap.InitializeMutations, ...)`, Step 2/9 at :183-187). `SkillRegistry` appears in Presentation exactly once — `SkillsScreenStateBuilder.cs:46` (`SkillRegistry.GetAllSkills()`). SkillRegistry is never explicitly initialized at bootstrap; it lazy-loads on first access (`SkillRegistry.EnsureInitialized`, `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/SkillRegistry.cs:74-96`) and is force-reset every Play-mode entry by `[RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod(SubsystemRegistration)] ResetOnPlayStart` (:144-152). MutationRegistry has no such reset — it is only initialized from the bootstrap call.

WHAT CHANGES: delete `GameBootstrap.cs:183-187` (the Step 2/9 block + the `PerformanceMarkers.Bootstrap.InitializeMutations` marker), delete `GrantShowcaseSpellMutations` (:1033-1069) and its call site (:331), delete the `Mutations` part block from the Player blueprint (Objects.json:868-876). Move any of the 6 showcase powers you still want dev-reachable into a skill-class list. `GivePlayerStartingSpells` and `StartingSpellKit` need no change. If you want a real chargen step later, note there is currently no UI scaffold to hang it on — `BootMenuController` is a keypress poll, not a screen.


## LevelingSystem — the +1 MP / +1 SP grants

`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Stats/LevelingSystem.cs`. `CheckLevelUp(Entity, Zone)` (:43-87) runs a `while (xpStat.Value >= XPToNextLevel(levelStat.Value))` loop (:49) — so multi-level jumps grant multiple MP/SP. Per iteration: XP debit (:52), `levelStat.BaseValue++` (:53), `hp.Max += 2; hp.BaseValue = hp.Max` (:57-62), then:
- **MP grant, :64-65**: `// +1 MP for mutation advancement` / `entity.GainMP(1);`
- **SP grant, :67-74**: `var spStat = entity.GetStat("SP"); if (spStat != null) spStat.BaseValue += 1;` — comment explicitly says it 'Mirrors the HP / MP grants above'.
Then a ring-wave FX (:76-83) and `MessageLog.AddAnnouncement($"You advance to level {newLevel}!")` (:85).

Entry point: `AwardKillXP(killer, victim, zone)` (:24-38) reads `victim.GetStatValue("XPValue", 0)`, bails if ≤0 or if killer lacks an `Experience` stat, adds to `xpStat.BaseValue`, logs 'You gain N XP.', then calls `CheckLevelUp`. Called from `CombatSystem.HandleDeath` per the docstring at :25-26.

**Asymmetry to note for the migration:** the SP grant writes `spStat.BaseValue += 1` directly and is null-guarded; the MP grant delegates to `Entity.GainMP` which fires a `GainedMP` game event (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Entities/Entity.cs:139-149`). That event has exactly one production listener — `IrritableGenomeMutation.HandleEvent` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/IrritableGenomeMutation.cs:39-42`). Deleting the MP grant therefore also deletes the only production `GainedMP` emission.

WHAT CHANGES: delete `LevelingSystem.cs:64-65`. If the user chooses +2 SP/level (plan D4 open question 1), change `:74` to `spStat.BaseValue += 2`. Tests that must move: `Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Entities/LevelingSystemTests.cs:44` and `:58` (`Assert.AreEqual(3, player.GetStatValue("MP"))`), `:136-137` (npc MP fixture), `:158` (the `CreatePlayer(..., int mp, int sp = 0)` helper signature — the `mp` param and the `Statistics["MP"]` line at :158 both go).


## MP — complete end-to-end census; MP is not merely unspendable, it DOES NOT EXIST on the player

**HEADLINE (contradicts plan F5's framing, strengthens its conclusion): the Player never has an MP stat, so the +1/level grant is a silent no-op in real play.**

Proof chain:
1. `Entity.GainMP(int)` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Entities/Entity.cs:139-149`) is `if (Statistics.TryGetValue("MP", out Stat stat)) {...; return true;} return false;` — it does NOT create the stat.
2. `grep -rn '"Name": "MP"' Assets/Resources/` → **0 hits**. The Player blueprint's `Stats` array (`Objects.json:894-936`) lists Hitpoints, Strength, Agility, Toughness, Level, Experience, SP, DV, Ego. No MP.
3. Inheritance chain checked programmatically: `Player` → `Creature` (stats: Hitpoints, Strength, Agility, Toughness, Intelligence, Willpower, Ego, Speed, XPValue) → `PhysicalObject` (no stats). No MP anywhere.
4. No production code writes `Statistics["MP"]` (grep: zero hits outside tests).
∴ every level-up calls `GainMP(1)` → `TryGetValue` fails → returns false, no event, no stat.

**Every production reader of "MP":**
- `Entity.cs:131-134` `CanGainMP()` → `Statistics.ContainsKey("MP")`. Zero production callers (only `MutationSystemTests.cs:1041`).
- `Entity.cs:139-149` `GainMP(int)` — sole production caller `LevelingSystem.cs:65`.
- `Entity.cs:154-167` `UseMP(int, string context)` — writes `stat.Penalty += amount`, fires `UsedMP` with `Amount`+`Context`. Production callers: `MutationsPart.cs:221` (SpendMPToIncreaseMutation), `:480` (BuyRandomMutation/Irritable branch, context "BuyNew"), `:529` (BuyRandomMutation), `:1024` (TryRandomBuy). All four are inside MutationsPart.
- `MutationsPart.cs:216` (`GetStatValue("MP",0) < 1` guard), `:467`, `:469`, `:520`, `:522`, `:582`, `:589`, `:592`, `:619`, `:1017`.
- `IrritableGenomeMutation.cs:53,56` — reads MP to auto-spend.
- `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Inventory/InventoryScreenData.cs:161` — `"MP"` is entry #4 in the `StatPriority[]` array (:157-168) that orders the character-sheet stat list; `:240` `case "mp": return "MP";` in `GetStatLabel`.
- `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Rendering/SidebarStateBuilder.cs:46` — `ComposeDualLine("HP", FindStat(...,"HP","0"), "MP", FindStat(..., "MP", "-"))`. `FindStat` (:95-108) returns the fallback when the label isn't in `PlayerStats`, so **the live sidebar renders `HP 40/40 | MP -` today.**

**Every MP spend path, and why all are unreachable:**
- `MutationsPart.SpendMPToIncreaseMutation(BaseMutation, string)` (:206-222) and the string overload (:224-231). Non-test production callers: only `MutationsPart.TryRandomRankIncrease` (:1012).
- `MutationsPart.BuyRandomMutation(int,int,Random)` (:460-487), `BuyRandomMutationOption` (:494-500), `BuyRandomMutation(MutationDefinition,...)` (:502-...), `RandomlySpendMutationPoints` (:576-...), `TryRandomRankIncrease` (:996-1013), `TryRandomBuy` (:1015-1025).
- The ONLY non-MutationsPart production callers of any of these are `UnstableGenomeMutation.cs:76,81` (`GetRandomBuyMutationOptions` + `BuyRandomMutationOption(cost: 0)`) and `IrritableGenomeMutation.cs:64` (`RandomlySpendMutationPoints`). Both classes are in the unreachable-14. Additionally `IrritableGenome.TrySpending` (:47-79) requires `MPSpentMemory > 0`, which is only incremented by a prior non-'BuyNew' `UsedMP` (:26-37) — circular, so even if the mutation were granted the path is inert until something else spends MP.

**CONFIRMED: MP is unspendable in normal play — and stronger, it is un-GRANTABLE and never displayed as a number.** The plan's F5 is right; the reason is deeper than stated.

WHAT CHANGES on delete: remove `Entity.cs:126-167` (CanGainMP/GainMP/UseMP block, incl. the `// --- MP Economy ---` banner at :126); `LevelingSystem.cs:64-65`; `InventoryScreenData.cs:161` (drop `"MP",` from StatPriority) and `:240` (drop the `case "mp"`); `SidebarStateBuilder.cs:46` — replace the MP half of the dual line (candidate: `"SP", FindStat(inventoryState, "SP", "0")`, which would finally surface the real currency; note `GetStatLabel`'s default branch at `InventoryScreenData.cs:245-249` already yields "SP" for the SP stat, so `FindStat(...,"SP",...)` works with no other change). Test updates: `SidebarStateBuilderTests.cs:44` asserts the literal `"HP 25/30 | MP 3"`; `:120` and `SidebarRendererTests.cs:333` seed `Statistics["MP"]`. `MutationSystemTests.cs:1004-1090, 1190-1230, 1302-1355` are the MP-economy tests and go with the system.


## SP — complete end-to-end census (the surviving currency)

**Declaration:** Player blueprint only — `Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json:925-931`: `{"Name":"SP","Value":0,"Min":0,"Max":999}`. No other blueprint has it, so NPCs never participate (this is deliberate — see `LevelingSystem.cs:67-71` comment).

**Every production reader of "SP" (grep `"SP"` across Assets/Scripts → 5 hits):**
1. `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/BuySkillAction.cs:117` — `var spStat = actor.GetStat("SP"); if (spStat == null) → FailureReason.ActorMissingSPStat` (:118-122). Then `result.SpBefore = spStat.BaseValue;` (:123) — **BaseValue, unclamped**. Debit at `:172` `spStat.BaseValue -= cost;`. Refund at `:181` on AddSkill rollback.
2. `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Stats/LevelingSystem.cs:72-74` — the +1/level grant.
3. `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Rendering/SkillsScreenStateBuilder.cs:41` — `int sp = actor.GetStatValue("SP", 0);` — **`GetStatValue` returns `Stat.Value`, which IS clamped to [Min,Max] and includes Bonus/Penalty/Boost** (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Stats/Stat.cs:27-38`). Used at `:139` for the InsufficientSP verdict and passed to the snapshot as `CurrentSP` (:102).
4. `Assets/Scripts/Scenarios/Custom/SkillTreeShowcase.cs:40` — `.SetStat("SP", 500)`.
5. Docstring mention at `SkillsScreenStateBuilder.cs:31`.

**BaseValue-vs-Value asymmetry (real, latent):** `BuySkillAction` gates and debits on `BaseValue`; the screen displays and gates on `Value` (clamped [0,999], modified by Bonus/Penalty/Boost). Today they agree because nothing ever sets SP Bonus/Penalty and BaseValue never exceeds 999. Any future SP-boost item, or a negative-Cost row (see the Cost dossier), desyncs them — the header would read 999 while purchases debit a larger true balance, or vice-versa.

**UI display:** SP is NOT in `InventoryScreenData.StatPriority[]` (`InventoryScreenData.cs:157-168`), but `BuildPlayerStats` (:170-220) appends every *remaining* stat alphabetically after the priority block (:191-205), so SP does appear on the character sheet's stats panel. Its label comes from `GetStatLabel`'s default branch (`:245-249`): length ≤ 3 → `ToUpperInvariant()` → "SP". The skills screen shows it explicitly: `SkillsScreenUI.cs:262-263` draws `$"SP: {_snapshot.CurrentSP}"` right-aligned in BrightCyan on the title row.

**Save:** SP is a plain `Stat` in `Entity.Statistics` and round-trips through the generic stat serializer — no MutationsPart-style hand-written saver.

**Tests:** 18 `"SP"` references across `Assets/Tests/`, concentrated in `LevelingSystemTests.cs` (the ST.4 section starting ~:120, incl. `PlayerBlueprint_HasSPStat_WithDefaultZero` and the counter-check `CheckLevelUp_EntityWithoutSPStat_DoesNotCrash` at :120-145) and `SkillsScreenStateBuilderTests.cs`.

WHAT CHANGES: nothing structural. Only if the user picks +2/level (`LevelingSystem.cs:74`). Consider ALSO adding SP to `StatPriority` and/or the sidebar dual-line to replace the vanishing MP slot — the currency that actually exists is currently the less visible of the two.


## SkillsScreenUI + SkillsScreenStateBuilder + BuySkillAction — the full purchase flow

**Entry:** `InputHandler.cs:417-424` — `if (SkillsScreenUI != null && InputHelper.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.X) && PlayerEntity != null) OpenSkillsScreen();`, gated on `InputState.Normal`. `OpenSkillsScreen()` at `:1573-1581` calls `SkillsScreenUI.Open(PlayerEntity)` then sets `_inputState = InputState.SkillsScreenOpen` (enum member declared `:121`). Per-frame dispatch `:505-508` → `HandleSkillsScreenInput()`. Wiring: `GameBootstrap.cs:628-637` (AddComponent, centered popup FG/BG tilemaps from `ZoneRenderer.CenteredPopupFgTilemap`/`CenteredPopupBgTilemap`, `popupOverlayCamera`, then `inputHandler.SkillsScreenUI = skillsScreenUI`). Listed in `ControlsReference.cs:32` as `new Row("X", "skills (spend skill points)")` and in the short hint at `:58`.

**Snapshot build** — `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Rendering/SkillsScreenStateBuilder.Build(Entity)` (:35-103):
- null actor → empty snapshot (:37-38); no `SkillsPart` → empty rows but real SP (:42-43).
- Iterates `SkillRegistry.GetAllSkills()` in registration order (:46). Per tree: emits ONE tree-root row (`IsTreeRoot: true`, `parentSkillName: ""`, :62-70), then one row per `skill.Powers[i]` (`IsTreeRoot: false`, `parentSkillName: skill.Name`, :90-98).
- Tree rows deliberately pass `minimum: ""`, `requires: ""`, `exclusion: ""` (:56-58) — 'skill trees don't carry Minimum in Qud'. `BuySkillAction.cs:90-92` does the same. So a tree's only gates are Cost + Attribute/Minimum (and Attribute-without-Minimum is a no-op, see below).
- **`FLAG_HIDDEN` is never consulted.** Only `FLAG_OBFUSCATED` is (`:134`). A `Hidden` row still renders and is still buyable. Zero JSON content sets `Flags` today (grep across all 12 Skills JSON files → 0 hits), so both flags are dead in content.
- Current content size: **12 trees + 67 powers = 79 rows.**

**Gating evaluator** `EvaluateRowState` (:118-143), ordered to match `BuySkillAction.Execute`:
1. `isOwned` → `Owned`, `isObfuscated=false` (:124-125).
2. `reqsMet = MeetsAttributeMinimum && MeetsRequires && !HasAnyExclusion` (:130-132).
3. `!reqsMet` → `RequirementsNotMet`, obfuscated iff `FLAG_OBFUSCATED` (:136-137).
4. `sp < cost` → `InsufficientSP` (:139-140).
5. else `Buyable` (:142).
Helpers `MeetsAttributeMinimum` (:145-170, pipe=OR-groups / comma=AND-conjuncts, `int.TryParse` failure `continue`s — a malformed Minimum silently passes), `MeetsRequires` (:172-182, all comma-separated classes must be `HasSkill`), `HasAnyExclusion` (:184-194). This logic is **duplicated** from BuySkillAction (acknowledged at `SkillsScreenStateBuilder.cs:20-25`).

**Render** — `SkillsScreenUI.Render()` (:243-335). POPUP_W=60 (:50), POPUP_MAX_VISIBLE=14 (:51), DESC_ROWS=1 (:52). Title 'Skills' + `SP: N` (:261-263). Tree-roots at col 3, powers at col 5 (`:287`). Name color by state (`NameColor`, :337-353): Owned=White, Buyable/InsufficientSP=BrightGreen, RequirementsNotMet=Gray-if-selected/DarkGray. **Cost text: `:295` `if (row.State != Owned && row.Cost > 0)` → `$"{row.Cost}sp"` right-aligned, color by `CostColor` (:355-364, Buyable=BrightCyan, InsufficientSP=BrightRed, ReqsNotMet=DarkGray). Owned rows render the literal `"owned"` in White (:301-309).** One-line description footer for the selected row (:322-330). Hint `"[Enter]buy [^v]nav [X/Esc]close"` (:334).

**Input** — `HandleInput()` (:134-174): X/Esc close (:138-142); Up/K, Down/J move the cursor one row (no page keys, no home/end, no hold-repeat); Return/KeypadEnter → `TryPurchaseSelected()`.

**Purchase** — `TryPurchaseSelected()` (:176-198): `BuySkillAction.Execute(PlayerEntity, row.Class)`; on success `MessageLog.Add($"You learn {display} (-{result.CostPaid}sp).")`; on failure `FormatFailure` (:203-225); then `RefreshSnapshot()` unconditionally (:197). Purchases do NOT end the turn (docstring :21-24).

**`BuySkillAction.Execute(Entity, string)`** (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/BuySkillAction.cs:72-196`), exact order:
1. Registry resolve: `TryGetSkillByClass` first (:86-93, reads Cost+Attribute, blanks Minimum/Requires/Exclusion), else `TryGetPowerByClass` (:94-101, reads all five), else `UnknownSkillClass` (:102-106).
2. `actor?.GetPart<SkillsPart>()` null → `ActorMissingSkillsPart` (:109-114).
3. `actor.GetStat("SP")` null → `ActorMissingSPStat` (:117-122). `SpBefore = spStat.BaseValue` (:123).
4. `skillsPart.HasSkill(class)` → `AlreadyOwned` (:127-132).
5. `SpBefore < cost` → `InsufficientSP` (:135-140).
6. `MeetsAttributeMinimum` → `StatMinNotMet` + failing attr name (:148-153).
7. `MeetsRequires` → `MissingPrereq` + first missing class (:156-161).
8. `HasAnyExclusion` → `Exclusion` + first blocker (:164-169).
9. Commit: `spStat.BaseValue -= cost` (:172), `CostPaid = cost` (:174), `skillsPart.AddSkill(className, source: "purchase")` (:176). If AddSkill returns false: refund `+= cost`, `CostPaid = 0`, return `AlreadyOwned` with `Detail="lifecycle-hook-rejected"` (:177-191).
Every branch emits `diag category="skill" kind="PurchaseAttempted"` with `{skillClass, succeeded, reason, detail, costPaid, spBefore, spAfter}` (`EmitDiag`, :297-314), gated on `Diag.IsChannelEnabled("skill")`.

**BUG FOUND (pre-existing, in the purchase flow you asked me to detail):** `SkillsScreenUI.FormatFailure` case `InsufficientSP` (:209-210) prints `r.CostPaid` — but on that path `CostPaid` is never assigned (it's only set at `BuySkillAction.cs:174`, after all gates pass). The player sees **"You need 0sp to learn Whirlwind (3 available)."** It should read `r.` ...the row's Cost, which `Result` does not carry. Fix: add a `Cost` field to `BuySkillAction.Result` set right after resolution (~:101), or have the UI use `row.Cost`.

WHAT CHANGES for the migration: nothing in this flow is mutation-aware. The only impact is **volume** — porting 29 powers into 6 existing trees + 1 new Rites tree takes the screen from 79 rows to ~108, against a 14-row viewport with single-row-step navigation only. Recommend adding PageUp/PageDown or tree collapse in the same milestone; it is a UX cliff, not a correctness one.


## Cost semantics — 0, negative, and missing; and the `Cost: -1` sentinel in plan D2 is UNSAFE

Schema: `SkillData.Cost` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/SkillData.cs:62`) and `PowerData.Cost` (`PowerData.cs:28`), both `public int Cost = -999;`. SkillData's docstring (:59-61) claims: 'Default -999 matches Qud's SkillFactory sentinel — "missing or unset" — so JSON authors MUST specify Cost explicitly **or the registry will warn**.'

**That warning does not exist.** `SkillRegistry.LoadFromJson` (`SkillRegistry.cs:163-210`) validates ONLY `Class` (warn+skip at :175-180 for skills, :198-203 for powers). Cost is never inspected. **Doc-vs-impl drift (CLAUDE.md Q4 class).** Verified: `grep -n Cost Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/SkillRegistry.cs` → 0 hits.

Behaviour by value, traced through both consumers:

**Cost > 0 (all shipped content).** Census of `Assets/Resources/Content/Data/Skills/*.json`: 12 trees + 67 powers, **zero missing `Cost`, zero ≤ 0**. Distribution: 71 rows at Cost 1, 4 at 50, 3 at 100, 1 at 25. Normal path.

**Cost == 0.** `SkillsScreenStateBuilder.cs:139` `if (sp < 0)` is false at SP 0 → row state `Buyable` (bright green). `SkillsScreenUI.cs:295` `row.Cost > 0` is FALSE → **no cost text drawn; the right margin is blank.** `BuySkillAction.cs:135` `SpBefore < 0` false → passes; `:172` `BaseValue -= 0` is a no-op; skill is granted free. Message: `"You learn X (-0sp)."` (`SkillsScreenUI.cs:187`). So Cost 0 = a working free-to-take row that renders with an empty cost column. Harmless but visually ambiguous with 'owned'.

**Cost < 0 (e.g. the plan's `-1` sentinel). BROKEN — it is an SP faucet.** `sp < -1` is always false → `Buyable`. `row.Cost > 0` false → no cost text, so the row looks identical to a Cost-0 row. `BuySkillAction.cs:172` executes `spStat.BaseValue -= (-1)` → **the player GAINS 1 SP for buying it.** `CostPaid = -1` → the log reads `"You learn X (--1sp)."` Because the row flips to `Owned` after purchase it can only be exploited once per node, but with N teach-only nodes the player farms N SP by opening the screen and pressing Enter N times. **The sentinel is not merely cosmetically wrong; it inverts the economy.**

**Cost missing entirely → -999. CATASTROPHIC.** Row renders as Buyable with no cost text; one Enter press executes `BaseValue -= -999` → **+999 SP**, i.e. the entire Max of the stat in one keypress. Note `Stat.Value` clamps to [Min,Max]=[0,999] (`Stat.cs:27-38`) so the header shows 999, but `BuySkillAction` reads `BaseValue`, which keeps climbing on repeats across different Cost-missing rows. There is no registry guard, no UI guard, and no test pinning it.

**DEFINITIVE ANSWERS:**
- A negative Cost is never safe as a sentinel. If you want one, add an explicit guard: in `BuySkillAction.Execute` right after resolution, `if (cost < 0) return EmitAndReturn(..., FailureReason.NotPurchasable, "");` (new enum member) and in `EvaluateRowState`, `if (cost < 0) return (SkillsScreenRowState.RequirementsNotMet, obfuscatedFlag);` — that is ~6 lines and makes `Cost: -1` mean what the plan wants.
- Independently, add the warn the docstring already promises: in `LoadFromJson`, after the Class check, `if (entry.Cost == -999) Debug.LogWarning($"SkillRegistry [{sourceLabel}]: '{entry.Class}' has no Cost — defaulting to sentinel -999, which currently GRANTS SP on purchase.")`. This is a standalone P1 fix independent of the migration.


## Teach-only (grimoire-granted, not buyable) nodes — DEFINITIVE answer on the current schema + UI

**QUESTION: can a teach-only node be represented in the CURRENT schema and UI without code changes? ANSWER: YES — via a `Requires` sentinel. NOT via a Cost sentinel.**

**Why it works.** The grimoire teach path and the purchase path are completely disjoint gates:
- `SkillsPart.AddSkill(BaseSkillPart, string)` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/SkillsPart.cs:88-143`) performs exactly ONE check — duplicate-by-`GetType()` (:94-98). **It never consults `SkillRegistry` at all: no Cost, no Minimum, no Requires, no Exclusion, no SP.** The string overload (:158-164) only adds reflection type-resolution. So a grimoire calling `AddSkill` bypasses every tree gate by construction.
- `BuySkillAction.Execute` reads all four gates from the registry (:86-101, :148-169).
∴ any registry gate that can never be satisfied makes a row unbuyable while leaving it teachable.

**The recipe (zero code changes):** on the power's JSON row set
```
"Requires": "FoundInAGrimoire",
"Flags": 2
```
where `FoundInAGrimoire` is a string that is not any skill or power class. Traced:
- `SkillsScreenStateBuilder.MeetsRequires` (:172-182) → `skills.HasSkill("FoundInAGrimoire")` → `SkillsPart.HasSkill` (:429-438) compares `GetType().Name == skillClassName` → always false → `reqsMet=false` → state `RequirementsNotMet`.
- `Flags: 2` = `SkillData.FLAG_OBFUSCATED` (:79). `SkillsScreenStateBuilder.cs:134,137` → `isObfuscated=true` → `displayName` becomes `"???"` (:64/:97) and the description footer renders `"???"` (`SkillsScreenUI.cs:325`). Row draws DarkGray (`NameColor`, :348-349) with its cost in DarkGray (`CostColor`, :361). **This is exactly the 'a power exists here that you must find' affordance.**
- Pressing Enter on it: `BuySkillAction` reaches step 7 and returns `MissingPrereq` with `Detail="FoundInAGrimoire"` → `SkillsScreenUI.FormatFailure` (:213-214) prints **"You must learn FoundInAGrimoire first."** ← the one rough edge. Pick a sentinel that reads as prose, e.g. `"Requires": "a grimoire"` → "You must learn a grimoire first." Still awkward but shippable.
- After the grimoire teaches it, `skills.HasSkill(realClass)` is true → `EvaluateRowState` returns `Owned` at :124-125 **before** the Requires check runs → the row shows the real name and the `"owned"` tag. The obfuscation correctly clears.

**Caveats, all verified:** (a) zero shipped JSON uses `Requires`, `Exclusion`, or `Flags` (grep across all 12 files → 0, 0, 0), so this is a first use of implemented-but-unexercised fields — write tests. (b) `HasSkill` is **case-sensitive** (`GetType().Name == skillClassName`, `SkillsPart.cs:434`) whereas the registry dictionaries are `OrdinalIgnoreCase` (`SkillRegistry.cs:44-60`); irrelevant for a never-matching sentinel but it bites when authoring real `Requires` chains. (c) `Cost` must still be > 0 or the Cost-sentinel hazards above apply — set it to any positive number; it is never charged because the row is never buyable.

**The clean alternative, if you'll accept ~10 lines:** add `public bool TeachOnly = false;` to `SkillData`/`PowerData`, then `if (entry.TeachOnly) return (SkillsScreenRowState.RequirementsNotMet, obfuscatedFlag);` at the top of `EvaluateRowState` (after the isOwned check) and a `FailureReason.TeachOnly` early-return in `BuySkillAction.Execute` after registry resolution, with a `FormatFailure` case returning e.g. `$"{display} can only be learned from a grimoire."` This gets the right message text, which the sentinel hack cannot. **Recommend this over the sentinel** — the sentinel is *possible*, the flag is *right*.


## Ability grouping by Class string — what actually reads it, and what does not

`ActivatedAbility.Class` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Abilities/ActivatedAbility.cs:31`, docstring at :28-30 says 'Category for grouping (e.g. "Physical Mutations", "Mental Mutations")'). Set once, in `ActivatedAbilitiesPart.AddAbility(displayName, command, abilityClass, targetingMode, range, sourceMutationClass)` at `:59` (`Class = abilityClass`).

**The complete domain of Class strings in production:**
| Value | Producer | Count |
|---|---|---|
| `"Grimoire Spells"` | `AcidSprayMutation.cs:19`, `ArcBoltMutation.cs:21`, `IceLanceMutation.cs:20`, `KindleMutation.cs:18`, `QuenchMutation.cs:18` (`AbilityClass` overrides) + 11 literal `AddMyActivatedAbility(..., "Grimoire Spells", ...)` call sites in other mutations | ~16 |
| `"Rites"` | `ConsumingRiteBase.cs:81` — one call site, 11 concrete rites | 11 |
| `"Physical Mutations"` | `FireBoltMutation.cs:12`, `IceShardMutation.cs:12`, `PoisonSpitMutation.cs:12` (overrides) + `ChainLightningMutation.cs:25`, `FrostNovaMutation.cs:23`, `FlamingHandsMutation.cs:26`, `PrismaticBeamMutation.cs:23` (literals) | 7 |
| `"Mental Mutations"` | `CalmMutation.cs:37` | 1 |
| `"Skills"` | `ActivatedAbilitySpec.Class` default (`ActivatedAbilitySpec.cs:31`) + ~25 explicit `Class = "Skills",` in skill files; applied at `SkillsPart.cs:131` `abilityClass: string.IsNullOrEmpty(spec.Class) ? "Skills" : spec.Class` | ~25 |
| `"Persuasion"` | `Persuasion_Dismiss.cs:43`, `Persuasion_Recruit.cs:108` — **the only skill that deviates from the "Skills" convention** | 2 |

**Who READS `Class` — exactly one consumer:** `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Rendering/AbilityManagerStateBuilder.cs:61` (`sourceClass: ability.Class`), used at `:74-81` as the PRIMARY sort key (`string.Compare(a.SourceClass, b.SourceClass, OrdinalIgnoreCase)`, tiebreak DisplayName) and surfaced at `AbilityManagerUI.cs:336` as a right-aligned column, truncated to 12 chars with a `~` by `TruncateClass` (`:392-399`).

**Who does NOT read Class, despite the docstring implying grouping:**
- The **bottom hotbar** (`HotbarStateBuilder.Build`, `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Rendering/HotbarStateBuilder.cs:11-58`) iterates `slot 0..GameplayHotbarLayout.SlotCount` and calls `abilities.GetAbilityBySlot(slot)`. `Class` is never touched. Its header is the hardcoded literal `"GRIMOIRES"` (:52) and its empty-slot text is `"No rite bound. Use the Abilities tab to assign one."` (:72, :76).
- The **Inventory Abilities tab** (`InventoryUI.RenderAbilitiesPanel`, `InventoryUI.cs:3258-3375`) also iterates slots 0..9 (`:3278`), not classes. `Class` is never read.
- Grimoire picker (`OpenGrimoirePicker`, `InventoryUI.cs:3431-3470`) filters on `SourceMutationClass`, not `Class`.

**POST-MIGRATION EFFECT:** grouping is a *sort order and a 12-char label in one modal*, nothing more. Once mutations are gone every row's `Class` is `"Skills"` except the two Persuasion rows, so `AbilityManagerStateBuilder`'s primary sort collapses to a no-op and the list becomes alphabetical-by-DisplayName with `Persuasion` rows sorted first (P < S). The right-hand column becomes a wall of identical `"Skills"` text.

RECOMMENDATION (a real design opportunity, not just cleanup): make `ActivatedAbilitySpec.Class` carry the **tree name** — `"Pyromancy"`, `"Cryomancy"`, `"Rites"`, `"Persuasion"`, `"Axe"`. One-line change per skill file, zero UI changes, and the M-screen instantly groups by school, which is strictly better than what mutations gave. `TruncateClass`'s 12-char budget fits every current tree name except none — all 12 tree names are ≤ 11 chars ('ShortBlades' = 11). Update `AbilityManagerStateBuilderTests.cs:35` which pins `abilityClass: "Skills"`.


## InputHandler.GetMutationListSummary — DEAD CODE, zero callers, replaced by nothing

`Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Input/InputHandler.cs:1329-1347`:
```
private static string GetMutationListSummary(MutationsPart mutations)
{
    if (mutations == null || mutations.MutationList.Count == 0) return "(none)";
    ... summary += mutation.DisplayName + " L" + mutation.Level; ...
}
```
**It is shown NOWHERE.** Repo-wide `grep -rn "GetMutationListSummary" Assets/` returns exactly one hit — the declaration itself. `InputHandler` is a single non-partial class (`InputHandler.cs:20 public class InputHandler : MonoBehaviour`), so there is no other file that could reference it. It is `private static` and unreferenced ⇒ unreachable.

**Provenance, confirmed in-source:** it was the log formatter for the deleted F6 grant-random-mutation debug key. `InputHandler.cs:628-631` states: `// ALPHA strip-debug SM1: dev keys exist only in DevMode. // (The old F6 grant-random-mutation branch was DEAD CODE — // SaveLoadInputController consumes F6 first — and has been // deleted outright.)`. The strip left two orphans behind: this method, and an **orphaned XML docstring with no method under it** at `:992-994` (`/// <summary> Debug utility: grant one random valid mutation to the player and log details. </summary>` immediately followed by the docstring for `TryDebugDumpBodyParts`). Also stale: the class docstring at `:16` still advertises `F6 (grant mutation)`.

**WHAT REPLACES IT: nothing is needed.** The player-facing equivalents already exist and are mutation-agnostic:
- Owned actives → the M-key ability manager (`AbilityManagerUI`, `InputHandler.cs:430-437`, `OpenAbilityManager`), which lists every `ActivatedAbility` with hotkey + cooldown + class.
- Owned skills → the X-key skills screen, where owned rows render the `"owned"` tag (`SkillsScreenUI.cs:301-309`).
If you want a console-dump equivalent for debugging, the one-liner is `string.Join(", ", skills.SkillList.Select(s => s.DisplayName))` over `SkillsPart.SkillList` — but there is no caller asking for it.

WHAT CHANGES: delete `InputHandler.cs:1329-1347` outright; delete the orphaned docstring at `:992-994`; fix the class docstring at `:16` to drop `F6 (grant mutation)`. No test references it (grep confirms).


## GrimoirePart end-to-end + the precise diff to teach skills

**Flow.** `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Items/GrimoirePart.cs` (115 lines total).
- `Name => "Grimoire"` (:11). Public fields set from blueprint params by `EntityFactory.ApplyParameters` reflection (`Assets/Scripts/Data/Factories/EntityFactory.cs:255-270`, public-field-then-property, `ConvertValue` handles string/int/bool/enum): `KnowledgeProperty` (:14), `MutationClassName` (:17), `MutationLevel` (:20), `LearnMessage` (:23), `AlreadyKnownMessage` (:26, default "You already know this.").
- Action declaration: on `GetInventoryActions` → `actions.AddAction("Read", "read", "ReadGrimoire", 'r', 20)` (:30-36).
- Dispatch: on `InventoryAction` with `Command=="ReadGrimoire"`, pulls `Actor` and calls `DoRead` (:38-47). The event is raised by `PerformInventoryActionCommand.cs:70` (`GameEvent.New("InventoryAction")`) and `InputHandler.cs:2594`. Reachable only from the inventory item-action menu.
- `DoRead` (:52-112), two disjoint branches:
  - **Spell branch** (`MutationClassName` non-empty, :55-86): `actor.GetPart<MutationsPart>()`; null → "The symbols are beyond your comprehension." (:60); `mutations.HasMutation(MutationClassName)` → `AlreadyKnownMessage` (:65-70); else `mutations.AddMutation(MutationClassName, MutationLevel)` (:72) → announcement `LearnMessage` or fallback `$"You study {ParentEntity.GetDisplayName()} and learn a new rite."` (:74-77); false → "The symbols are beyond your comprehension." (:81).
  - **Knowledge branch** (:88-111): empty `KnowledgeProperty` → "The pages are blank."; already present → `AlreadyKnownMessage`; else `actor.Properties[KnowledgeProperty] = "true"`.
- **The book is never consumed.** No `RemoveObject`, no charge decrement. Deliberate — see `GrimoireChargePart`'s docstring (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Magic/GrimoireChargePart.cs:12-19`): reading TEACHES, carrying the inked book lets you CAST.
- **No diag emission on read.** Violates the CLAUDE.md observability rule; add one during the migration.

**Blueprint census (parsed from `Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json`):** 32 blueprints carry a `Grimoire` part. **27 have `MutationClassName`** (all `MutationLevel: "1"`), 5 are knowledge-only (`PurifyWaterGrimoire`→KnowsPurifyWater, `MendingRiteGrimoire`→KnowsMendingRite, `KindleRiteGrimoire`→KnowsKindleRite, plus the param-less `GrimoireCopy` and `RiteGrimoire` templates). **11 of the 27 additionally carry a `GrimoireCharge` part** — exactly the eleven rites (StormAnvil, HangingBolt, RenderedSteam, ScaldingVeil, Fulmination, ShatteredRime, StillHeart, VerdigrisBloom, HollowCoin, SunderingWord, BloodletterLedger). The other 16 (incl. `WateringGrimoire`→ConjureRainMutation) have no charge part.

**Second consumer:** `ConversationActions.cs:220-279` `CopyGrimoire` — creates a `GrimoireCopy` entity and copies `KnowledgeProperty`, `LearnMessage`, `AlreadyKnownMessage`, **and `MutationClassName` + `MutationLevel`** (:265-270, added by farming audit F8). Any rename must be mirrored here or copies read as blank pages again.

---
**PRECISE DIFF SKETCH — grimoire teaches a skill.**

1. `GrimoirePart.cs` — replace :16-20 with:
```csharp
/// <summary>If set, grants this skill (spell) when read instead of a knowledge property.</summary>
public string SkillClassName = "";
```
(Drop `MutationLevel` entirely — `SkillsPart.AddSkill` has no level parameter; skills are flat, which matches plan F3.)

2. `GrimoirePart.cs` — replace the spell branch, :55-86:
```csharp
if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(SkillClassName))
{
    var skills = actor.GetPart<SkillsPart>();          // needs `using CavesOfOoo.Skills;`
    if (skills == null) { MessageLog.Add("The symbols are beyond your comprehension."); e.Handled = true; return false; }
    if (skills.HasSkill(SkillClassName)) { MessageLog.Add(AlreadyKnownMessage); e.Handled = true; return false; }
    if (skills.AddSkill(SkillClassName, source: "grimoire"))
    {
        MessageLog.AddAnnouncement(!string.IsNullOrEmpty(LearnMessage)
            ? LearnMessage
            : $"You study {ParentEntity.GetDisplayName()} and learn a new rite.");
    }
    else MessageLog.Add("The symbols are beyond your comprehension.");
    e.Handled = true; return false;
}
```
Notes: `SkillsPart` lives in namespace `CavesOfOoo.Skills` while `GrimoirePart` is in `CavesOfOoo.Core` — the `using` is required. `source: "grimoire"` is a NEW value for the `skill/Added` diag `source` field (existing values: "purchase", "starting-kit", "scenario:prebuy", "wish", "npc-teach"); it makes grimoire acquisition queryable via `diag_query category=skill kind=Added`.

3. `SkillsPart.AddSkill(string)` resolves the class by three-tier reflection (`SkillsPart.cs:457-472` + `ResolveSkillType` :474-500): `Type.GetType(name)` → `Type.GetType("CavesOfOoo.Skills." + name)` → all-assembly walk; rejects abstract and non-`BaseSkillPart` types. So the blueprint value must be the **exact** class name (`SkillsPart.HasSkill` is case-sensitive, :434) — e.g. `"Pyromancy_Kindle"`.

4. **27 blueprint param swaps** in `Objects.json`: for each of the 27, `"Key": "MutationClassName"` → `"Key": "SkillClassName"` and the value `<X>Mutation` → `<Tree>_<Power>`; DELETE the sibling `{"Key":"MutationLevel","Value":"1"}` object (an unmatched param is only a silent reflection miss, but leaving it is drift). No other blueprint field changes; the `GrimoireCharge` parts on the 11 rite books stay untouched.

5. `ConversationActions.cs:265-270` — `copyPart.MutationClassName = grimoirePart.MutationClassName; copyPart.MutationLevel = grimoirePart.MutationLevel;` → `copyPart.SkillClassName = grimoirePart.SkillClassName;`.

6. `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Farming/FarmingAccessGrant.cs:63-65` — `player.GetPart<MutationsPart>()` + `HasMutation("ConjureRainMutation")` → `player.GetPart<SkillsPart>()` + `HasSkill("<Hydromancy_ConjureRain>")`. **This is the only mutation reader outside `Gameplay/Mutations/` besides GrimoirePart and SaveSystem** — easy to miss.

7. `GrimoireTooltipData` keys must be re-keyed to skill class names, or the picker/hotbar lose their tooltips — see that dossier.

8. `EntityFactory.CreatePart` (`:230-248`) resolves the blueprint part name `"Skills"` → `SkillsPart` via the `typeName + "Part"` fallback (:236-238), so the Player's existing `{"Name":"Skills","Params":[]}` block (Objects.json:864-867) already gives every player a `SkillsPart`. Nothing to add there.

**Behavioural equivalence check (verified):** `SkillsPart.AddSkill` calls `skill.DeclareActivatedAbility(ParentEntity)` (`SkillsPart.cs:122`) and, when non-null with a non-empty Command, registers it on `ActivatedAbilitiesPart` (:125-138) — which auto-binds to the first empty hotbar slot (`ActivatedAbilitiesPart.cs:69-77`), identical to the mutation path via `BaseMutation.AddMyActivatedAbility` (`BaseMutation.cs:210-227`). So 'read grimoire → power appears on the hotbar immediately' survives unchanged. The ONE difference: `SkillsPart.cs:134` passes `sourceMutationClass: ""`.


## The ink economy — two different 'Ink's, and grimoire charges have NO refill path

**There are two unrelated things called 'ink'. Do not conflate them.**

**(1) Per-book grimoire charges** — `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Magic/GrimoireChargePart.cs`. `Charges = 10` (:31), `MaxCharges = 10` (:34), `ChargesPerVial = 5` (:36), `HasCharge => Charges > 0` (:38), `TrySpend()` (:42-47, decrements, false when empty), `Refill(int)` (:51-58, clamps to MaxCharges, returns actual added). Attached to exactly the 11 rite-grimoire blueprints. `StackerPart.cs:60-61` explicitly refuses to merge any entity carrying a `GrimoireChargePart` — so charged books never stack (correct: stacking would erase per-book charges).

Spend path: `GrimoireInk.FindInked(Entity caster)` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/GrimoireInk.cs:21-33`) walks `caster.GetPart<InventoryPart>().Objects`, returns the first item whose `GrimoireChargePart.HasCharge` is true, else null. Callers: `ConsumingRiteBase.cs:131` (the shared spine) plus five rites that reimplement it — `StormAnvilMutation.cs:108`, `HangingBoltMutation.cs:94`, `ScaldingVeilMutation.cs:75`, `FulminationMutation.cs:103`, `RenderedSteamMutation.cs:83`. Each then calls `grimoire.TrySpend()` (`:138`, `:117`, `:102`, `:83`, `:111`, `:91` respectively). The base enforces the invariant that ink is checked **after** targets are gathered (`ConsumingRiteBase.cs:129-137`, comment: 'a rite that can do nothing must never take a charge for it'); no-ink → `Reject(caster,"no_ink")` + "The pages are dry — no ink to spend."

**FINDING: `GrimoireChargePart.Refill` and `ChargesPerVial` have ZERO callers in production.** Exhaustive grep of `Assets/Scripts/` for `.Refill(` and `ChargesPerVial` returns only the declarations. **There is no way to re-ink a grimoire in the shipped game.** Every rite book is a strictly-10-cast consumable, contradicting the part's own docstring (:20-22: '~10 charges with **cheap re-inking**. The prime→detonate loop should become a habit before it becomes a ration.'). Doc-vs-impl drift with real gameplay consequence.

**(2) The Ink wallet** — a completely separate integer, `Entity.IntProps["Ink"]`, seeded to 50 on the Player (`Objects.json`, Player `IntProps`: `Drams=50`, `Ink=50`). Owned by `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Economy/RentalSystem.cs`: `GetInk` (:34-38), `SetInk` (:41-45, clamps at 0), `AddInk` (:48-52). It is the **weapon-rental currency**, unrelated to spellcasting. `RENTAL_FRACTION = 0.25` (:26), `REFUND_FRACTION = 0.50` (:29).

`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Items/InkVialPart.cs` — despite the name and despite `GrimoireChargePart.ChargesPerVial` existing, **`InkVialPart` refills the RENTAL wallet, not book charges**: `:40` `RentalSystem.AddInk(actor, InkAmount)` with `InkAmount = 25` (:17); message "You decant the ink. (+25 ink, N total)" (:42); diag `category="trade" kind="InkRefilled"` (:44-51); consumes one from the stack (:57-64). Its docstring (:4-11) is explicit that it exists for the Quartermaster rental economy.

**MIGRATION IMPLICATIONS.** (a) `GrimoireInk` currently lives in `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/` but has zero mutation dependencies — it takes an `Entity` and returns a `GrimoireChargePart`. **Move it to `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Magic/` verbatim; do not rewrite it.** It is the one piece of the Mutations folder that ports with a `git mv`. (b) `ConsumingRiteSkillBase : BaseSkillPart` must preserve the after-targets ink check (`ConsumingRiteBase.cs:129-137`) and the `OnCastResolved` caster-reward hook (`:56-71`, which exists specifically because `ApplyPayoff` is gated on target survival — the Bloodletter's-Ledger bug documented at :63-70). (c) The missing re-ink path is a pre-existing hole, not caused by the migration — but M2 is the natural place to close it: `InkVialPart` could gain a second branch that offers 'Re-ink' when the actor carries a `GrimoireChargePart` below max, calling `Refill(ChargesPerVial)`. Flag it to the user as a scope decision rather than assuming it.


## ActivatedAbility.SourceMutationClass — every reader, exhaustively

Field: `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Abilities/ActivatedAbility.cs:59` — `public string SourceMutationClass = "";`, docstring :53-58 ('Class name of the mutation that spawned this ability... Used by the Abilities tab and the grimoire picker to look up tooltip data via GrimoireTooltipData. Empty for abilities that aren't sourced from a mutation.').

**WRITERS (2 production):**
- `ActivatedAbilitiesPart.AddAbility(..., string sourceMutationClass = "")` param at `:52`, assigned `:64` `SourceMutationClass = sourceMutationClass ?? ""`.
- `BaseMutation.AddMyActivatedAbility` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/BaseMutation.cs:210-227`) passes `sourceMutationClass: GetType().Name` at `:226`. **This is the ONLY code that ever puts a non-empty value in the field.**
- `SkillsPart.cs:134` passes `sourceMutationClass: ""` — every skill-sourced ability has it empty today.

**READERS — complete list (6 production sites, all in Presentation):**
1. `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/UI/InventoryUI.cs:3300` — `GrimoireTooltipData.TryGet(ability.SourceMutationClass, out var tooltip)` in `RenderAbilitiesPanel`'s name line; `tooltip.DisplayName` **overrides** `ability.DisplayName` (:3301-3303) and `tooltip.ColorCode` tints the row (:3309-3310).
2. `InventoryUI.cs:3334` — same panel, second `TryGet` for the flavor/mechanics/signature sub-rows (:3339-3352) and the `anyTooltip` fallback stub "(no inline description available)" (:3354-3359).
3. `InventoryUI.cs:3443` — **`if (GrimoireTooltipData.IsGrimoireMutation(ab.SourceMutationClass)) grimoires.Add(ab);`** inside `OpenGrimoirePicker` (:3431-3470). **This is the load-bearing one.** It is the filter that decides which abilities can be bound to a hotbar slot from the Abilities tab.
4. `InventoryUI.cs:3655` — the picker popup's own row rendering (`TryGet` → DisplayName + ColorCode + theme glyph, :3655-3677).
5. `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Input/InputHandler.cs:3871` — `GetAbilityDisplayName(ActivatedAbility)` (:3866-3875): `GrimoireTooltipData.GetOrDefault(ability.SourceMutationClass)` → tooltip DisplayName if set, else `ability.DisplayName`; null ability → the literal `"That rite"`.
6. `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Rendering/HotbarStateBuilder.cs:24` — `GetOrDefault(ability.SourceMutationClass)`, feeding DisplayName (:26-28), `tooltip.ColorCode` (:38) and `tooltip.Mechanics` (:39) into every bottom-hotbar slot snapshot.

Test readers: `Tier1ActivatedAbilitiesTests.cs:73` (`SourceMutationClass = ""` in a save fixture), `SpellcraftArcaneSurgeTests.cs:49` (`sourceMutationClass: ""`). Also referenced in `GrimoireTooltipData.cs:45`'s docstring.

**THE MIGRATION HAZARD, stated plainly:** every one of these six readers keys `GrimoireTooltipData` off this field, and every skill writes `""`. If you delete mutations without re-keying, then: reader 3 makes `grimoires` **always empty** → the grimoire picker shows an empty list → **the player can no longer bind abilities to hotbar slots from the inventory Abilities tab at all** (the M-key manager's `[0-9]bind` still works, so it is not a total lockout, but the primary affordance dies). Readers 1/2/4/5/6 degrade gracefully — `TryGet`/`GetOrDefault` return `default`, so DisplayName falls back to `ability.DisplayName`, colors go to `default(Color)`/White, and flavor/mechanics/signature rows vanish, leaving the "(no inline description available)" stub on every row.

**FIX (choose one, do it in the same milestone as M3/M4):** (a) rename the field to `SourceClass` and have `SkillsPart.cs:134` pass `skill.GetType().Name`, then re-key `GrimoireTooltipData`'s 21 entries from `nameof(KindleMutation)` to `nameof(Pyromancy_Kindle)` etc. — preserves all six readers verbatim; or (b) delete the field and re-point `IsGrimoireMutation` at a new predicate (e.g. `SkillRegistry.TryGetPowerByClass(...)` on a `Rites`/grimoire-taught tree). (a) is the smaller blast radius and keeps the tooltip corpus.


## Hotbar, Abilities tab, grimoire picker, ability manager — the four ability surfaces and what each needs

**(A) Bottom gameplay hotbar** — `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Rendering/HotbarStateBuilder.cs` + `HotbarRenderer.cs` + `GameplayHotbarLayout.cs`. `Build(player, selectedSlot, pendingAbility)` (:11-58) enumerates `slot 0..GameplayHotbarLayout.SlotCount`, `abilities.GetAbilityBySlot(slot)`. Per slot: tooltip via `SourceMutationClass` (:23-24), display name (tooltip wins, :26-28), 6-char `BuildShortName` (:88-110, initials for multi-word, else truncate), `BuildGlyph` = first alphanumeric uppercased (:112-125), cooldown, usable, selected, pending. **Mutation-flavored strings that must change:** the panel header is the literal `"GRIMOIRES"` (:52); `BuildSummaryText` returns `"No rite bound. Use the Abilities tab to assign one."` at :72 and :76; footer `"[] cycle  [Enter] cast"` (:54). Note `"rite"` is arguably still right post-migration if the Rites tree lands — decide deliberately.

**(B) Inventory 'Abilities' tab** — `InventoryUI.cs`, `PANEL_ABILITIES = 3` (:41), tab label drawn at `:2141` (`DrawText(37, 0, "Abilities", ...)`). `RenderAbilitiesPanel` (:3258-3375): header `"Abilities"` + subtitle `"These bindings drive the bottom hotbar."` (:3261-3262); iterates **slots**, not classes (:3278). Layout consts `ABILITIES_INFO_X=6`, `ABILITIES_ROWS_PER_OCCUPIED=4` (name+flavor+mechanics+signature), `ABILITIES_ROWS_PER_EMPTY=1`, `ABILITIES_ROW_SEPARATOR=1` (:45-48). Occupied rows draw four tooltip-driven lines (:3334-3359); empty rows draw `"---"` (:3328). Mouse hit-testing via cached `_slotScreenRows` (`GetAbilitySlotAtMouse`, :3382-3421).
Input (`:3160-3208`): `R` unbinds (`AssignAbilityToSlot(Guid.Empty, cursor)`, :3175-3179); **Enter/Space opens the grimoire picker for the cursor slot** (:3182-3187); number keys 1-9/0 jump to a slot AND open its picker (:3189-3207). Mouse click also opens it (:343).

**(C) Grimoire picker popup** — `InventoryUI.OpenGrimoirePicker(int targetSlot)` (:3431-3470). Geometry `POPUP_GRIMOIRE_W=70`, 4 rows/entry, 6 visible (:3425-3428). **Population loop (:3436-3445) is the choke point:** it walks `abilities.AbilityList` and admits an entry only `if (GrimoireTooltipData.IsGrimoireMutation(ab.SourceMutationClass))`. Selection callback assigns via `ap.AssignAbilityToSlot(chosenId, slot)` (:3455-3461). Cursor starts on the slot's current occupant (:3464-3469). Rows keyed a)–z) (:3664-3670).
**Pre-existing coverage gap, measured:** 27 mutations are grimoire-taught but `GrimoireTooltipData` has only 21 rows. The six with NO tooltip — `ChillDraftMutation`, `ConjureWaterMutation`, `DryingBreezeMutation`, `HearthwarmMutation`, `KindleFlameMutation`, `WardGleamMutation` — are **already invisible in this picker today**. `GrimoireTooltipData.cs:48-55` documents this exact hazard and :134-139 records that the 11 rites were fixed after a cold-eye audit; these six utility spells were never fixed.

**(D) M-key ability manager** — `AbilityManagerUI.cs` + `AbilityManagerStateBuilder.cs`. Opened at `InputHandler.cs:430-437` (KeyCode.M, InputState.Normal), state `InputState.AbilityManagerOpen` (:122), dispatch :511-514, wired `GameBootstrap.cs:643-652`. `Build` (:35-84) snapshots ALL of `AbilityList` (no filter — this is the one surface that shows skill-sourced abilities today), sorts by `Class` then `DisplayName` (:74-81). Row render `:302-338`: `[hotkey]`, name (28 chars max), `ready`/`NT`, and the truncated Class column. Footer `"[Enter]use [0-9]bind [R]unbind [M/Esc]close"` (:373-374). `BuildRowDescription` (:130-142) synthesizes the description line.

**POST-MIGRATION CHANGE LIST:** (1) surface (C) breaks completely unless `SourceMutationClass`/`GrimoireTooltipData` is re-keyed — see that dossier; note the honest alternative is to **delete the grimoire picker and route slot-binding entirely through (D)**, which already works for skills and has a better UX (`[0-9]bind`). (2) Surfaces (A)/(B) degrade to name-only rows without the tooltip corpus — porting the 21 `GrimoireTooltip` records is the cheapest way to keep the flavor/mechanics/signature text that content authors already wrote. (3) `AbilityManagerStateBuilder`'s sort collapses unless `Class` carries the tree name (see the grouping dossier). (4) Test files touching these: `HotbarStateBuilderTests.cs`, `HotbarRendererTests.cs`, `AbilityManagerStateBuilderTests.cs` (pins `abilityClass: "Skills"` at :35), `FarmingAuditFixTests.cs:291-302` (pins `IsGrimoireMutation("ConjureRainMutation")==true`), `ConsumingRiteTests.cs:536` (asserts every rite type name has a tooltip row).


## Character sheet, look mode, tooltips — the mutation-listing surfaces (there are none)

**Character sheet.** The inventory screen's stats panel is built by `InventoryScreenData.BuildPlayerStats(Entity)` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Inventory/InventoryScreenData.cs:170-220`). It emits `StatPriority[]` order first (`:157-168`: Hitpoints, Level, Experience, **MP**, Strength, Agility, Toughness, Intelligence, Willpower, Ego, Speed), then every remaining `actor.Statistics` key alphabetically (:191-205) — which is how **SP** reaches the panel — then synthesizes AV (`CombatSystem.GetAV`) and DV rows (:207-219). Labels via `GetStatLabel` (:229-251). Layout consts `STATS_PANEL_X=1`, `STATS_PANEL_Y=2`, `STATS_PANEL_ROWS=7` (`InventoryUI.cs:49-51`).
**There is NO mutation list on the character sheet.** The five inventory panels are `PANEL_EQUIPMENT=0`, `PANEL_INVENTORY=1`, `PANEL_TINKERING=2`, `PANEL_ABILITIES=3`, `PANEL_CRAFTING=4` (`InventoryUI.cs:38-42`). No mutations panel, no skills panel. The only power-listing surface inside the inventory is the Abilities tab, which lists *hotbar slots*, not owned mutations/skills.

**Sidebar.** `SidebarStateBuilder.Build` (`Assets/Scripts/Presentation/Rendering/SidebarStateBuilder.cs:35-67`) composes 4 dual-value vital lines (:44-54): `HP|MP`, `LV|XP`, `AV|DV`, `WT|DR`. It pulls values out of `InventoryScreenData.Build(player)` by **label** via `FindStat` (:95-108). Because the Player has no MP stat, the live line is `MP -` (the fallback at :46). No mutation or skill listing anywhere in the sidebar; the bottom panel is LOG or THOUGHTS (`BuildThoughtEntries`, :64, :78-...).

**Look mode.** `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Look/LookQueryService.cs` + `LookSnapshot.cs` + `Assets/Scripts/Presentation/UI/LookOverlayRenderer.cs`. Grep for 'mutation' across all three returns exactly ONE hit — a comment at `LookQueryService.cs:280` referencing `NoFightGoal (from M2.2 CalmMutation, ...)`. **Look mode does not enumerate mutations or skills on any entity.** Only that stale comment needs touching.

**Tooltips.** The only tooltip corpus in the game is `GrimoireTooltipData` (21 records) and it is reached exclusively through `ActivatedAbility.SourceMutationClass` — see that dossier. There is no separate mutation-description tooltip surface.

**Other mutation-flavored player-facing prose to sweep in M5:** `ControlsReference.cs:32` `"skills (spend skill points)"` (already correct), `:33` `"ability manager (bind abilities to slots)"` (correct); `HotbarStateBuilder.cs:52` header `"GRIMOIRES"` and :72/:76 `"No rite bound..."`; `InputHandler.cs:3868` `"That rite"` fallback; `GrimoirePart.cs:77` `"...and learn a new rite."`; `AbilityManagerStateBuilder.cs:31-33` docstring `"you know no rites"`; `InputHandler.cs:16` class docstring `F6 (grant mutation)`; `GameBootstrap.cs:183` log `"Step 2/9: Initializing mutations..."`.

**CONCLUSION: no character-sheet/look/tooltip surface enumerates mutations.** The mutation system's entire UI footprint is: the two MP display slots (sidebar + stats panel), the ability-tooltip corpus, and the grimoire picker's filter. That is a much smaller UI surface than 43 classes suggests — the migration's UI risk is concentrated almost entirely in `SourceMutationClass` → `GrimoireTooltipData`.


## Scenario builders + remaining non-UI mutation touchpoints (M5 sweep list)

**`Assets/Scripts/Scenarios/Builders/PlayerBuilder.cs`** — `AddMutation(string mutationClassName, int level = 3)` at `:148-160`: gets `MutationsPart`, warns `"[Scenario] Player.AddMutation('X'): player has no MutationsPart — skipping."` (:153) if absent, else `mutations.AddMutation(mutationClassName, level)` (:156) with a second warning on false (:158). Docstring example at `:18`, cross-reference at `:137`. Referenced in `ScenarioContext.cs:114`'s usage example.
**Callers of `PlayerBuilder.AddMutation` (5 scenarios, 8 call sites):** `LiquidSpellTestBench.cs:116-119` (ArcBolt, Conflagration, IceLance, AcidSpray — all at level 5), `PacifiedWarden.cs:36` (Calm L3), `CalmTestSetup.cs:35` (Calm L3), `CalmThenWitness.cs:43` (Calm L3), `CropFarmShowcase.cs:56` (`nameof(ConjureRainMutation)`, L1). Note `PacifiedWarden.cs:12` also has a docstring line '(Calm auto-binds when learned from StartingMutations)'.
The skill-side equivalent already exists and is used directly (no builder method): `RecruitShowcase.cs:74-75` and `SkillTreeShowcase.cs:60-62` call `skills.AddSkill(nameof(X), source: "scenario:prebuy")` after fetching `GetPart<SkillsPart>()`. **Recommend adding `PlayerBuilder.AddSkill(string className)` mirroring :148-160** so the 8 call sites convert 1:1 instead of each scenario hand-rolling the part lookup. `SkillTreeShowcase.cs:40` also does `.SetStat("SP", 500)`.

**Save system** — `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Save/SaveSystem.cs`: dispatch at `:1256-1257` (`else if (part is MutationsPart mutations) SaveMutationsPart(...)`) and `:1285-1286` (Load). `SaveMutationsPart` (:1676-1691) writes `StartingMutations`, then a count + class-name string per `MutationList` entry, then the `MutationMods` and `MutationGeneratedEquipment` tracker lists via `WritePublicFields`. `LoadMutationsPart` (:1693-1720) reads `StartingMutations`, then **reads and DISCARDS** the class-name strings (`:1697-1699`: `for (...) reader.ReadString();`) — confirming plan F8 — then rebuilds both tracker lists. Delete all four blocks. `SkillsPart` has no hand-written saver (docstring `SkillsPart.cs:15-22`); it rebuilds `SkillList` in `OnAfterLoad` (:45-58) by filtering `ParentEntity.Parts` for `BaseSkillPart`. `SaveSystemCoverageGapTests.cs` touches this area.

**Farming access gate** — `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Farming/FarmingAccessGrant.cs:63-65`: `player.GetPart<MutationsPart>()` + `HasMutation("ConjureRainMutation")` inside `HasFarmingAccess` (:47-68). Easy to miss — it is the only mutation reader in `Gameplay/` outside `Gameplay/Mutations/`, `GrimoirePart`, and `SaveSystem`. Must repoint to `SkillsPart.HasSkill(<ported class>)`.

**Blueprint-side** — `EntityFactory.CreatePart` (`Assets/Scripts/Data/Factories/EntityFactory.cs:230-248`) resolves part names by exact type name, then `name + "Part"`, then by walking every registered Part type and comparing its `Name` override (:240-246 — note this **instantiates every Part type** on a miss, which is why an unknown part name is expensive as well as warned). Deleting `MutationsPart` while `Objects.json` still lists `{"Name":"Mutations"}` on Player produces `EntityFactory: unknown part type 'Mutations' on blueprint 'Player'` (:210) — a warning, not a crash. Remove the blueprint block in the same commit.

**Test census** (`grep -rln` over `Assets/Tests/`): **47 files reference 'Mutation', 73 reference 'Skill'** — matches plan F12. UI-specific test files that will need edits: `SidebarStateBuilderTests.cs` (:44 pins the literal `"HP 25/30 | MP 3"`; :120 seeds MP), `SidebarRendererTests.cs` (:333 seeds MP), `LevelingSystemTests.cs` (:44, :58, :136-137, :158), `MutationSystemTests.cs` (the whole MP-economy block :1004-1090, :1190-1230, :1302-1355), `FarmingAuditFixTests.cs:291-302`, `ConsumingRiteTests.cs:536`, `AbilityManagerStateBuilderTests.cs:35`, `SkillsScreenStateBuilderTests.cs`, `HotbarStateBuilderTests.cs`, `HotbarRendererTests.cs`, `SaveSystemCoverageGapTests.cs`, `Tier1ActivatedAbilitiesTests.cs:73`, `SpellcraftArcaneSurgeTests.cs:49`.


======================================================================
# Part 2
======================================================================

> **NOTE:** **F17 verified at source — 6 of the 11 rites are dead from the keybind, not 4.** The audit line (Docs/SYSTEMS-AUDIT-2026-08.md:123) names four *call sites* (ConjureWater/HangingBolt/Fulmination/ConsumingRiteBase), not four rites; ConsumingRiteBase is one call site covering six subclasses. Root cause chain: `GameEvent.SetParameter(string, int)` writes `IntParameters` (GameEvent.cs:124-128); `GameEvent.GetParameter<T>(name)` reads ONLY `Parameters` (GameEvent.cs:154-159) and returns `default` on miss; `InputHandler.ResolveAbilityCommand` passes `dx`/`dy` as `int` (InputHandler.cs:3140-3141) while casting Zone/RNG/SourceCell to `(object)` (:3137-3139). **BROKEN: HangingBolt (:62-63), Fulmination (:71-72), and via ConsumingRiteBase.cs:99-100 — ShatteredRime (Cone), StillHeart, HollowCoin, BloodletterLedger (SingleTarget). NOT BROKEN: StormAnvil, RenderedSteam, ScaldingVeil (all SelfCentered, read `SourceCell`), VerdigrisBloom and SunderingWord (Radius shape — exempted by the `Shape != RiteShape.Radius` clause at ConsumingRiteBase.cs:115, and SelfCentered abilities never enter AwaitingDirection: InputHandler.cs:3052-3055, :1716-1719).** AI casts are identically dead (AIHelpers.cs:471-472, same int overload). Player-visible symptom is exactly "The rite fails to resolve." (InputHandler.cs:3157-3160) because the no_direction path emits a diag record but no MessageLog line.

> **NOTE:** **Why 2,487 lines of green rite tests never caught it: every single test calls `rite.Cast(zone, dx, dy)` directly.** Verified — zero `FireEvent` / `GameEvent.New` calls exist in Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Magic/*.cs. The public `Cast` overloads are a test seam that bypasses `HandleEvent` entirely. The port MUST add an end-to-end event-dispatch test or the same class of bug will recur behind the same green suite.

> **NOTE:** **ConsumingRiteBase's headline docstring claim is false.** "The first five rites each wrote that out longhand… This base fixes the spine" (ConsumingRiteBase.cs:12-17) and "The two invariants it enforces for **everyone**" (:19). Only the six SM9 rites inherit it; StormAnvil/HangingBolt/RenderedSteam/ScaldingVeil/Fulmination still extend BaseMutation directly and have three mutually-incompatible `Cast` signatures — `Cast(Zone, Cell)`, `Cast(Zone, int, int)`, `Cast(Zone)`. Both invariants ARE separately true of all eleven, but by hand-copied code, not by enforcement. The port is the chance to make the docstring true.

> **NOTE:** **Three dispatcher hazards will silently regress every rite unless designed for.** (H1) `SkillsPart.cs:284` applies `ability.CooldownRemaining = ability.MaxCooldown` unconditionally after `OnCommand` — a refused rite would eat 25-50 turns of cooldown, where the mutation path only cools down on success (ConsumingRiteBase.cs:185). The mitigation the code comments claim (SkillsPart.cs:280-283, "OnCommand can set CooldownRemaining = 0 before returning") **does not work** — line 284 overwrites it. (H2) `TryRouteSkillCommand` returns true regardless of what OnCommand did (SkillsPart.cs:287), so `e.Handled` is set and InputHandler falls through to `EndTurnAndProcess()` (:3172) — a refused rite would also cost a turn. (H3) `SkillsPart.HandleEvent` never sets `BlocksTurnAdvance`, which every rite currently sets (ConsumingRiteBase.cs:102). Fix H1+H2 once at the dispatcher (bool return or `ctx.SuppressCooldown`) — it benefits every future active skill.

> **NOTE:** **The base needs five new hooks before all eleven fit.** In descending order of necessity: (1) **multi-attribute damage** — `DamageAttribute` is a single string (ConsumingRiteBase.cs:46, :151-152) but StormAnvil/HangingBolt/Fulmination each add `Electric`+`Lightning` and RenderedSteam adds `Fire`+`Heat`; (2) **a rider hook** — `res.Riders` is read by exactly one rite (StormAnvilMutation.cs:141-161) and no base hook exposes it; (3) **a pre-damage multiplier hook** — RenderedSteam's PAIR_BONUS lands before damage (RenderedSteamMutation.cs:100) and `ApplyPayoff` is post-damage; (4) **an extra-diag-payload hook** — `paralysisTurns`, `steamDetonations`, `wroteCharge`, `selfTargeted` are all bespoke payload fields the generic record cannot emit; (5) **an unconditional per-target hook** — Fulmination's tile write must fire even on a kill, and `ApplyPayoff` is survivor-gated. Plus a `SelfOnly` shape (or a plain-BaseSkillPart escape hatch) for Scalding Veil, which matches none of the spine.

> **NOTE:** **Rites are element-blind about which book they drain.** `GrimoireInk.FindInked` (GrimoireInk.cs:21-33) returns the first inked `GrimoireChargePart` in inventory index order regardless of which rite is casting — casting Shattered Rime can drain the Storm Anvil book. Pinned as known by `ConsumingRiteHypothesisTests.Hypothesis_CastingOneRite_MayDrainAnotherRitesBook` (:223). Preserve or change deliberately; don't let it change by accident during the port.

> **NOTE:** **No rite reads the `"RNG"` event parameter.** InputHandler sets it (:3138) and AIHelpers sets it (:464); ConsumingRiteBase.HandleEvent reads only Zone/dx/dy (:98-100) and none of the five legacy rites read it either. So `BleedingEffect`'s Toughness saves (BloodletterLedgerMutation.cs:52-56 omits the rng arg) and `StunnedEffect`'s saves run on wall-clock seeds. `SkillEventContext.Rng` is populated by the skill dispatcher (SkillsPart.cs:275) — thread it during the port for free determinism.

> **NOTE:** **No rite can touch scenery.** All three targeting paths are creature-only: `RiteTargeting.FirstCreatureInLine` (RiteTargeting.cs:34, `Tags.ContainsKey("Creature")`), `SpellTargeting.GetCreaturesInRadius` (:108-110, `HasTag("Creature")`), `GetCreaturesInCone` (:281-283). Skills use `SkillLine.Collect`, which additionally collects `AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget` scenery (SkillLine.cs:115-121, AbilityTargeting.cs:38-52). That is the concrete content of the migration plan's F11 "fixes by construction" — but note it forces a second change: `CombatSystem.ApplyDamage` early-returns on anything without a Hitpoints stat (CombatSystem.cs:831-835), so newly-reachable scenery needs `DestructionSystem.RouteDamage` (the pattern every ported elemental skill already uses — Pyromancy_FlameJet.cs:100, Cryomancy_RimeGrip.cs:77).

> **NOTE:** **Doc-vs-impl drift found while reading, fix during the port.** (a) GrimoireTooltipData has Rendered Steam's and Scalding Veil's cooldowns **swapped** — tooltip says CD 30/CD 35 (GrimoireTooltipData.cs:158, :165) but the code is 35 (RenderedSteamMutation.cs:27) and 30 (ScaldingVeilMutation.cs:23). The other nine agree. (b) ShatteredRimeMutation.cs:43 comments "Every mark spent adds a shard" but the code applies exactly one `ShatterArmorEffect` regardless of mark count (:45-46). (c) RenderedSteamMutation.cs:128 produces the string "bodyies" for any detonation count ≠ 1. (d) The wildcard-table tuning paragraph is duplicated verbatim in three files (HollowCoinMutation.cs:26-34, SunderingWordMutation.cs:22-30, BloodletterLedgerMutation.cs:23-31).

> **NOTE:** **Diag contract to preserve verbatim.** Category `"spell"` (default-on, Diag.cs:120), kinds `RiteCast` / `RiteRejected` from the rites and `ResonanceFired` / `ResonanceDeclined` from ResonanceSystem (:252-280). Two payload fields are test-pinned: `statusesConsumed` and `inkLeft` (ConsumingRiteAdversarialTests.cs:369-375), and a refusal must emit zero `RiteCast` records (:378-392). Filing rule is also pinned: SingleTarget files under the victim, Radius/Cone under the caster (ConsumingRiteBase.cs:168-174; tests at ConsumingRiteTests.cs:457 and :477). The `rite = Name` payload value uses the short mutation `Name` ("ShatteredRime"), not the class name — skills conventionally set `Name => nameof(Class)`, so keep the short overrides or every existing diag query breaks. Ported rites additionally get `skill/CommandRouted` and `skill/CommandRejected` for free from the dispatcher (SkillsPart.cs:286, :294).

> **NOTE:** **Reject reason strings currently in use, to carry across:** `no_direction`, `no_target`, `no_ink` (base, ConsumingRiteBase.cs:117/123/133); `no_zone_or_cell` (StormAnvilMutation.cs:90, unique); `no_zone` (HangingBoltMutation.cs:78); `caster_not_wet` (ScaldingVeilMutation.cs:70, unique). RenderedSteamMutation.cs:71 returns false on a null zone/cell with **no record at all** — the one genuinely silent refusal path in the feature. `BaseSkillPart.EmitSkillRejectedDiag`'s conventional tags (BaseSkillPart.cs:316-329) already cover `no_target` / `no_direction` / `no_zone` / `line_blocked`; `no_ink` and `caster_not_wet` are new and should be documented there.

> **NOTE:** **Level machinery is entirely dead for the rites — confirms plan F3.** Not one of the eleven reads `Level`, `BaseLevel`, `CalcLevel`, `GetMutationCap` or `RapidLevel`. `ConsumingRiteBase.Mutate` calls `base.Mutate` (:79) purely to satisfy the lifecycle. `MutationType` (returning "Rite" in all eleven) has **zero consumers outside Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/** — verified by grep. Both can be dropped without a compatibility shim.

> **NOTE:** **Blueprint + content changes the port must make, enumerated.** 11 grimoire blueprints in Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json (StormAnvilGrimoire :25612 … BloodletterLedgerGrimoire :26288), each carrying `Grimoire{MutationClassName: "<X>Mutation", MutationLevel: 1, LearnMessage, AlreadyKnownMessage}` + `GrimoireCharge{10, 10, 5}`, all inheriting `RiteGrimoire`. `GrimoirePart` needs a `SkillClassName` branch beside the `MutationClassName` one (GrimoirePart.cs:54-86) — or repoint the existing field at `SkillsPart.AddSkill` (SkillsPart.cs:159-165 already resolves `CavesOfOoo.Skills.<ClassName>` by reflection). `GrimoireTooltipData` keys off the mutation class name (docstring at :44-46 says so explicitly) and is **load-bearing beyond tooltips** — `IsGrimoireMutation` filters the InventoryUI grimoire picker (:48-55, :252-257), so a rite missing from that table becomes invisible in the picker. Rekey all 11 entries. `GrimoireTooltipDataCompletenessTests` and `ConsumingRiteTests.EveryRiteAppearsInTheGrimoirePicker` (:527) enforce this.


## ConsumingRiteBase (the shared spine) — deep dossier

FILE: /Users/steven/caves-of-ooo/Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/ConsumingRiteBase.cs (215 lines, namespace CavesOfOoo.Core)

**CRITICAL FALSE PREMISE — READ FIRST.** The docstring says "The first five rites each wrote that out longhand… This base fixes the spine" (lines 12-17). It did NOT. Verified at the class declarations:
- StormAnvilMutation.cs:30 `: BaseMutation`
- HangingBoltMutation.cs:21 `: BaseMutation`
- RenderedSteamMutation.cs:23 `: BaseMutation`
- ScaldingVeilMutation.cs:20 `: BaseMutation`
- FulminationMutation.cs:28 `: BaseMutation`
