Nested test doubles at L433–639 (`TestMutation`, `RankedTestMutation`, `TrackingMutation`, `MPEventProbePart`, `RandomBuyOverridePart`, `ChimericHookProbePart`, `BodyRebuildProbePart`, `BodyAffectingTestMutation`) all die with the file — none have a skill analogue worth porting except the event-probe Part pattern.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Mutations/GrimoireSpellTests.cs

**42 tests / 1310 lines / 126 hard refs. Classification: BEHAVIOUR — the single highest-value port target in the migration.** This file is M1's acceptance suite.

It exercises 18 of the 29 reachable powers end-to-end against the REAL blueprint file (`LoadRealFactory()` L24–31 reads `Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json`) and the real thermal/material/liquid pipelines — not stubs. Every one of these invariants must be re-asserted against the ported skill.

**Invariants pinned, per power:**
- **Kindle** (L214–285): raises target `ThermalPart.Temperature` above baseline (L214); ignites a low-FlameTemp combustible (`HasEffect<BurningEffect>`, L237); emits radiant heat to the cell *adjacent to the target*, not just the target (L258) — this is the one that catches a naive port that only touches the primary cell.
- **Quench** (L287–399): applies `WetEffect` (L287); lowers temperature (L309); extinguishes an existing `BurningEffect` (L333); **prevents** a subsequent ignition (L366) — that last one is the fire-suppression-threshold contract, equivalent to `HydromancyJetBlastTests.FireSuppressionThreshold = 0.35f` (`HydromancyJetBlastTests.cs:34`).
- **Conflagration** (L400–513): damages creatures in radius (L400); applies Burning to creatures (L424); heats non-creature objects in radius (L444 — the Wall/Terrain reach that F11 says `SpellTargeting` currently gets wrong); chain-ignition across neighbouring combustibles (L467); **excludes the caster** (L488, counter-check).
- **IceLance** (L514–583): shatters brittle frozen metal (L514); extinguishes a burning target (L553).
- **AcidSpray** (L584): organic target takes damage AND loses `MaterialPart.Combustibility`.
- **ArcBolt** (L628–721): wet creature doubles charge (L628 — same 0.2 conduction threshold as `HydromancyJetBlastTests.cs:31`); chains to an adjacent conductor on `EndTurn` (L655), i.e. the chain is deferred to the turn boundary, not immediate.
- **RimeNova** (L722–781): extinguishes a burning barrel (L722); freezes creatures in radius (L750).
- **Thunderclap** (L782–858): electrifies a METAL prop but NOT a wood prop (L782, counter-check pair in one test); wet creature takes double damage (L818). NB F11 flags Thunderclap as using a stale 0–1 conductivity threshold — **the ported test should assert the corrected threshold and the divergence must be logged**, this is the one place the port intentionally changes behaviour.
- **EmberVein** (L859–916): ignites a row of four crates (L859); hits a creature and CONTINUES to a prop behind it (L887) — pierce semantics.
- **KindleFlame** (L917): heats a torch-like prop but NOT a creature (targeting discrimination).
- **DryingBreeze** (L944, L1269): removes `WetEffect` in radius; ignores entities beyond radius 1 (counter-check).
- **Hearthwarm** (L968–1033): applies a HearthAura effect (L968); cooks raw meat over exactly 3 turns (L991 — a multi-turn state machine); fails on an empty cell (L1294).
- **ConjureWater** (L1034–1168): wets target cell and extinguishes a burning entity (L1034); spawns at the FIRST burning cell when one is in range (L1063); spawns at the LAST passable cell when blocked (L1095); fails on zero direction (L1128); spawns an actual water-puddle Entity (L1143). Per D1 this is the one "reachable duplicate" being kept as a distinct utility — all five invariants must survive.
- **ChillDraft** (L1169): cools nearby entities.
- **WardGleam** (L1192–1268): clears Acidic and Charred from EQUIPPED items (L1192); returns false when there is nothing to cleanse (L1219, counter-check); skips UNequipped items (L1244, counter-check).

**Plus 4 GrimoirePart tests (L140–212) — these are the M3 acceptance tests:** reading a grimoire grants the power and registers exactly one activated ability with the right `DisplayName` (L140); reading twice does not duplicate the ability (L158); reading with no receiving Part fails gracefully without crashing (L176); the legacy `KnowledgeProperty` path still works (L190 — **this one has no mutation reference and survives verbatim**).

**Port mechanics:** the call shape is `mutations.AddMutation(new XMutation(), 1); var x = mutations.GetMutation<XMutation>(); x.Cast(zone, zone.GetCell(5,5), dx, dy, new Random(42));`. The skill equivalent is `var skill = Fix<Tree_Power>(caster); skill.OnCommand(Ctx(caster, zone, dx, dy));` — see `PyromancyFlameJetTests.cs:70-75`. Fixtures to rewrite: `CreateCaster` (L37–58, adds `ActivatedAbilitiesPart` + `MutationsPart` → swap `MutationsPart` for `SkillsPart`), `CreateCombustibleObject` (L82–110, carries `ThermalPart`+`MaterialPart`+`FuelPart` — **keep verbatim, no skill file currently has an equivalent**), `CreateGrimoireItem` (L111–122, sets `MutationClassName`/`MutationLevel` → `SkillClassName`).


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Magic/ConsumingRiteTests.cs

**34 tests / 810 lines / 52 hard refs. Classification: BEHAVIOUR — M2's acceptance suite.** All 11 Rites are `ConsumingRiteBase : BaseMutation` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/ConsumingRiteBase.cs:26`), so every test here is a mutation test despite living under `Magic/`.

**The shared discipline (must survive the port to `ConsumingRiteSkillBase`):**
- `EveryRite_CastCold_IsNearlyWorthless` (L81): generic `Check<T>(int allowance) where T : ConsumingRiteBase, new()`, asserts damage ≤ allowance when nothing is consumed. This is *the* balance invariant separating rites from skills.
- `EveryRite_HitsHarderWhenFed`: resonance multiplier > 1.0 when statuses are present.
- `EveryRite_SpendsInkOnACast_AndNoneOnAMiss`: ink economy — `GrimoireChargePart.Charges` decrements on success only.

**Per-rite payoff pins (cold-eye Q3 additions — the comment at L547 states these caught a real mutation-testing gap, "the rite's entire non-damage payoff was unasserted"):** ShatteredRime shatters armour and only when fed (L545), is a cone catching a clump, breaks ice creatures too. StillHeart cold-cast sleeps nobody, sleeper wakes when struck, sleeps longer for more marks, removes an elite instead of killing it, does not buff the enemy it sleeps. VerdigrisBloom strips armour across radius, re-seeds acid only when fed, still resisted by acid immunity. HollowCoin is untyped so nothing resists it, spends three statuses not two, spends statuses no other rite can reach. SunderingWord debuffs the whole radius, rider-guard holds on a cold cast. BloodletterLedger heals the caster only for marks spent, opens Bleeding scaled by marks, still pays when its own damage kills, cast cold heals nothing.

**Diag contracts:** `SingleTargetRite_FilesItsDiagUnderTheVictim` vs `RadiusRite_StillFilesItsDiagUnderTheCaster` — the `TargetId` field differs by targeting shape. Port these exactly; the skill diag category is `skill`, the rite records are `RiteCast`.

**Content gates (become M3 tests):** `EveryRiteGrimoire_SpawnsInkedAndTeachesARealRite` (L~655–686) and `EveryRiteAppearsInTheGrimoirePicker` (L527–540).

**Fixture:** `Caster<T>(zone,x,y,out caster) where T : ConsumingRiteBase, new()` at L59–74 — note it does `caster.AddPart(new MutationsPart())` then `caster.AddPart(rite); rite.Mutate(caster,1)`, i.e. it attaches the rite Part **directly**, bypassing `MutationsPart.AddMutation`. The skill port is simpler: `SkillsPart.AddSkill(new T(), source:"test")` does both. `Creature()` at L44–57 builds full 4-element resistance stats + `StatusEffectsPart` + `Body` — reusable verbatim. `Setup()` L29–39 initialises `ResonanceSystem` from `Resources/Content/Data/Resonance/Resonance.json` and `EntityFactory` from `Objects.json` — the rite tests are content-integration tests, not unit tests, and the port must keep that.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Magic/ConsumingRiteAdversarialTests.cs

**21 tests / 460 lines / 32 hard refs. Classification: BEHAVIOUR (adversarial). PORT ALL 21.** This is the CLAUDE.md §Adversarial-sweep artifact for the rite system; it is regression infrastructure the migration must not drop.

Bug classes probed, each of which the skill port re-exposes:
- **Null/boundary:** `Adversarial_NullZone_RefusesWithoutThrowing`, `Adversarial_CasterWithNoInventory_RefusesWithoutThrowing`, `Adversarial_NoTarget_ConsumesNothingAnywhere`, `Adversarial_ZeroDirectionOnADirectionalRite_Refuses`, `Adversarial_RadiusRiteAcceptsZeroDirection` (the counter-check pair to the previous — directional refuses, radius accepts).
- **Ink/state atomicity:** `Adversarial_EmptyBook_RefusesAndSaysWhy`, `Adversarial_EmptyBookDoesNotEatTheTargetsStatuses` (no partial mutation on the refusal path), `Adversarial_OneCast_SpendsExactlyOneCharge_EvenAcrossManyTargets`, `Adversarial_SecondCastFindsNothingLeftToSpend`, `Adversarial_TwoCastersDoNotShareEachOthersInk` (cross-actor isolation).
- **Mid-execution death:** `Adversarial_OneDyingTargetDoesNotStopTheRest` (loop snapshot stability), `Adversarial_TargetKilledByTheDamage_GetsNoRiders`.
- **Anti-exploit:** `Adversarial_LedgerCannotBeFarmedForInfiniteHealing`, `Adversarial_LedgerCannotHealAboveMaximum`.
- **Self-referential gate:** `Adversarial_RiteNeverHitsItsOwnCaster`.
- **Idempotence:** `Adversarial_ReCastOntoAlreadyDebuffedTarget_DoesNotThrow`.
- **Diag dispatch:** `Adversarial_FailedCastEmitsRejected_NotCast` and `Adversarial_SuccessfulCastEmitsRiteCast_WithMarksAndInk` — the both-branches-emit contract from CLAUDE.md §Observability.
- **Content uniqueness:** `Adversarial_EveryRiteGrimoireNamesADistinctMutation` (L400–416, iterates 6 grimoire blueprints, `HashSet.Add(grim.MutationClassName)` → becomes `SkillClassName`); `Adversarial_EveryRiteDeclaresADistinctCommand` (L419, explicit `new ConsumingRiteBase[]{...}` list); `Adversarial_EveryRiteDeclaresAReadableResonanceTable` (L437–458, explicit array of 6, asserts `ResonanceSystem.Preview(target, r.Element, r.Slots).Multiplier > 1.0f` — catches a rite whose element string resonates with nothing).

First hard ref at L63 (`TearDown` / fixture). Whole file breaks compile on `ConsumingRiteBase` deletion.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Magic/ConsumingRiteHypothesisTests.cs

**8 tests / 313 lines / 9 hard refs. Classification: BEHAVIOUR (hypothesis-driven deep audit per CLAUDE.md). PORT ALL 8.** These probe the ink economy's cross-system seams and are the hardest invariants to re-derive:
- `Hypothesis_SecondCopyOfARiteBook_MakesTheRiteCastableAgain` and `..._ItsInkIsNotLostOnPickup` — `GrimoireInk.FindInked` picks *an* inked book, so a second copy restores castability. The plan's §4 risk row explicitly names this (`Docs/MUTATIONS-TO-SKILLS-MIGRATION.md:105`).
- `Hypothesis_SplittingAStackOfRiteBooks_DoesNotDuplicateInk` — inventory stack-split × charge interaction.
- `Hypothesis_CastingOneRite_MayDrainAnotherRitesBook` — **this test documents a known cross-book leak**; read the assertion before porting, it may pin current behaviour rather than desired behaviour.
- `Hypothesis_ATwoSlotRite_LeavesTheThirdStatusForTheFollowUp` — the slot-consumption boundary.
- `Hypothesis_ReCastingStillHeart_ExtendsTheSleepRatherThanWastingTheCharge` — stacking semantics.
- `Hypothesis_RadiusRite_CaughtFollowerDoesNotBecomeAPermanentEnemy` — **CROSS-SYSTEM: rite × follower/faction.** Highest-risk single test in the rite set.
First hard ref L62.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Magic/RiteTests.cs

**16 tests / 401 lines / 20 hard refs. Classification: BEHAVIOUR. PORT ALL 16.** Covers the other 4 rites (StormAnvil, HangingBolt, RenderedSteam, ScaldingVeil) plus the base-class ink contract.

Invariants: `ARite_SpendsAnInkCharge`; `ARite_WithNoInk_RefusesAndSaysWhy`; `ARite_WithNoTarget_DoesNotWasteACharge` (the three base-class pins the plan's M2 says to port first). HangingBolt converts marks into paralysis rather than damage, more marks → longer paralysis, cold cast paralyses nothing. RenderedSteam detonates only on a target that is BOTH wet and burning, the pair hits harder than either alone, and with only one of the pair it does not blind (counter-check). ScaldingVeil scalds whoever strikes you (a retaliation-shaped reactive), consumes the caster's own water, refuses without spending ink when dry, and does NOT scald on sourceless damage (counter-check — this is the `attacker == null` guard).

`EveryRiteGrimoire_SpawnsInkedAndTeachesARealMutation` (L364–388) is a content gate: for each of 4 grimoire blueprints it asserts the blueprint exists, carries `GrimoireChargePart` with `Charges > 0`, has a `GrimoirePart`, `MutationClassName` is non-empty, and `Type.GetType("CavesOfOoo.Core." + name + ", CavesOfOoo")` resolves. **Port target: `SkillClassName` + `SkillRegistry` lookup, not raw reflection.**

Fixtures: `Creature` L44–56 (adds `ActivatedAbilitiesPart` + `MutationsPart`), `Caster` L58–79 (inventory + `StormAnvilGrimoire` with 10 charges), `BookOf` L81, `Learn<T> where T : BaseMutation, new()` L84 (`caster.AddPart(m); m.Mutate(caster,1)`). First hard ref L55.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Magic/FulminationTests.cs

**9 tests / 248 lines / 3 hard refs, but 8 of 9 tests call the `Caster` helper. Classification: BEHAVIOUR (cross-system). PORT ALL 9.**

This is the *conductivity proof-of-concept* file: Fulmination's charge propagates through liquid/metal terrain, and the tests contrast a dry stone room (`SameRite_DryStoneRoom_ChargeGoesNowhere`, L~100) against wet/grated rooms. It is the strongest existing evidence for the elemental-pipeline × terrain contract, and it is currently only reachable through a mutation.

Most important test: `TheRiteItself_ContainsNoKnowledgeOfAnyOfThis` — asserts the rite class has no hard-coded terrain knowledge, i.e. the conduction lives in the shared pipeline. **If the port re-implements conduction inside the skill class, this test correctly fails.** Keep it.

Fixture `Caster(zone,x,y)` L72–87 returns `(Entity caster, FulminationMutation rite)`, attaches `MutationsPart` + `InventoryPart` + a `FulminationGrimoire` with 10 charges, then `c.AddPart(rite); rite.Mutate(c,1)`. `Grate(zone,x,y)` L90 uses the real `MetalGrate` blueprint via `_factory`. Setup resets `LiquidRegistry` (L55) as well as `ResonanceSystem`.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Mutations/ProjectileMutationTests.cs

**12 tests / 427 lines / 31 hard refs. Classification: MIXED — mostly dead content, but carries three invariants that MUST be re-homed.**

**DIES with the content (7 tests):** `PrismaticBeam_Cast_DamagesEveryCreatureBeforeWall_AndEmitsBeamSequence` (L94), `IceShard_Cast_AppliesStun` (L131), `PoisonSpit_Cast_AppliesPoison` (L272), `FrostNova_Cast_DamagesRadiusTwo_AndStunsOnlyRadiusOne` (L292), `ChainLightning_Cast_ChainsToNearestUniqueTargets` (L328), `FireBolt_Mutate_RegistersDirectionalAbilityMetadata` (L31), `FrostNova_Mutate_RegistersSelfCenteredAbilityMetadata` (L44). All target the 14 unreachable classes (F2). Note `FrostNova_Cast_DamagesRadiusTwo_AndStunsOnlyRadiusOne` is a genuinely good radius-gradient counter-check whose shape is worth copying into `Cryomancy_ColdSnap`'s tests even though the class dies.

**MUST RE-HOME (5 tests, invariants outlive the classes):**
1. `FlamingHands_Mutate_RegistersAdjacentAbilityMetadata` (L19–29) — asserts `ability.TargetingMode == AbilityTargetingMode.AdjacentCell` and `ability.Range == 1`. FlamingHands is reachable (Player blueprint). Port directly; the skill analogue is `DeclareActivatedAbility(null)` spec assertion — see `PyromancyFlameJetTests.cs:95-105`.
2. `FireBolt_Cast_ResistedHit_LogsActualPostResistanceDamage_NotRawRoll` (L151) — **the message-log honesty invariant.** A resisted hit must log the post-resistance number, not the raw dice roll. This is a whole-pipeline contract that applies to every ported power.
3. `FireBolt_Cast_NoResistanceStat_LogsFullRawDamage` (L201) — counter-check to #2.
4. `FireBolt_Cast_EmitsMutationDamageDiag_WithMutationClassDiceElementAndActualAmount` (L230–270) — **the diag payload schema.** Asserts a `category="damage" kind="MutationDamage"` record whose `PayloadJson` contains `"mutationClass":"FireBoltMutation"`, `"diceRolled":"2d4"`, `"rawRoll":N`, `"elementAttribute":"Fire"`, `"actualDamage":N`, with `ActorId == caster.ID` and `TargetId == target.ID`. Emitted at `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:126`. **The kind string and every payload key must get a decided successor in M1** — see the dedicated diag row.
5. `FireBolt_Cast_DamagesFirstCreature_AppliesBurning_AndEmitsProjectileFx` (L57) — the line-stops-at-first-creature semantic + `AsciiFxBus` projectile emission. The FX-bus assertion has no equivalent in any current skill test file; port it or the ported powers lose their visual-emission coverage entirely.

Setup L11–17 calls `AsciiFxBus.Clear()`, `MessageLog.Clear()`, `Diag.ResetAll()` — the `AsciiFxBus.Clear()` is the part no skill fixture currently does.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Mutations/CalmMutationTests.cs

**4 tests / 212 lines / 13 hard refs. Classification: BEHAVIOUR (cross-system: mutation × AI goal stack). PORT ALL 4.** Calm is one of the two powers on the Player blueprint, so this is live content.

Invariants, precisely:
1. `CalmMutation_AppliesNoFightGoalOnHit_WithBaseDurationAndLevel1` (L35–61): cast succeeds against a target in line; target's `BrainPart.HasGoal<NoFightGoal>()` is true; the goal is the **top** of the stack (`brain.PeekGoal() as NoFightGoal` is non-null); `goal.Duration == 50` from formula `BaseDuration(40) + Level*10`.
2. `CalmMutation_LevelScalesDuration` (L64–92): table-drives (3→70, 5→90, 10→140). **This is one of the 8 Level-referencing classes (F3). Per the flat-port decision the ported skill has no level, so this test must be REPLACED by a single flat-duration pin, and the divergence recorded in the migration doc's divergences table.**
3. `CalmMutation_Idempotent_DoesNotStackOrExtendIfAlreadyPacified` (L94–125): pre-pacify with `NoFightGoal(duration:10)`, cast again at level 5, assert `brain.GoalCount` unchanged AND `goal.Duration` still 10 (neither replaced nor extended). Two-sided — port both halves.
4. `CalmMutation_Cast_ZeroDamage_NoDamageLogLine_NoMutationDamageDiag_HpUnchanged` (L127–170): `DamageDice="0"` is a deliberate no-op — no damage log line, **no `category="damage" kind="MutationDamage"` record** (queried at L161), HP unchanged. This is a negative diag assertion; after the diag kind is renamed this test would pass vacuously if the query string isn't updated. Flag.

Call shape: `mutations.AddMutation(new CalmMutation(), level); calm.Cast(zone, zone.GetCell(5,5), 1, 0, new Random(42))`. Fixture `CreateCaster` at ~L172.

**17 tests / 409 lines / 7 hard refs. Classification: MIXED (CROSS-SYSTEM: mutation × AI).** 4 Calm tests must port; the other 13 (WitnessedEffect, NoFightGoal-via-dialogue) are unaffected but sit in the same compilation unit, so the file breaks wholesale.

Port targets:
- `CalmMutation_OnHit_AlreadyPacified_LogsAlreadyAtPeace` (L215–~255): asserts the message-log line "X is already at peace." on the idempotent path — the *user-visible* half of `CalmMutationTests`' idempotence test. Uses `mutations.AddMutation(new CalmMutation(),1)` at L231–233.

- `CalmMutation_BaseDuration_IsForty` (L278–286): `BaseDuration == 40`. Ports to a named constant pin (cf. `Pyromancy_FlameJet.JET_INTENSITY` style, `PyromancyFlameJetTests.cs:170-176`).

Helper `CreateCalmCaster` at ~L372–380 adds `MutationsPart` (L379) — this is what breaks the whole file. Swap to `SkillsPart`.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/AI/M2AdversarialTests.cs

**11 tests / 396 lines / 10 hard refs. Classification: MIXED (CROSS-SYSTEM: mutation × AI goal stack).** 3 Calm adversarial tests port; 8 WitnessedEffect tests are collateral compile damage.

Port targets:
- `CalmMutation_TwoTargetsInLine_OnlyFirstIsPacified` (L133–~160): two targets at (7,5) and (9,5), caster at (5,5), cast direction (1,0); asserts t1 pacified and t2 NOT. **Line-stops-at-first-creature semantics.** Note F11: the ported skill routes through `SkillLine`, which (unlike `LineTargeting`) does not skip Wall/Terrain — so this test's *shape* survives but a new counter-check for "stops at a wall too" should be added.
- `CalmMutation_AfterPriorNoFightGoalRemoved_PushesNewOne` (L168–~200): push a `NoFightGoal(30)`, remove it, `Assume.That(!brain.HasGoal<NoFightGoal>())`, then cast → a new goal is pushed. Guards against a "has-ever-been-pacified" latch.
- `CalmMutation_BaseDurationRuntimeChange_AffectsNextCast` (L202–~230): sets `calm.BaseDuration = 100` then casts, asserting the field is read at cast time not at Mutate time. **Port only if the skill keeps a mutable duration field; if the port hard-codes a const, this test dies and the constant becomes the pin instead.**

`WitnessedEffect_OnStackWithDifferentType_ReturnsTrueNoMutation` is a NAME false positive ("NoMutation" = no state change) but its enclosing file uses `CreateCalmCaster` at L143/L351. First hard ref L143.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Combat/ThrownMutationCombatAdversarialTests.cs

**17 tests / 739 lines / 16 hard refs. Classification: MIXED and the highest-risk CROSS-SYSTEM file in the census** — it is the only place mutation damage is exercised against the *real* retaliation/faction pipeline rather than synthetic `CombatSystem.ApplyDamage(int,...)` calls (its own docstring, L13–29, says exactly that).

**Must port (6 tests, all cross-system):**
- `RealMutationCast_AgainstNeutralNpc_ProvokesRetaliation_EndToEnd` (L385): a real `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.Cast` against a neutral NPC flips it hostile. **The single most important cross-system test in the migration** — the skill port must re-prove that spell damage provokes retaliation through the production pipeline, not a stub.
- `MutationFullyResisted_DoesNotProvokeRetaliation` (L443): counter-check — 100% resistance ⇒ zero damage ⇒ no hostility. Guards the "damage > 0" gate on the retaliation hook.
- `FireBolt_FullResistance_LogsZeroDamage_DiagShowsZeroActualDamage` (L244): diag `actualDamage` is 0, not the raw roll. Queries `Category="damage", Kind="MutationDamage"` at L275.
- `FireBolt_WithSpellcraftEmpower_MessageAndDiagReflectSkillBonus_NotJustResistance` (L283, diag query L318): **mutation × skill crossover** — a Spellcraft skill bonus must appear in both the message and the diag payload. This one already spans both systems and is the natural template for the ported test.
- `MutationDamageDiag_MutationClassField_ReflectsConcreteSubclass_NotHardcoded` (L323, diag query L342): the payload's class field is the concrete subclass, not the base. Ports to "`skillClass` reflects the concrete skill".
- `TargetWithBody_UsesSelectHitLocationAndGetPartAV_NoCrash` (L199) — thrown-weapon side, but shares the file's fixture.

**Collateral (11 tests, thrown-weapon only, no mutation semantics):** the Agility/`MaxStrengthBonus`/`ThrowHitRoll`/`ThrowPenetration` groups. They still fail to compile because `CreateMutationCaster` (first hard ref L260) is in the same class. Cheapest fix: rename the helper and swap `MutationsPart`→`SkillsPart`.

## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Farming/FarmingAuditFixTests.cs

**20 tests / 470 lines / 10 hard refs. Classification: MIXED (CROSS-SYSTEM: mutation × farming × grimoire-picker UI).** ~5 tests are mutation-bearing; 8 tests call `MakeLoadedPlayer` (first hard ref L212, `player.AddPart(new MutationsPart())`).

**The two tests that pin production APIs the migration must replace:**
- `Sm7d_ConjureRain_HasGrimoirePickerEntry` (L~288–303): asserts `GrimoireTooltipData.IsGrimoireMutation("ConjureRainMutation")` is true (L295), `GrimoireTooltipData.TryGet("ConjureRainMutation", out var tip)` succeeds (L297), and the counter-check `IsGrimoireMutation("NoSuchMutation")` is false (L302). The comment at L291 states the grimoire picker **filters by `IsGrimoireMutation`** — a power missing from that table is invisible in the UI. `GrimoireTooltipData` has 4 production consumers: `InventoryUI.cs:3300,3334,3443,3655`, `InputHandler.cs:3871`, `HotbarStateBuilder.cs:24`. **M3 must ship an `IsGrimoireSkill` equivalent plus rows for all 27 taught powers, or every grimoire-taught skill vanishes from the picker and the hotbar tooltip.**
- `Sm7d_CopyGrimoire_CopiesMutationTeachingFields` (L324–350): asserts a copied grimoire preserves `copyPart.MutationClassName == "ConjureRainMutation"` (L346) and `copyPart.MutationLevel == 1` (L348). Direct M3 port to `SkillClassName`; `MutationLevel` has no successor under a flat port — **drop the field, and drop this half of the assertion deliberately.**

Also: `Sm7c_PlayerKnowingConjureRain_ShouldNotGrant` (an anti-double-grant gate) and `Sm7d_RainDrops_NeverTravelBelowTheCropRow` (an FX-geometry pin, uses `GrantRain`-style setup at L419–422).


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Save/Tier1MutationsPartTests.cs

**6 tests / 198 lines / 23 hard refs. Classification: INFRASTRUCTURE — dies entirely. Delete, do not port.** All six exist only to pin `MutationsPart` serialization.

- `MutationsPart_Empty_RoundTrips` (L39): after `PartRoundTripHelper.RoundTripEntityViaTokenGraph`, `MutationList.Count==0`, `StartingMutations==""`, `MutationMods.Count==0`, `MutationGeneratedEquipment.Count==0`.
- `MutationsPart_StartingMutations_String_RoundTrips` (L54): the scalar survives.
- `MutationsPart_AddedMutation_Survives_ViaPartsScan` (L68): the contract that `MutationList` stays in sync with `ParentEntity.Parts` so `OnAfterLoad`'s parts-scan rebuild works.
- `MutationsPart_MutationMods_RoundTrip` (L101) / `MutationsPart_MultipleMutationMods_AllSurvive` (L131).
- `MutationsPart_DiscardedMutationListBytes_DontCorruptStream` (L162) — this one pins the vestigial hand-written saver from plan F8 (`SaveSystem.cs:1697-1699` reads class-name strings and discards them).

**The equivalent skill-side coverage ALREADY EXISTS and is better**: `Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Save/Tier3CollectionRoundTripTests.cs` L57–265 has 6 `Adversarial_SkillsPart_*` tests covering empty-list round-trip, one-skill + `OnAfterLoad` identity resolution, concrete-subtype preservation, `ParentEntity` wiring, multi-skill, and the `RoundTripEntityWithBodies` vs `RoundTripEntityViaTokenGraph` distinction (L192–238, which pins that the WithBodies helper is INSUFFICIENT for `OnAfterLoad`-dependent Parts). **Net: −6 tests, zero coverage loss.** Note `Tier3CollectionRoundTripTests.cs:17` and `:186` name `MutationsPart` in comments only — soft, comment edit.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Skills/Wsp7MagicSkillsTests.cs

**15 tests / 350 lines / 4 hard refs. Classification: CROSS-SYSTEM — this file already lives on the skill side but calls the mutation-namespaced helper.** Fix is a RENAME, not a port; but the plan (M4) says "`MutationDamageHelpers` (fold into skill helpers)" and that must not lose these.

`MutationDamageHelpers` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/MutationDamageHelpers.cs:38`) is **consumed by the skill system**: `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/BaseSkillPart.cs:201`, `SkillEventDispatcher.cs:170`, `SpellcraftSkill.cs:30,35`, plus `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs`. It cannot be deleted with the mutations — it must be moved/renamed and its callers updated.

The 4 tests (L248–325) pin `ApplySpellDamage`:
1. `ApplySpellDamage_TagsDamageWithSpellAndElement_AndAppliesResistance` (L251–283): target with `HeatResistance = 50`, `ApplySpellDamage(target, baseDamage:20, elementAttribute:"Fire", attacker, zone:null)` ⇒ HP delta exactly 10. The failure message (L281) spells out the pre-WSP7 bug: an untyped `Damage` silently bypasses the whole resistance pipeline.
2. `ApplySpellDamage_NoTarget_ReturnsZero_NoCrash` (L285).
3. `ApplySpellDamage_ZeroBaseDamage_ReturnsZero` (L295) — "no skill query, no damage".
4. `ApplySpellDamage_WithSpellcraft_DealsBonusDamage` (L305–325): caster with `SpellcraftSkill` ⇒ base 10 lands as 11.

Also in this file (no mutation ref, but adjacent): `SkillEventDispatcher.GetSpellDamageModifier` additive stacking of Spellcraft + Pyromancy (L~240). **ACTION: rename the type (e.g. `SpellDamageHelpers`), move to `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/`, update these 4 call sites + the 4 production call sites, keep the tests otherwise identical.** Comments at L14, L18, L155, L248 also say "mutation" and should be reworded in M5.

## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Scenarios/PlayerBuilderTests.cs

**21 tests / 311 lines / 5 hard refs. Classification: INFRASTRUCTURE for the scenario builder — 3 tests, all must be REWRITTEN to `AddSkill`, not deleted.** M5 explicitly calls for `PlayerBuilder.AddMutation` → `AddSkill`.

- `AddMutation_AttachesMutationAsPart` (L141–158): `ctx.Player.AddMutation("FireBoltMutation", level: 2)` then `player.GetPart<FireBoltMutation>()` non-null with `BaseLevel == 2`. The comment L148 notes the effective level is capped by `GetMutationCap` (`level/2+1`) at Level 1 — a wrinkle that vanishes under a flat port.
- `AddMutation_DefaultLevel3_WhenOmitted` (L160–171): default level is 3.
- `AddMutation_UnknownClass_LogsAndSkips` (L173–184): expects a log matching `AddMutation returned false` and `Assert.DoesNotThrow`. **This is the fail-soft path that makes the three ScenarioCustomSmokeTests silently green — see that row.**

Production target: `Assets/Scripts/Scenarios/Builders/PlayerBuilder.cs:148-158` (`AddMutation(string, int level = 3)`, warns at L153 when there is no `MutationsPart` and at L158 when `AddMutation` returns false). Doc example at `ScenarioContext.cs:114` and `PlayerBuilder.cs:18` also need updating.

**Downstream scenarios that call it (all under `Assets/Scripts/Scenarios/Custom/`):** `LiquidSpellTestBench.cs:116-119` (ArcBolt, Conflagration, IceLance, AcidSpray at level 5), `PacifiedWarden.cs:36` (CalmMutation), `CalmTestSetup.cs:35` (CalmMutation), `CropFarmShowcase.cs:56` (`nameof(ConjureRainMutation)`), `CalmThenWitness.cs`. `LiquidSpellTestBench` is the deterministic self-auditing bench from CLAUDE.md — its matrix is built on 4 mutations and must be re-pointed in M5.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/TestSupport/VerifierTests.cs + PlayerVerifier.cs + ScenarioVerifier.cs

**VerifierTests.cs: 66 tests / 5 hard refs — 4 tests affected, but the whole 66-test file breaks compile. PlayerVerifier.cs: 0 tests, 3 hard refs, and it is the shared harness so its breakage is transitive to everything using `ctx.Verify()`.**

- `PlayerVerifier.HasMutation(string)` at `Assets/Tests/EditMode/TestSupport/PlayerVerifier.cs:120-130` — `Player.GetPart<MutationsPart>()`, fails if null, then `mutations.HasMutation(name)`. **Replace with `HasSkill(string)` calling `SkillsPart.HasSkill`.** Docstring at L116–118 and the class summary at L10 also say "mutations".
- `ScenarioVerifier.cs:32` — the fluent-API usage example in the class docstring shows `.HasMutation("FireBoltMutation")`. Comment-only; update in M5.
- `VerifierTests.Player_HasMutation_Passes` (L304–310): `ctx.Player.AddMutation("FireBoltMutation")` then `.HasMutation("FireBoltMutation")`.
- `VerifierTests.Player_HasMutation_Missing_Fails` (L312–318): counter-check, `Assert.Throws<AssertionException>`.
- `VerifierTests.Player_HasPartOfType_Passes` (L574–579): `ctx.Verify().Player().HasPartOfType<MutationsPart>()` on the REAL `"Player"` blueprint.
- `VerifierTests.Player_HasPartOfType_Missing_Fails` (L581–588): same generic on the STUB player, expects failure. **The counter-check pair survives migration to `SkillsPart` — verified: the real Player blueprint carries both `Skills` and `Mutations` parts (`Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json`, Player entry), and `ScenarioTestHarness.BuildStubPlayer` (`ScenarioTestHarness.cs:126-138`) adds no Parts at all, so `HasPartOfType<SkillsPart>()` passes on the real blueprint and fails on the stub exactly as today.**


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/TestSupport/ScenarioTestHarness.cs + ScenarioTestHarnessTests.cs

**ScenarioTestHarnessTests.cs: 10 tests / 1 hard ref. ScenarioTestHarness.cs: comments only (soft).**

`CreateContext_WithRealPlayerBlueprint_HasMutationsPart` (L58–69) asserts `ctx.PlayerEntity.GetPart<MutationsPart>()` is non-null (L64) with the message "Real Player blueprint should include MutationsPart (the stub doesn't)" (L65), plus `InventoryPart` (L66). **Rename to `..._HasSkillsPart` and swap the generic** — safe, the Player blueprint already declares a `Skills` part.

`ScenarioTestHarness.cs:75` and `:122` are docstring mentions ("InventoryPart, MutationsPart, LightSourcePart" / "Deliberately excludes InventoryPart, MutationsPart, BrainPart"). Comment edits for M5.

**Note the ordering trap:** the harness is used by `VerifierTests` (66), `ScenarioCustomSmokeTests` (55), `SkillsScreenStateBuilderTests` (13) and others. Because there is one asmdef, a broken harness breaks all of them at once.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Presentation/Rendering/HotbarStateBuilderTests.cs + HotbarRendererTests.cs

**HotbarStateBuilderTests.cs: 2 tests / 2 hard refs (L22 `nameof(KindleMutation)`, L29 `nameof(QuenchMutation)`). HotbarRendererTests.cs: 2 tests / 1 hard ref (L109 `nameof(KindleMutation)`). Classification: INFRASTRUCTURE — fix by re-pointing the identifier, do not delete.**

Both pass the mutation class name as `ActivatedAbility.SourceMutationClass`, which `HotbarStateBuilder.cs:24` feeds to `GrimoireTooltipData.GetOrDefault(...)` to resolve the tooltip. `HotbarStateBuilderTests.Build_UsesActivatedAbilitySlots_AndTooltipDisplayData` is the only test proving the hotbar tooltip pipeline resolves at all.

**Production coupling to rename together:** `ActivatedAbility.SourceMutationClass` (`Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Abilities/ActivatedAbility.cs:59`), the ctor param `sourceMutationClass` (`ActivatedAbilitiesPart.cs:64`), and the doc-comment at `GrimoireTooltipData.cs:45`. Two more tests reference the same names: `Tier1ActivatedAbilitiesTests.cs:73` (`SourceMutationClass = ""` in a save round-trip fixture) and `SpellcraftArcaneSurgeTests.cs:49` (`sourceMutationClass: ""` named argument). All four are one-line mechanical edits — but they will each break the build independently if missed.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Save/Tier1ActivatedAbilitiesTests.cs

**6 tests / 1 hard ref at L73 (`SourceMutationClass = ""` inside an `ActivatedAbility` initializer, L63–74). Classification: INFRASTRUCTURE — survives, one-token edit.**

The test builds an `ActivatedAbility` with `Class = "CudgelTree"`, `TargetingMode = AdjacentCell`, `Range = 1`, `CooldownRemaining = 4`, `MaxCooldown = 6`, pushes it into both `AbilityList` and `AbilityByGuid`, round-trips via `PartRoundTripHelper.RoundTripEntityViaTokenGraph`, and asserts the list survives. This is the save-side proof that `ActivatedAbilitiesPart` (which the migration KEEPS) serializes correctly — do not lose it.

If `SourceMutationClass` is renamed (e.g. to `SourceSkillClass`), update this initializer in the same commit.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Skills/SpellcraftArcaneSurgeTests.cs

**4 tests / 1 hard ref at L49 (`sourceMutationClass: ""` named argument, range/param list `range: 1, sourceMutationClass: ""`). Classification: INFRASTRUCTURE — one-token edit, but it is on the SKILL side already.**

Proves the `ActivatedAbilitiesPart.AddAbility(...)` signature is used by skill code with a mutation-named parameter. Renaming the parameter breaks this file. Trivial, but easy to miss because the file has no other mutation reference and greps for `Mutation` in `Skills/` look empty at a glance.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Skills/SkillsPartTests.cs — the propagation contract

**21 tests / 246 skill refs / 7 mutation refs, all in comments or command-name literals. Classification: SOFT (compiles fine) but SEMANTICALLY GUTTED by the migration — flag for review, not for deletion.**

Two tests exist specifically to prove SkillsPart coexists with mutations:
- `HandleEvent_CommandNotMatchingAnyOwnedSkill_DoesNotMarkHandled` (L552–575): fires `GameEvent.New("CommandFireBolt")` at an entity owning only a test skill, asserts `ev.Handled == false` with the message "Command events for unowned skills must NOT be marked Handled by SkillsPart — must propagate to mutations / other Parts" (L572–574). Comment at L556–557: "Otherwise SkillsPart would block mutation commands when a player has both skills + mutations."
- `HandleEvent_NoMatchingSkill_EmitsCommandRejectedDiagWithNoMatchReason` (L655–690): same `"CommandFireBolt"` literal at L674 with the inline comment "// mutation, not owned"; asserts exactly one `category=skill kind=CommandRejected` record whose payload contains `"CommandFireBolt"` and `"no_match"`.

**After migration these still pass** — the command string is arbitrary and the propagation/diag behaviour is unchanged — but their stated rationale evaporates. **They should be RETAINED (the propagation contract still matters for non-skill Parts) with comments and the literal updated** to something like `"CommandNotAThing"`. Other soft refs: L243 ("convention from MutationsPart pattern"), L456, L557, L574, L663.


## Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Combat/PenetrationDiagTests.cs — RENAME TRAP

**14 tests / 10 lowercase-and-capital 'Mutation' hits, ZERO mutation-system references. Classification: FALSE POSITIVE — but the highest-probability accidental-breakage site in M5.**

Three tests are named `PreDamageMutation_*` (L325, L350, L371) and query `Kind = "PreDamageMutation"` (L338, L363, L382). This diag kind is emitted at `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Combat/CombatSystem.cs:898` and means *a `BeforeTakeDamage` listener mutated the damage amount* — it has nothing whatsoever to do with the mutation system.

**Do not let M5's "sweep player-facing 'mutation' prose" pass rename this.** Any global find-replace of `Mutation` across `Assets/Scripts` + `Assets/Tests` silently changes a live diag contract that `diag_query`/`diag_assert` consumers depend on. Same trap, lower stakes, in: `RollPenetrationsAdversarialTests.cs` (10 hits, all "mutation-resistance testing" prose, L10-14, L27, L56, L66, L141-148), `CriticalHitAdversarialTests.cs` (L9, L21, L230), `CriticalHitTests.cs` (L119-122, L176, L211-216), `GasPlasmaPartTests.cs:366`, `ItemTakenEventTests.cs:150,156`, `Wsp84SkillSystemAdversarialTests.cs:30`, `CombatContentAdversarialTests.cs:13`.

## SILENT-PASS HAZARDS — tests that stay green while testing nothing

Six distinct hazards, ordered by severity.

**H1 (CRITICAL) — `ScenarioCustomSmokeTests.cs`, 3 tests.** `CalmTestSetup_Applies_WithoutThrowing` (L54–67), `PacifiedWarden_Applies_WithoutThrowing` (L93–107), `CalmThenWitness_Applies_WithoutThrowing` (L126–138). Each sets `UnityEngine.TestTools.LogAssert.ignoreFailingMessages = true` (L58, L98, L129) and asserts only `Assert.DoesNotThrow(...)`. The scenarios call `ctx.Player.AddMutation("CalmMutation", level:3)` (`Scenarios/Custom/CalmTestSetup.cs:35`, `PacifiedWarden.cs:36`), and `PlayerBuilder.AddMutation` is **fail-soft** — it `Debug.LogWarning`s and returns (`PlayerBuilder.cs:153,158`). After M4 the player gets no Calm at all, the warning is suppressed by design, nothing throws, and **all three tests stay green while the scenarios are inert.** The comment at L56 even pre-announces this ("CalmMutation which doesn't exist until M2"). Fix: after M3 these must assert the power was actually granted (`ctx.Verify().Player().HasSkill(...)`), or `ignoreFailingMessages` must be removed.

**H2 (HIGH) — vacuous reflection loops in the rite suite.** `ConsumingRiteTests.EveryRiteAppearsInTheGrimoirePicker` (L527–540) does `foreach (var t in typeof(BaseMutation).Assembly.GetTypes())` filtered by `typeof(ConsumingRiteBase).IsAssignableFrom(t)`. `ConsumingRiteTests.cs:677` uses the same assembly scan to resolve `grim.MutationClassName`. If the port renames the base class and the filter no longer matches anything, **the loop body never executes and the test passes with zero assertions.** Fix on port: add `Assert.Greater(count, 0, "precondition: the scan found rites")` before the loop's assertions. These are the only two `Assembly.GetTypes()` scans in the whole EditMode suite — everything else uses explicit arrays (e.g. `ConsumingRiteAdversarialTests.cs:443-448`), which fail loudly instead.

**H3 (MEDIUM) — `AbilityManagerStateBuilderTests.Build_SortsByClassThenName_ForDeterministicOrder` (L149–174).** Adds abilities with the literal class strings `"Skills"` and `"Mutations"` (L159–162) and asserts `"Mutations"` sorts before `"Skills"` (L167–169). After M5 collapses all ability classes to `"Skills"`, this test still passes (the sort is string-generic) but no longer describes any state the game can reach. **It is testing a two-class world that no longer exists** — the real post-migration risk is that every ability shares one class and the secondary DisplayName sort becomes the only ordering, which this test would not catch. Rewrite with the real post-migration class strings (tree names).

**H4 (MEDIUM) — negative diag assertions.** `CalmMutationTests.CalmMutation_Cast_ZeroDamage_NoDamageLogLine_NoMutationDamageDiag_HpUnchanged` (L127, query at L161) asserts **zero** records for `Category="damage", Kind="MutationDamage"`. Once the kind is renamed, the query matches nothing by construction and the assertion is trivially satisfied regardless of behaviour. Same shape risk in any ported test that asserts absence of a record. Fix: assert absence of the NEW kind, and add a positive control in the same test.

**H5 (LOW) — `VerifierTests.Player_HasPartOfType_Missing_Fails` (L581–588).** Asserts the stub player LACKS `MutationsPart`. Naively swapping the generic to `SkillsPart` keeps it honest only because `BuildStubPlayer` (`ScenarioTestHarness.cs:126-138`) adds no Parts — **verified safe**, but re-verify if the stub ever gains a `SkillsPart`.

**H6 (LOW) — `SaveSystemDeepDiveTests` L120/L134** uses `"ExtraArmMutation_42"` as a manager-ID string. Passes forever; its referent (`ExtraArmPrototypeMutation`) is deleted, so the test's stated scenario becomes fictional.


## CROSS-SYSTEM CONTRACTS — the risky set, ranked

Ranked by likelihood of silent breakage.

**1. mutation × retaliation/faction (HIGHEST).** `ThrownMutationCombatAdversarialTests.RealMutationCast_AgainstNeutralNpc_ProvokesRetaliation_EndToEnd` (L385) + `MutationFullyResisted_DoesNotProvokeRetaliation` (L443). Only place spell damage meets the real hostility pipeline. Supporting prose in `RetaliationHookTests.cs:8-20,55,104,138`.

**2. mutation × farming/crops.** `CropWateringTests` (12), `CropsWateringAdversarialTests` (6 of 22), `FarmingAuditFixTests` (~5 of 20). `ConjureRainMutation.MOISTURE_TICKS` is read directly by tests (L86, L138) and the crop wetting is a top-up-not-additive contract. **The plan document does not mention farming at all.**

**3. mutation × grimoire-picker/hotbar UI.** `GrimoireTooltipData.IsGrimoireMutation` gates picker visibility (`FarmingAuditFixTests.cs:291-302`; `InventoryUI.cs:3443`) and `SourceMutationClass` drives tooltips (`InventoryUI.cs:3300,3334,3655`, `InputHandler.cs:3871`, `HotbarStateBuilder.cs:24`). Tests: `FarmingAuditFixTests` Sm7d pair, `HotbarStateBuilderTests` (2), `HotbarRendererTests` (1).

**4. mutation × diag.** Kind `"MutationDamage"` emitted at `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:126`; asserted in `ProjectileMutationTests.cs:260` (full payload schema), `ThrownMutationCombatAdversarialTests.cs:275,318,342`, and negatively in `CalmMutationTests.cs:161`. Rite diag: `RiteCast` / `Rejected` in `ConsumingRiteAdversarialTests`; `SingleTargetRite_FilesItsDiagUnderTheVictim` vs `RadiusRite_StillFilesItsDiagUnderTheCaster` in `ConsumingRiteTests`.

**5. mutation × skill (already entangled).** `MutationDamageHelpers.ApplySpellDamage` is called from `BaseSkillPart.cs:201`, `SkillEventDispatcher.cs:170`, `SpellcraftSkill.cs:30,35`. Tests: `Wsp7MagicSkillsTests` L248–325 (4). Plus `SkillsPartTests` L552–575 / L655–690 (event propagation to mutation Parts).

**6. mutation × AI goal stack.** Calm pushes `NoFightGoal`. `CalmMutationTests` (4), `M2AdversarialTests` (3), `M2CoverageGapTests` (4). Plus `ConsumingRiteHypothesisTests.Hypothesis_RadiusRite_CaughtFollowerDoesNotBecomeAPermanentEnemy` (rite × follower/faction).

**7. mutation × elemental/thermal/material pipeline.** The entire `GrimoireSpellTests` surface: `ThermalPart.Temperature`, `BurningEffect`, `WetEffect`, `MaterialPart.Combustibility`, `FuelPart`, chain ignition, conductivity. Element-string facts pinned by comment in `LiquidCoatElementAliasTests.cs:73,85` (ArcBolt→`"Electric"`, Conflagration→`"Heat"`) and `Wsp71ElementalTreesTests.cs:186`.

**8. mutation × tile/liquid propagation.** `TilePropagationTests` L274, L296 (FlamingHands ignites tile oil, oil spreads).

**9. mutation × resonance.** All rite tests initialise `ResonanceSystem` from `Resources/Content/Data/Resonance/Resonance.json`; `Adversarial_EveryRiteDeclaresAReadableResonanceTable` asserts every rite's element resonates (`Multiplier > 1.0f`).

**10. mutation × save.** `Tier1MutationsPartTests` (6, all die), `Tier3CollectionRoundTripTests.cs:17,186` (comments), `SaveSystemDeepDiveTests` L95–134 (manager-ID). Per the user directive, save *compatibility* is a non-issue; the *round-trip contract* for the surviving `ActivatedAbilitiesPart` and `SkillsPart` is not.


## SKILL-SIDE FIXTURE CANON — what ported tests must copy

**73 skill-referencing files / 914 test methods; `Gameplay/Skills/` = 50 files / 616 tests.** There is one dominant modern idiom, established across four files that share it near-verbatim. Copy from these.

**CANONICAL ELEMENTAL-POWER TEMPLATE — `Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Skills/PyromancyFlameJetTests.cs` (23 tests, 5 helpers).** This is the file to clone for every ported elemental mutation.
- `Setup()` L31–36: `MessageLog.Clear(); SkillRegistry.ResetForTests(); Diag.ResetAll();` — **add `AsciiFxBus.Clear()` when porting `ProjectileMutationTests`, which needs it (`ProjectileMutationTests.cs:13`).**
- `MakeBodied(name, hp)` L38–51: Creature tag, Hitpoints, the four elemental resistance stats, Toughness, `RenderPart`, `StatusEffectsPart`, `ActivatedAbilitiesPart`, `SkillsPart`.
- `MakeWall(zone,x,y)` L53–59: Solid-tagged entity — needed by any line/beam test.
- `Ctx(actor, zone, dx, dy, seed = 0)` L61–68: builds `SkillEventContext { Attacker, Defender, Zone, Rng = new Random(seed), DirectionX, DirectionY }`.
- **`Fix<T>(actor) where T : BaseSkillPart, new()` L70–75**: `var skill = new T(); actor.GetPart<SkillsPart>().AddSkill(skill, source: "test"); return skill;` — **this single helper replaces the `AddMutation`+`GetMutation<T>` pair everywhere.**
- `Burning(e)` L77–78 and `Reasons()` L80–87: `Reasons()` concatenates `PayloadJson` from `DiagQuery.Apply(new DiagQuery.Filter { Category="skill", Kind="SkillRejected", Limit=20 })` — **the standard way to assert *why* a cast refused.** Rite refusal tests (`ARite_WithNoInk_RefusesAndSaysWhy`) port straight onto this.
- Cast shape: `skill.OnCommand(Ctx(atk, zone, 1, 0));`
- Spec-assertion shape (L95–105): `var spec = new Pyromancy_FlameJet().DeclareActivatedAbility(null); Assert.AreEqual("CommandFlameJet", spec.Command); Assert.AreEqual(AbilityTargetingMode.DirectionLine, spec.TargetingMode); Assert.AreEqual(Pyromancy_FlameJet.JET_LENGTH, spec.Range); Assert.Greater(spec.Cooldown, 0);` — **this is the direct successor to `ProjectileMutationTests`' `*_Mutate_Registers*AbilityMetadata` tests.**
- Balance-gradient pin (L170–176): compares two named constants across powers (`JET_INTENSITY > SPIT_INTENSITY`). Use this shape for the rites' cold-cast allowances.

**CANONICAL THRESHOLD/CROSS-TREE TEMPLATE — `HydromancyJetBlastTests.cs` (28 tests).** Adds two named constants at L29–34 with source citations: `ConductionThreshold = 0.2f` (ElectrifiedEffect.OnApply) and `FireSuppressionThreshold = 0.35f` (`ThermalPart.cs:117`), plus `Moisture(e)` L83–88. `JetBlast_SoaksPastBothInteractionThresholds` (L112–137) is the template for Quench/ConjureWater/DryingBreeze ports. `JetBlast_SoakingThenElectrifying_DoublesTheCharge_EndToEnd` (L139+) is the template for cross-tree combos (RenderedSteam's wet+burning pair, ArcBolt's wet-doubling), including the `AlwaysZeroRng` deterministic-RNG helper.

**Other files sharing `Fix<T>`:** `GalvanismGroundSurgeTests.cs` (43 tests — the largest single-power file), `CryomancyRimeGripTests.cs` (26), and `World/ZoneTileWritingTests.cs`.

**PLUMBING TEMPLATES (do not re-invent):**
- Activated-ability lifecycle: `SkillActivatedAbilityTests.cs` (8) — nested `TestActiveSkill : BaseSkillPart` overriding `DeclareActivatedAbility` (L26–36: `DisplayName`, `Command`, `Class = "Skills"`, `TargetingMode`, `Range`, `Cooldown`) and `OnCommand`. Pins `ActivatedAbilityID` stamping, `TryRouteSkillCommand` dispatch, cooldown gate, `RemoveSkill` cleanup.
- `SkillsPart` CRUD + diag: `SkillsPartTests.cs` (21) — its docstring L8–28 enumerates the contract (AddSkill instance/string overloads, rollback on hook failure, duplicate returns false, `OnAfterLoad` rebuild from `ParentEntity.Parts`, `skill/Added` + `skill/Removed` diag, remove-unowned no-op counter-check). Nested stub must be `public` and namespace-qualified so `Type.GetType("CavesOfOoo.Tests.SkillsPartTests+TestStubSkill")` resolves (L33–38).
- JSON tree loading: `SkillRegistryTests.cs` (6) — inline JSON string literal with `Skills[].{Name,Class,Cost,Description,Powers[].{Name,Class,Cost,Attribute,Minimum,Description}}`. **This is the shape the new `Rites.json` must match.** Existing trees: `Assets/Resources/Content/Data/Skills/` has 12 files (Acrobatics, Axe, Corrosion, Cryomancy, Cudgel, Galvanism, Hydromancy, LongBlades, Persuasion, Pyromancy, ShortBlades, Spellcraft) — **no Rites.json yet, confirming D3's "NEW tree".**
- SP economy: `BuySkillActionTests.cs` (14) — `MakeActor(sp, agility, strength)` L32–43; pins 9 result paths + `skill/PurchaseAttempted` diag + SP-unchanged counter-check on every failure.
- Save round-trip: `Tier3CollectionRoundTripTests.cs` L57–265 (6 `Adversarial_SkillsPart_*`). Use `PartRoundTripHelper.RoundTripEntityViaTokenGraph` (`PartRoundTripHelper.cs:137`) for anything depending on `OnAfterLoad`; `RoundTripEntityWithBodies` (L74) deliberately bypasses it.
- UI: `SkillsScreenStateBuilderTests.cs` (13) — row states Owned/Buyable/InsufficientSP/RequirementsNotMet, flag obfuscation, prereq + exclusion gating, null-actor empty snapshot. `LivePlayerBlueprint_HasSkillsPartAttached` (L36) and `LivePlayerBlueprint_BuildSnapshot_HasNonEmptyRows` (L47) are the live-blueprint smoke tests M3 should extend for grimoire-taught rows.
- Adversarial sweep template: `Wsp84SkillSystemAdversarialTests.cs` (37) — docstring L11–33 lists the six bug classes and section banners. **The ported rite adversarial file should be merged into this pattern or mirror it.**

**Content-integration setup (needed by the ported grimoire/rite tests, NOT present in any current skill test file):** `EntityFactory` + real `Objects.json` load — copy from `GrimoireSpellTests.cs:24-31` or `ConsumingRiteTests.cs:29-39` (which also inits `ResonanceSystem` from `Resonance.json`).


## COUNTS AND NET DELTA

**Q7 answer, with the arithmetic shown.**

**Test methods that genuinely exercise mutations: ~245.** Breakdown (excluding name-only false positives like `*_NoMutation`):
- `MutationSystemTests` 94 (but only 71 are mutation-semantic; 22 are `ActivatedAbilitiesPart` and 1 is `TurnManager_EndTurn_FiresEndTurnEvent`)
- `GrimoireSpellTests` 41 of 42
- `ConsumingRiteTests` 34 · `ConsumingRiteAdversarialTests` 21 · `RiteTests` 16 · `FulminationTests` 8 of 9 · `ConsumingRiteHypothesisTests` 8 = 87
- `ProjectileMutationTests` 12 · `CalmMutationTests` 4
- `CropWateringTests` 10 of 12 · `CropsWateringAdversarialTests` 6 of 22 · `FarmingAuditFixTests` ~8 of 20 = 24
- `ThrownMutationCombatAdversarialTests` 6 of 17
- `M2CoverageGapTests` 4 · `M2AdversarialTests` 3
- `Tier1MutationsPartTests` 6 · `Wsp7MagicSkillsTests` 4 · `PlayerBuilderTests` 3 · `VerifierTests` 4 · `TilePropagationTests` 2 · `HotbarStateBuilderTests` 1 · `HotbarRendererTests` 1 · `ScenarioTestHarnessTests` 1 · `Tier1ActivatedAbilitiesTests` 1 · `SpellcraftArcaneSurgeTests` 1

**Test methods in files that fail to COMPILE: 489**, across 26 files. This is the number that matters operationally (one asmdef), and it is 2× the semantic number — 244 tests are pure collateral (e.g. 11 of 17 `ThrownMutationCombat`, 16 of 22 `CropsWateringAdversarial`, 62 of 66 `VerifierTests`, 13 of 17 `M2CoverageGap`, 16 of 18 `TilePropagation`).

**Fate of the ~245:**
- **DIE, no replacement: ~78.** 62 from `MutationSystemTests` (BaseMutation level/cap math 7, MutationRegistry 1, MutationsPart CRUD 12, ExtraArm 3, LevelMutation 1, MP economy 5, RandomBuy 6, Esper/Chimera 2, Genome 4, MutationMods 3, DuplicateRanked 1, GetMutatePool 1, Sync 3, Telepathy 4, Regeneration 5, StartingMutations 4) + 6 `Tier1MutationsPartTests` (already superseded by `Tier3CollectionRoundTripTests`' 6 SkillsPart tests) + ~7-10 `ProjectileMutationTests` for unreachable powers.
- **SURVIVE, moved or one-token edit: ~40.** 22 `ActivatedAbilitiesPart` tests (move to a new file — **the biggest silent-loss risk if `MutationSystemTests.cs` is deleted wholesale**), 4 `Wsp7MagicSkillsTests` (rename the helper type), 4 `VerifierTests`, 3 `PlayerBuilderTests`, 2 Hotbar, 1 each `Tier1ActivatedAbilities`/`SpellcraftArcaneSurge`/`ScenarioTestHarnessTests`.
- **PORT to skill tests: ~127.** 41 grimoire spells + 87 rites (minus ~1 overlap) ≈ 127, plus Calm 4+7, farming 24, cross-system 8.

**Realistic net delta.** Hard floor: **−78** if every port is 1:1. But the ported tests will not be 1:1 — current skill-test density runs ~7–9 tests per power (`Pyromancy` 3 powers → 23 tests; `Hydromancy` 3 → 28; `Galvanism` GroundSurge alone → 43), versus ~2.3 tests per power in `GrimoireSpellTests` (41 tests / 18 powers). Porting 29 powers at even a conservative 5 tests/power yields ~145 where 127 exist today.

**Expected: EditMode goes from 6730 to roughly 6750–6900, i.e. net −80 (worst case, ports done lazily 1:1 and the 22 ActivatedAbilities tests are lost) to +170 (ports done at current skill-test density).** The plan's own guard rail (`Docs/MUTATIONS-TO-SKILLS-MIGRATION.md:104`, "test count must not drop by more than the infra-test count") should be quantified as: **the suite must not drop below 6652** (6730 − 78). Any larger drop means behaviour coverage was lost, not just infrastructure.

**Also required by M2 (new, not a port):** a `RitesAdversarialTests.cs`, since the Rites tree will re-satisfy ≥2 of CLAUDE.md's adversarial-gate surfaces (state atomicity via ink, stacking semantics, cross-actor, anti-exploit). Budget ~20–40 additional tests there.


======================================================================
# Part 4
======================================================================

> **NOTE:** **CROSS-CUTTING BLOCKER #1 — Deleting `MutationDamageHelpers` silently kills every elemental-tree passive.** `MutationDamageHelpers.ApplySpellDamage` (Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/MutationDamageHelpers.cs:56-84) is the **only production caller** of `SkillEventDispatcher.GetSpellDamageModifier` (verified by grep across all of Assets/Scripts excluding Tests: the single call site is MutationDamageHelpers.cs:65, invoked from DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:104). Not one shipped skill routes damage through it — `Pyromancy_EmberSpit` (EmberSpit.cs:98), `Pyromancy_FlameJet` (FlameJet.cs:100), `Pyromancy_Pyroclasm` (Pyroclasm.cs:124), `Cryomancy_RimeGrip` (RimeGrip.cs:77), `Galvanism_Overload` (Overload.cs:123), `Galvanism_RailSpike` (RailSpike.cs:97) all call `DestructionSystem.RouteDamage` directly. Consequence today: `PyromancySkill` (+25% Heat on Burning), `CryomancySkill`, `GalvanismSkill`, `CorrosionSkill`, `SpellcraftSkill` (+1), `Spellcraft_Empower` (+2), `Pyromancy_Cinder`, `Pyromancy_HeartFlame` (+100%×3), `Spellcraft_LeyTap` — **nine skills, six of them shipped tree roots** — only ever modify mutation damage. M1 must move `ApplySpellDamage` into a skill-side helper (e.g. `SkillCombatHelpers.ApplySpellDamage`) and route ALL ported spell damage through it, and M5 should retrofit the six existing skill actives. Otherwise the migration deletes the tree economy while every test stays green.

> **NOTE:** **CROSS-CUTTING BLOCKER #2 — No skill in the game emits FX, and `SkillsPart` cannot hold the turn.** `grep -rln 'AsciiFx' Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/` returns **nothing**. Meanwhile 8 of the 18 mutations emit blocking FX (`EmitProjectile`, `EmitChargeOrbit`, `EmitBeam`, `EmitRingWave`, `EmitBurst`, `EmitParticle`) and 8 set `e.SetParameter("BlocksTurnAdvance", true)` — Kindle/IceLance/ArcBolt/AcidSpray/Quench/Calm via DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:65, plus EmberVein:59, Conflagration:53, RimeNova:56, Thunderclap:56, FlamingHands:51. `SkillsPart.HandleEvent` (SkillsPart.cs:395-420) sets `e.Handled = true` and returns false but **never sets `BlocksTurnAdvance`**, and `InputHandler.ResolveAbilityCommand` reads it at InputHandler.cs:3151 to decide between `InputState.WaitingForFxResolution` and immediate `EndTurnAndProcess()`. Port the FX as-is and the turn advances while the animation plays. M1's first step must add the plumb (simplest: have `TryRouteSkillCommand` return a flag, or have `SkillsPart.HandleEvent` set `BlocksTurnAdvance` when `ZoneRenderer.HasBlockingFx` would be true after dispatch).

> **NOTE:** **CROSS-CUTTING BLOCKER #3 — `SkillEventContext` has no `TargetCell`.** `InputHandler.ResolveAbilityCommand` sets `TargetCell` only for `AbilityTargetingMode.AdjacentCell` (InputHandler.cs:3144-3145), and `SkillsPart.HandleEvent` lifts only `Zone`, `RNG`, `DirectionX`, `DirectionY` (SkillsPart.cs:401-405) into a ctx that has no cell field (SkillEventContext.cs:23-89). Three of the 18 are AdjacentCell and read `TargetCell`: `KindleFlameMutation.cs:38`, `HearthwarmMutation.cs:38`, `FlamingHandsMutation.cs:43`. Their ports must derive the cell as `zone.GetCell(actorPos.x + ctx.DirectionX, actorPos.y + ctx.DirectionY)` — DirectionX/Y ARE set for every targeting mode (InputHandler.cs:3140-3141, verified) — or scan all 8 neighbours the way `Pyromancy_Pyroclasm.cs:60-75` does. Either add `TargetCell` to `SkillEventContext` once (cleaner) or repeat the derivation three times.

> **NOTE:** **CROSS-CUTTING BLOCKER #4 — Ported spells vanish from the grimoire picker and every tooltip.** Eight production sites key off `ActivatedAbility.SourceMutationClass`: `InventoryUI.cs:3300, 3334, 3443, 3655`, `InputHandler.cs:3871`, `HotbarStateBuilder.cs:24`, plus `GrimoireTooltipData`. `SkillsPart.AddSkill` registers abilities with `sourceMutationClass: ""` (SkillsPart.cs:134), so every ported power gets an empty key. `InventoryUI.cs:3443` filters the learned-grimoires list with `GrimoireTooltipData.IsGrimoireMutation(ab.SourceMutationClass)` — an empty key fails, so ported spells become **invisible in the grimoire picker**, exactly the failure `GrimoireTooltipData.cs:48-55` documents as load-bearing ("once its hotbar slot is reassigned it can only be re-bound through the M-key ability manager"). M3 must rename the field to something class-agnostic (e.g. `SourceClass`) and have `SkillsPart` populate it with `skill.GetType().Name`. Related: `GrimoireTooltipData` is already stale and incomplete — Kindle's row says "CD 4" vs code CD 6 (GrimoireTooltipData.cs:66 vs KindleMutation.cs:15); Quench's says "1d4 cold" vs code 1d3 untyped (line 73 vs QuenchMutation.cs:17); Conflagration's says "CD 18" vs code 15 (line 80 vs ConflagrationMutation.cs:15); and **six of the 18 have no row at all** (KindleFlame, Hearthwarm, ChillDraft, ConjureWater, DryingBreeze, WardGleam) so they are already invisible in the picker today.

> **NOTE:** **Cooldown semantics change on port.** Mutations charge the cooldown conditionally inside `Cast` — `KindleFlameMutation.cs:75-76` (only if something was heated), `HearthwarmMutation.cs:66` (only after the precondition passed), `WardGleamMutation.cs:81` (only if something was cleansed), `ConjureWaterMutation.cs:112` (only on a successful placement). `SkillsPart.TryRouteSkillCommand` applies it **unconditionally** after `OnCommand` returns (SkillsPart.cs:284: `ability.CooldownRemaining = ability.MaxCooldown;`), with an escape hatch documented at SkillsPart.cs:281-283 ("it can manually set ability.CooldownRemaining = 0 before returning"). Decide per-power whether a wasted cast burns the cooldown and write the test either way; four of the 18 change behaviour by default.

> **NOTE:** **The `"The rite fails to resolve."` UX disappears.** Mutations that return false from `Cast` leave `e.Handled` false, and `InputHandler.cs:3157-3163` prints that generic line. When a skill IS routed but internally bails, `SkillsPart.HandleEvent` has already set `e.Handled = true`, so the player sees **nothing at all**. Every port of a can-fail power (KindleFlame, Hearthwarm, WardGleam, ConjureWater, ChillDraft, DryingBreeze) must emit its own player-facing failure line plus `EmitSkillRejectedDiag(ctx, reason)` (BaseSkillPart.cs:337-352, with the conventional reason vocabulary at 316-329). This is a net improvement — the generic line is itself a silent-failure smell — but it is work, not a freebie.

> **NOTE:** **Diag coverage is near-zero and must be authored, not ported.** Across all 18 mutations there are exactly **three** diag emissions: `damage/MutationDamage` from the projectile base (DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:122-137, and Calm never reaches it because its dice are "0"), `crop/RainConjured` from ConjureRain (ConjureRainMutation.cs:103-105), and `effect/ObjectEffectRefused` inherited free by ArcBolt/AcidSpray/Quench from `ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply` (ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:131-140). **Zero rejection records exist anywhere in the mutation family.** The skill side already has the substrate (`BaseSkillPart.EmitSkillRejectedDiag` + `SkillsPart`'s `CommandRouted`/`CommandRejected`), so per CLAUDE.md §Observability every port should add reject records for no_direction / no_target / line_blocked / no_zone at minimum.

> **NOTE:** **Ability-class strings and the AI.** Mutations register with `abilityClass` = `"Grimoire Spells"` (16 of 18), `"Physical Mutations"` (FlamingHandsMutation.cs:26), or `"Mental Mutations"` (CalmMutation.cs:37). `ActivatedAbilitySpec.Class` defaults to `"Skills"` (ActivatedAbilitySpec.cs:31) and `SkillsPart.AddSkill` falls back to `"Skills"` (SkillsPart.cs:131). Grep the UI for grouping/filtering on those three strings before M5's sweep. Separately: `AIHelpers.TryUseRangedAbility` **skips every AdjacentCell ability** (`if (ability.TargetingMode == AbilityTargetingMode.AdjacentCell) continue;`, AIHelpers.cs:457) and never sets `TargetCell`, so KindleFlame / Hearthwarm / FlamingHands are AI-unusable before and after the port — no regression, but do not claim AI parity for them.

> **NOTE:** **Cost conventions in shipped content are already split, so cost variety is safe.** Every elemental-tree row is `Cost: 1` (Pyromancy.json, Cryomancy.json, Galvanism.json, Hydromancy.json, Corrosion.json, Spellcraft.json), but `Persuasion.json` uses `Cost: 100` (root), `100` (Recruit), `25` (Dismiss) plus `Attribute`/`Minimum` stat gating. `BuySkillAction` fully supports arbitrary integer cost (`spStat.BaseValue -= cost`, BuySkillAction.cs:172), `Requires` prereqs (BuySkillAction.cs:155-160), `Exclusion` (163-168) and stat minimums (147-152) — and **no elemental row uses any of them today**. Against +1 SP/level (LevelingSystem.cs:71-73), Persuasion's 100 is effectively the existing teach-only sentinel, which answers the plan's open question §5.2 with a shipped precedent.

> **NOTE:** **Balance is the real risk, not mechanics.** The mutations were tuned in isolation and are systematically cheaper than their skill counterparts: EmberVein CD 12 vs RailSpike 45 and FlameJet 35; Conflagration CD 15 vs FlameJet 35; RimeNova CD 15 vs ColdSnap 30, delivering a ~24-turn full action lockout (RimeNovaMutation.cs:78-83 does the arithmetic) vs a 6-turn movement slow; Thunderclap CD 18 vs Overload 50, with no conductivity precondition and doubled damage on wet; IceLance CD 8 vs RimeGrip 35 while freezing twice as hard; ArcBolt CD 7 vs RailSpike 45. Ported at their current numbers into the same trees, **eight existing skill actives become strictly dominated** — including two of the four `StartingSpellKit` grants (`Cryomancy_RimeGrip`, `Pyromancy_EmberSpit`, StartingSpellKit.cs:50-53). The plan's Risk table flags "29 powers at 1sp floods the economy"; the sharper risk is cooldown deflation. Recommend a single cooldown-normalisation pass across the merged tree as an explicit M1 sub-step with a user checkpoint, rather than per-power judgement calls.

> **NOTE:** **Test roster to port (M1 must not drop coverage).** `Assets/Tests/EditMode/Gameplay/Mutations/GrimoireSpellTests.cs` holds **42 tests** covering 15 of the 18 (Kindle ×3, Quench ×4, Conflagration ×5, IceLance ×2, AcidSpray ×1, ArcBolt ×2, RimeNova ×2, Thunderclap ×2, EmberVein ×2, KindleFlame ×1, DryingBreeze ×2, Hearthwarm ×3, ConjureWater ×5, ChillDraft ×1, WardGleam ×3, plus 4 GrimoirePart tests). `CalmMutationTests.cs` holds 4 (including `CalmMutation_LevelScalesDuration`, which must be rewritten when Level is flattened). FlamingHands is covered across `MutationSystemTests.cs:1583-1640`, `ProjectileMutationTests.cs`, and `TilePropagationTests.cs:261-300` (`FlamingHands_IgnitesOilOnTheTile`). ConjureRain is covered in `CropWateringTests.cs`, `CropsWateringAdversarialTests.cs`, `FarmingAuditFixTests.cs`. Also touched: `LiquidCoatElementAliasTests.cs` (Conflagration, ArcBolt), `Wsp71ElementalTreesTests.cs` (ArcBolt), `HotbarStateBuilderTests.cs` + `HotbarRendererTests.cs` (Kindle, Quench), `ElementalTargetingTests.cs` (FlamingHands, docstring only), `PlayerBuilderTests.cs` + `ScenarioCustomSmokeTests.cs` (`PlayerBuilder.AddMutation`), `ThrownMutationCombatAdversarialTests.cs` (Calm).

> **NOTE:** **Honesty bounds on this dossier set.** (1) All numbers, line citations, and call sites were read from source in this session; docstrings were checked against code and the disagreements are called out (Kindle CD, Quench element, Conflagration CD, ThermalPart flame gates). (2) The FlameTemperature census behind the KindleFlame finding was computed over `Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json` only — if any other content file authors a Thermal part, the "DryBrush is the only target" conclusion needs re-checking. (3) `Level` is asserted constant-1 in live play on the strength of `BaseMutation.CalcLevel` (BaseMutation.cs:152-172) plus the plan's F5 claim that `SpendMPToIncreaseMutation` has no non-test callers; I did not independently re-grep that call graph. (4) Tree assignments and SP costs are design judgement built on the code's power budgets, not derived from code — every one of them is a user decision. (5) I did not run the game or the test suite; nothing here is verified by execution.


## KindleMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/KindleMutation.cs (45 lines).

BASE CLASS: `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase` (Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:11). The base supplies, in full: (a) ability registration in `Mutate` (line 30-39) via `AddMyActivatedAbility(DisplayName, CommandName, AbilityClass, AbilityTargetingMode.DirectionLine, AbilityRange)`; (b) `HandleEvent` (48-68) which reads `Zone`/`SourceCell`/`RNG` from the OBJECT param dict and `DirectionX`/`DirectionY` via `GetIntParameter` (53-57 — the CORRECT dictionary), guards `zone==null || sourceCell==null || (dx==0&&dy==0)` (59-60), calls `Cast`, then sets `BlocksTurnAdvance=true` + `e.Handled=true` (65-67); (c) `Cast` (70-165): `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact(zone, ParentEntity, sourceCell.X, sourceCell.Y, dx, dy, AbilityRange)` (75-82) → bail on empty path (84-85) → `AsciiFxBus.EmitProjectile(zone, trace.Path, FxTheme, trail:true, blocksTurnAdvance:true)` (87) → if `trace.HitEntity != null`: `DiceRoller.Roll(DamageDice, rng)` (92), `MutationDamageHelpers.ApplySpellDamage(target, damage, ElementAttribute, ParentEntity, zone)` (104-105), damage message (111-113), `damage/MutationDamage` diag (122-137), then the on-hit gate (147-152: `target.GetStatValue("Hitpoints",0) > 0 || (!HasTag("Creature") && DestructiblePart != null && !IsDestroyed)`) → `ApplyOnHitEffect`; else blocked/dissipate messages (154-161); finally `CooldownMyActivatedAbility(ActivatedAbilityID, CooldownTurns)` (163).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandKindle" (KindleMutation.cs:11). FxTheme = `AsciiFxTheme.Fire` (14). CooldownTurns = **6** (15). AbilityRange = **5** (16). DamageDice = **"1d4"** (17). AbilityClass = "Grimoire Spells" (18). ImpactVerb = "sears" (19). ElementAttribute = **"Fire"** (20) — maps to `DamageAttributeFlags.Heat` (Damage.cs GetFlagForAttribute case "Fire"/"Heat") and satisfies `PyromancySkill.OnGetSpellDamageModifier`'s gate (PyromancySkill.cs:53 accepts "Heat" OR "Fire"). Name/DisplayName = "Kindle", MutationType "Mental" (22-24).

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. Base reads params, traces line, emits fire projectile FX with trail, holds the turn.
2. On hit: roll 1d4 → `ApplySpellDamage` adds `SkillEventDispatcher.GetSpellDamageModifier(...)` (MutationDamageHelpers.cs:65-66), builds `Damage` with attributes `"Spell"` + `"Fire"` (74-78), and delivers via `DestructionSystem.RouteDamage` (83) so scenery's `DestructiblePart` pool is hit rather than a missing Hitpoints stat.
3. On-hit effect (KindleMutation.cs:26-43): builds `GameEvent.New("ApplyHeat")`, sets `Joules = FireDose.Ignition` (34) = **600f** (FireDose.cs:65, `TinderIgnition(300f) * 2.0`), `Radiant=false` (35), `Source=ParentEntity` (36), `Zone=zone` (37), `target.FireEvent(heatEvent)` (38), `heatEvent.Release()` (39).
4. `MaterialSimSystem.EmitHeatToAdjacent(target, zone, 60f)` (42) — MaterialSimSystem.cs:103-139 divides by 8 → 7.5J per neighbouring ThermalPart entity. Effectively decorative at that magnitude.
5. Base applies the 6-turn cooldown.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact` (base:75); `AsciiFxBus.EmitProjectile` (base:87); `DiceRoller.Roll` (base:92); `MutationDamageHelpers.ApplySpellDamage` (base:104) → `SkillEventDispatcher.GetSpellDamageModifier` (MutationDamageHelpers.cs:65) → `DestructionSystem.RouteDamage` (MutationDamageHelpers.cs:83); `GameEvent "ApplyHeat"` → `ThermalPart.HandleApplyHeat` (ThermalPart.cs:34) → TryIgnite → wet-suppression at Moisture>0.35 (ThermalPart.cs:~117) → `TryIgnite` event → `MaterialPart` veto when Combustibility==0 → `BurningEffect`; `MaterialSimSystem.EmitHeatToAdjacent` (KindleMutation.cs:42). NOT called: `ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply`, `ZoneTileStateSystem`.

DIAG: one record — category `damage`, kind `MutationDamage`, payload `{mutationClass, diceRolled, rawRoll, elementAttribute, actualDamage}` (base:122-137), only when damage > 0. Zero rejection records anywhere in the base — no `no_direction`, `no_target`, `line_blocked` equivalents exist today.

LEVEL: **not read** (0 references).

BROKEN PATH: **LineTargeting Wall/Terrain skip.** `LineTargeting.GetFirstTargetableObject` (LineTargeting.cs:171) does `if (entity.HasTag("Creature") || entity.HasTag("Wall") || entity.HasTag("Terrain")) continue;`. The `Terrain` base blueprint carries Tag `Terrain` (Objects.json), and Bush, Campfire, WaterPuddle, OilSlick, **DryBrush**, TarSeep all inherit it — so a Kindle bolt flies straight THROUGH every one of them and through breakable `Wall`s. The one fire spell whose whole purpose is lighting things cannot hit the game's most flammable tile scenery. Secondary defect: LineTargeting.cs:174 accepts any `PhysicsPart` holder, so a corpse or dropped item one cell nearer eats the bolt (audit F12). SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: `SkillLine.Collect(zone, actor, x, y, dx, dy, range, out blockedByWall)` (SkillLine.cs:90-133) — collects creatures first within each cell (113), then any non-Creature passing `AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget` (118-121: DestructiblePart-not-Gone OR MaterialPart OR ThermalPart, AbilityTargeting.cs:38-52), and stops on `cell.IsSolid()` AFTER collecting the cell (129). Take `line[0]` like `Pyromancy_EmberSpit` does (Pyromancy_EmberSpit.cs:80). NOTE the corpse-priority defect survives the port: SkillLine still returns a nearer corpse before a further monster.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS (all from the base, `DisplayName`="Kindle", verb="sears"):
- hit: `"<caster> sears <target> for <N> damage!"` (base:111-113)
- solid blocker: `"<caster>'s Kindle splashes against an obstacle!"` (base:156)
- nothing hit: `"<caster>'s Kindle dissipates harmlessly."` (base:160)
- downstream: `"<target> catches fire!"` (BurningEffect.cs:83)
- grimoire learn: `"Flame-script blazes across the page. The kindle rite burns itself into your mind."` / already-known `"You already know the kindle rite."` (KindleGrimoire, Objects.json)

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: `Pyromancy_EmberSpit` (Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/Pyromancy_EmberSpit.cs). Numeric deltas — damage: 1d4 (avg 2.5) vs flat `SPIT_DAMAGE = 3` → **−0.5 avg, +variance**; range: 5 vs `SPIT_RANGE = 4` → **+1**; cooldown: 6 vs `COOLDOWN = 8` → **−2 turns**; ignition: 600J through the thermal pipeline vs `PyroIgnition.TryIgnite(target, 0.6f, ...)` (EmberSpit:107) which applies `BurningEffect(0.6)` straight through `ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply` (PyroIgnition.cs:72-74); EmberSpit additionally writes `ZoneTileStateSystem.WriteResidue(..., "embers", 4, ...)` (EmberSpit:88-89) and calls `ResolveAfterAbility` (114) — Kindle does neither.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Pyromancy** (matches plan D3). Recommended class name `Pyromancy_Kindle`, command kept as `"CommandKindle"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**. Reasoning: it is functionally EmberSpit-with-a-hotter-pulse and EmberSpit is already the tree's Cost-1 filler and a `StartingSpellKit` grant (StartingSpellKit.cs:50). Shipping both at Cost 1 makes Kindle strictly better (−2 CD, +1 range, 600J vs 0.6-intensity ignition), so either (i) price Kindle at 1 and retune EmberSpit's identity note ("cheapest cooldown in the tree", EmberSpit.cs:26-28, which a 6-CD sibling breaks and its pinning test enforces), or (ii) fold Kindle INTO EmberSpit and keep only the grimoire as an alternate acquisition route. Option (ii) is the honest merge and I recommend it if the user accepts losing the distinct command name.


## KindleFlameMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/KindleFlameMutation.cs (81 lines).

BASE CLASS: `BaseMutation` directly (line 5) — NOT the projectile base. From `BaseMutation` it uses only `AddMyActivatedAbility` (BaseMutation.cs:210-227), `RemoveMyActivatedAbility` (232-237) and `CooldownMyActivatedAbility` (242-247). Everything else (the ~60 lines of `CalcLevel`/`GetMutationCap`/`RapidLevel` at BaseMutation.cs:116-201) is inert for this class.

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandKindleFlame" (line 7). COOLDOWN = **2** (line 8) — the shortest cooldown of any of the 18. Registered `AddMyActivatedAbility(DisplayName, COMMAND, "Grimoire Spells", AbilityTargetingMode.AdjacentCell, 1)` (17-22). No damage dice, no element attribute, no FX theme, no RNG use.

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. `HandleEvent` (32-44): early-out unless `e.ID == COMMAND`; reads `Zone` (37) and **`TargetCell`** (38) from the object dict. NOTE: no `SourceCell`, no direction, no RNG. Calls `Cast(zone, targetCell)`; on false returns true (unhandled) → InputHandler prints `"The rite fails to resolve."` (InputHandler.cs:3159). On true sets `e.Handled = true` (42) but **never sets `BlocksTurnAdvance`** — the turn advances immediately.
2. `Cast` (46-79): null-guards zone/targetCell/ParentEntity (48-49).
3. Reverse-iterates `targetCell.Objects` (52).
4. **Skips every `Creature`** (55-56) — this is a props-only spell.
5. Gate (58-60): `ThermalPart thermal = entity.GetPart<ThermalPart>(); if (thermal == null || thermal.FlameTemperature >= 250f) continue;`
6. For each survivor: `GameEvent.New("ApplyHeat")` with `Joules = FireDose.Cantrip` (66) = **150f** (FireDose.cs:59, `TinderIgnition(300) * 0.5`), `Radiant=false`, `Source=ParentEntity`, `Zone=zone`, fire, release (62-71). Sets `affectedAny = true`.
7. Cooldown applied **only if something was affected** (75-76). Returns `affectedAny`.
8. **Zero message-log output on success.** The only player feedback is whatever `BurningEffect.OnApply` emits downstream.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `GameEvent "ApplyHeat"` → `ThermalPart.HandleApplyHeat` (ThermalPart.cs:34) only. Nothing else — no `RouteDamage`, no `ObjectStatusMatrix`, no `ZoneTileStateSystem`, no `AsciiFxBus`, no `MaterialSimSystem`.

DIAG: **none.** Zero records on any path.

LEVEL: **not read** (0 references).

SporeShambler `Inherits: Creature` so it is already excluded by the creature guard at line 55-56. TarSeep is permanently un-ignitable (audit F3: Volatility 30 → effective flame point `200 − 30*100 = −2800` via `ThermalPart.GetEffectiveFlameTemperature`, so the upward crossing can never fire). **OilSlick sits at exactly 250 and is excluded by the `>=`.** Net: in all shipped content KindleFlame can do exactly one thing — light a DryBrush (Temp 25, FlameTemperature 180, HeatCapacity 0.4 → `delta = 150/0.4 = 375` → 400°, ignites in one cast). You learn kindle flame."` / `"You already know kindle flame."` (KindleFlameGrimoire, Objects.json). The port MUST add a success and a failure line — with no `BlocksTurnAdvance` and no message, a successful cast is currently indistinguishable from a no-op.

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: none is close. Shape-wise the nearest active is `Pyromancy_Pyroclasm` (AdjacentCell, Range 1, scans all 8 neighbour cells for a target via `AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget`, Pyromancy_Pyroclasm.cs:60-75) — copy that neighbour-scan idiom since ctx has no TargetCell. Numeric deltas vs Pyroclasm: cooldown 2 vs `COOLDOWN = 40` → **−38**; damage 0 vs `consumedDuration × DAMAGE_PER_BURN_TURN(3)`; radius: single cell vs 3×3.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Pyromancy** (matches plan D3). Class `Pyromancy_KindleFlame`, command `"CommandKindleFlame"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**. Reasoning: it is a true cantrip (CD 2, no damage, props only) and after the FlameTemperature gate is removed it becomes the cheap "light the campfire / torch / brush" utility the fiction promises. At Cost 1 it is the natural first Pyromancy purchase and does not compete with EmberSpit (which damages creatures). If the gate is NOT removed, do not port it at all — it is one-blueprint dead content.

6. Per-cell heat pass (106-132): for every `trace.Path` point, get the cell, reverse-iterate `cell.Objects` with a re-fetched `Count` guard (`if (i >= cell.Objects.Count) continue;` 115) because reactions can mutate the collection; skip `ParentEntity` (117-118); if `entity.HasPart<ThermalPart>()` (120) fire `ApplyHeat` with `Joules = FireDose.Attack` (124) = **300f** (FireDose.cs:62), Radiant false, Source, Zone (125-129).

The class compensates with the separate per-cell ThermalPart pass, so a row of crates DOES ignite (the docstring's signature moment, 6-13) but never takes a point of damage. Also `HasBlockingSolid` (SpellTargeting.cs:305-320) stops the beam on any non-creature with the `Solid` tag — so a Tree (Tag `Solid`, Objects.json) ends the beam at the tree's cell but the tree is still heated by the path pass, since the blocking cell IS added to `Path` before the return (56-57, 68-72). Then damage via `DestructionSystem.RouteDamage` with attributes `Fire`+`Heat` so crates take structural damage as well as heat.
