Deltas vs RailSpike: range 7 vs `SPIKE_RANGE = 6` → **+1**; damage 2d6 (avg 7) vs flat `SPIKE_DAMAGE = 5` → **+2 avg**; cooldown 12 vs `COOLDOWN = 45` → **−33 turns** (Ember Vein is drastically cheaper for a comparable AoE — the single biggest balance gap in the whole port). Reasoning: this is the tree's long-range heavy and at CD 12 it out-performs both Flame Jet (CD 35) and Rail Spike (CD 45) on every axis. Either raise its cooldown into the 30-40 band on port (my recommendation: **CD 30**, preserving 2d6/range 7) or leave the numbers and gate it behind cost + prereq. Do not ship CD 12 at Cost 1 — it makes Flame Jet and Rail Spike unbuyable.

Also on port it MUST route ignition through `PyroIgnition.TryIgnite` — otherwise it is the one spell that makes soaking a target pointless, which contradicts the grammar the whole Hydromancy tree teaches (PyroIgnition.cs:19-28).


## FlamingHandsMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/FlamingHandsMutation.cs (149 lines). **One of the two mutations on the Player blueprint** (`StartingMutations = "FlamingHandsMutation:1,CalmMutation:1"`, Objects.json:872) — no grimoire teaches it.

BASE CLASS: `BaseMutation` directly (line 11). `MutationType => "Physical"` (17) — the only Physical one of the 18.

DECLARATION: COMMAND_NAME = "CommandFlamingHands" (13). COOLDOWN = **10** (14). Registered `AddMyActivatedAbility("Flaming Hands", COMMAND_NAME, "Physical Mutations", AbilityTargetingMode.AdjacentCell, 1)` (23-28) — ability class is **"Physical Mutations"**, not "Grimoire Spells". Damage: `Level × 1d4` (110-111). Heat: `FireDose.Attack + (Level − 1) × FireDose.Cantrip` (134-135) = **300f at Level 1**.

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. `HandleEvent` (38-57): reads `TargetCell` (43), `Zone` (44), `RNG` (45); guards null cell/zone (47-48); calls `Cast`; sets `BlocksTurnAdvance = true` (51) and `e.Handled = true` (52) **unconditionally** — this mutation always "succeeds" from the input layer's point of view.
2. `Cast` (62-147): `AsciiFxBus.EmitChargeOrbit(zone, ParentEntity, 1, 0.15f, Fire, true)` (67-68); `AsciiFxBus.EmitBurst(zone, targetCell.X, targetCell.Y, Fire, true, delay: 0.15f)` (71-72).
3. **Tile write** (79-81): `ZoneTileStateSystem.ApplyFireToTile(zone, targetCell.X, targetCell.Y, ParentEntity, Name)` → `AddHeat(zone, x, y, 1, ...)` (ZoneTileStateSystem.cs:205-210), then `ZoneTileStateSystem.ResolveAfterAbility(zone, ParentEntity)` (81) so an oil slick goes up on the cast rather than at end of turn.
4. Target collection (87-93): builds `List<Entity>` from `targetCell.Objects` filtered by **`AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget(candidate, ParentEntity)`** (91) — creatures, DestructiblePart holders, MaterialPart or ThermalPart holders (AbilityTargeting.cs:38-52).
5. Empty → `MessageLog.Add($"{caster} blasts the empty space with flames!")` (97).
6. Per target, reverse-iterated (104-142): skip self (107); `totalDamage = Σ_{d<Level} DiceRoller.Roll(4, rng)` (109-111) — at Level 1 this is a single 1d4; build `new Damage(totalDamage)` + `AddAttribute("Heat")` (121-122); `int landed = DestructionSystem.RouteDamage(target, heatDmg, ParentEntity, zone)` (123-124); log the LANDED value (125); then `ApplyHeat` with `FireDose.Attack + (Level-1)*FireDose.Cantrip` (134-135), Radiant false, Source, Zone (136-140).
7. `CooldownMyActivatedAbility(ActivatedAbilityID, 10)` (146) — applied unconditionally, even on an empty cell.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `AsciiFxBus.EmitChargeOrbit` (67), `AsciiFxBus.EmitBurst` (71); `ZoneTileStateSystem.ApplyFireToTile` (79-80) + `ResolveAfterAbility` (81); `AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget` (91); `DiceRoller.Roll(int, rng)` (111); `DestructionSystem.RouteDamage` (123-124); `GameEvent "ApplyHeat"` → ThermalPart (128-140).

DIAG: **none.**

LEVEL: **READ — 5 references** (8, 60, 103, 110, 135). It is one of only two of the 18 that touch Level. In live play `Level` is always 1: `BaseMutation.CalcLevel` (152-172) = `BaseLevel(1) + GetRapidLevelAmount()(0) + MutationsPart.GetLevelAdjustmentsForMutation(0)`, capped by `GetMutationCapForLevel(entityLevel) = entityLevel/2+1 ≥ 1`, and `SpendMPToIncreaseMutation` has zero non-test callers (plan F5). So flatten to: **damage = 1d4, heat = 300f (FireDose.Attack)** with zero balance drift.

BROKEN PATH: **NONE of the four.** This is the ONE already-fixed mutation of the 18 — it uses `AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget` instead of a creature-tag filter (91, with the fix documented in the comment at 84-86), `DestructionSystem.RouteDamage` instead of `ApplyDamage` (123, comment 117-120), `ZoneTileStateSystem.ApplyFireToTile` for the ground layer (79, comment 74-78), and `FireDose.Attack` instead of the old `totalDamage * 5` (135, comment 130-133). Port it verbatim — it is the reference implementation. The ONE thing it lacks is the `PyroIgnition` wet-suppression route (it applies raw heat, which DOES respect ThermalPart's own 0.35 moisture gate at ThermalPart.cs:~117, so it is already consistent). Porting hazard: AdjacentCell means `SkillEventContext` has no `TargetCell` — derive it as `zone.GetCell(actorPos.x + ctx.DirectionX, actorPos.y + ctx.DirectionY)` (InputHandler.cs:3140 sets DirectionX/Y for ALL targeting modes, verified) and reject with `EmitSkillRejectedDiag(ctx, "no_direction")` when both are 0.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS:
- empty cell: `"<caster> blasts the empty space with flames!"` (97)
- per target: `"<caster> blasts <target> with flames for <landed> damage!"` (125)
- grimoire: none — Player-blueprint grant only.

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: `Pyromancy_EmberSpit`. Deltas: shape adjacent-cell-AoE (every elemental target in ONE cell) vs line range-4 single target; damage 1d4 (avg 2.5) vs flat `SPIT_DAMAGE = 3` → **−0.5 avg**; cooldown 10 vs 8 → **+2**; heat 300J thermal vs `PyroIgnition.TryIgnite(0.6f)`; tile write: FlamingHands writes fire to the tile (79) where EmberSpit writes `"embers"` residue for 4 turns (EmberSpit.cs:88-89).

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Pyromancy** (matches plan D3). Class `Pyromancy_FlamingHands`, command `"CommandFlamingHands"`. Recommended SP cost: **1** — and because it is a Player-blueprint starting power, it should be added to `StartingSpellKit.SpellClasses` (StartingSpellKit.cs:48-54) rather than bought, replacing the `StartingMutations` param. Note the kit currently grants 4 spells one per element; adding FlamingHands makes two Pyromancy grants, so consider swapping it in FOR `Pyromancy_EmberSpit` (it is the more distinctive of the two: adjacent AoE that writes fire to the ground) or keeping both and flagging the kit-size change for user review.


## HearthwarmMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/HearthwarmMutation.cs (70 lines).

BASE CLASS: `BaseMutation` directly (line 5).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandHearthwarm" (7). COOLDOWN = **4** (8). Registered `AddMyActivatedAbility(DisplayName, COMMAND, "Grimoire Spells", AbilityTargetingMode.AdjacentCell, 1)` (17-22). No damage, no dice, no RNG, no element attribute, no FX.

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. `HandleEvent` (32-44): reads `Zone` (37) and `TargetCell` (38). If `Cast` returns false, returns true (unhandled) → `"The rite fails to resolve."` (InputHandler.cs:3159). On success sets `e.Handled = true` (42) but **never `BlocksTurnAdvance`**.
2. `Cast` (46-68): null-guards (48-49).
3. **Precondition scan** (51-61): forward-iterates `targetCell.Objects`, sets `hasThermalTarget = true` on the first `HasPart<ThermalPart>()`; if none found, `return false` — the cast is refused, no cooldown, and the player sees the generic rite-failure line.
4. `ParentEntity.ApplyEffect(new HearthAuraEffect(targetCell.X, targetCell.Y, duration: 3, joulesPerPulse: 60f), ParentEntity, zone)` (63-65) — **the effect goes on the CASTER, not the target cell**; it is a persistent emitter that remembers a coordinate pair.
5. `CooldownMyActivatedAbility(ActivatedAbilityID, 4)` (66).

THE EFFECT (Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Effects/Concrete/HearthAuraEffect.cs, 77 lines):
- `Duration = 3` by default (18) or from the ctor arg (26); `JoulesPerPulse = 60f` (14/25); `TargetX`/`TargetY` (12-13).
- `OnApply` logs `"<caster> kindles a gentle hearth aura."` (31).
- `OnTurnStart(target, context)` (34-59): reads `Zone` from the turn event (36), gets `zone.GetCell(TargetX, TargetY)` (40), reverse-iterates its objects (44), skips non-ThermalPart (47-48), fires `ApplyHeat` with `Joules = 60f`, `Radiant = false`, `Source = <the aura's carrier>`, `Zone` (50-56), then calls **`MaterialReactionResolver.EvaluateReactions(entity, zone, null)`** (57) — the only one of the 18 that drives the reaction resolver directly on a heat pulse.
- `OnStack` (61-73): re-points TargetX/Y, takes the larger Duration — so recasting retargets rather than stacking.
- `GetRenderColorOverride() => "&y"` (75).
- Total delivered: **3 pulses × 60J = 180J into a fixed cell**, and the cell does not follow the caster.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `Entity.ApplyEffect(HearthAuraEffect)` (63); then per-turn `GameEvent "ApplyHeat"` → ThermalPart (HearthAuraEffect.cs:50-56) and `MaterialReactionResolver.EvaluateReactions` (HearthAuraEffect.cs:57).

DIAG: **none** (neither the mutation nor the effect).

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: none of the four named classes. Real defects: (a) the precondition scan (51-61) means aiming at a bare floor cell prints the generic `"The rite fails to resolve."` with no explanation — exactly the silent-failure class the observability doctrine forbids; (b) no `BlocksTurnAdvance` and no FX, so the cast is visually invisible; (c) AdjacentCell → no `TargetCell` in `SkillEventContext`. SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: derive the cell from `ctx.DirectionX/Y` (see FlamingHands dossier), replace the `HasPart<ThermalPart>()` scan with `AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget`, and emit `EmitSkillRejectedDiag(ctx, "no_thermal_target")` + a player-facing line instead of silently refusing. Keep `HearthAuraEffect` unchanged — it is not part of the mutation system and survives M4 deletion.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS:
- on apply (from the effect): `"<caster> kindles a gentle hearth aura."` (HearthAuraEffect.cs:31)
- on failure: none authored — the InputHandler's `"The rite fails to resolve."` is all the player gets.
- grimoire learn: `"Steady embers glow between the lines. You learn hearthwarm."` / `"You already know hearthwarm."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: nothing in any tree applies a persistent self-carried emitter. Closest by intent is `Pyromancy_Oilmark` (writes a persistent ground state, no damage — Pyromancy_Oilmark.cs); closest by shape is `Pyromancy_Pyroclasm`'s adjacency scan. Deltas vs Oilmark: Hearthwarm has CD 4 vs Oilmark's cooldown, delivers heat over 3 turns rather than laying fuel, and is single-cell rather than 4-cell.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Pyromancy** (matches plan D3). Class `Pyromancy_Hearthwarm`, command `"CommandHearthwarm"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**. Reasoning: it is a pure non-combat utility (its shipped test is `Hearthwarm_CooksRawMeat_Over3Turns`, GrimoireSpellTests) with no damage and a 4-turn cooldown. It is the tree's "cook a meal / dry a thing / slowly light a stubborn prop" node and is priced correctly at the floor. Do NOT raise its cooldown — the short CD is what makes a 60J-per-pulse aura useful at all.


## IceLanceMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/IceLanceMutation.cs (44 lines).

BASE CLASS: `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase` (line 11). See the KindleMutation dossier for the full base walkthrough (DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:30-167) — identical machinery: DirectionLine registration, `GetIntParameter` direction reads, `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact`, projectile FX, `ApplySpellDamage`, damage message, `MutationDamage` diag, the on-hit gate at 147-152, and the cooldown at 163.

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandIceLance" (13). FxTheme = `AsciiFxTheme.Ice` (16). CooldownTurns = **8** (17). AbilityRange = **6** (18). DamageDice = **"1d6"** (19). AbilityClass = "Grimoire Spells" (20). ImpactVerb = "impales" (21). ElementAttribute = **"Cold"** (22) → `DamageAttributeFlags.Cold`, satisfies `CryomancySkill.OnGetSpellDamageModifier` (CryomancySkill.cs:42).

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST:
1-2. As Kindle: trace, ice projectile FX, roll 1d6, `ApplySpellDamage` with `"Spell"`+`"Cold"` attributes through `RouteDamage`, log, diag.
3. `ApplyOnHitEffect` (28-42) is a **single heat event and nothing else**: `GameEvent.New("ApplyHeat")` with `Joules = -300f` (36), `Radiant = false` (37), `Source = ParentEntity` (38), `Zone = zone` (39), `target.FireEvent` (40), `Release` (41).
4. The design is deliberate and documented (30-34): **do NOT pre-apply `FrozenEffect`.** Letting `ThermalPart.HandleApplyHeat` cross `FreezeTemperature` downward means `ThermalPart.TryFreeze` (ThermalPart.cs:~150-172) applies `FrozenEffect(cold: 1.0f)` AFTER the cooling, so `FrozenEffect.OnApply`'s brittle-shatter check runs against the post-cool temperature and brittle metal actually shatters. Contrast RimeNova, which DOES pre-apply.
5. Base applies the 8-turn cooldown.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact` (base:75); `AsciiFxBus.EmitProjectile` (base:87); `DiceRoller.Roll` (base:92); `MutationDamageHelpers.ApplySpellDamage` (base:104) → `GetSpellDamageModifier` → `RouteDamage`; `GameEvent "ApplyHeat"` → `ThermalPart.HandleApplyHeat` → `TryFreeze` → `FrozenEffect` (IceLanceMutation.cs:35-41). NOT called: `ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply` (this is why the audit notes "Ice Lance freezes stone" — `TryFreeze` fires a cancellable `TryFreeze` event but there is no material listener that vetoes on the Freezable tag list, unlike `ObjectStatusMatrix.Table[typeof(FrozenEffect)]` which requires Wet/Water/Liquid/Ice/Organic, ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:89).

DIAG: one `damage/MutationDamage` record (base:122-137).

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: **LineTargeting Wall/Terrain skip** (LineTargeting.cs:171) — same as Kindle: bolts pass through Bush/Campfire/WaterPuddle/OilSlick/DryBrush/TarSeep/breakable Wall, which for a freeze spell is especially costly (a WaterPuddle is the single most sensible Ice Lance target and it is unhittable). Plus LineTargeting.cs:174's corpse/loot priority (audit F12). Secondary, unlisted in the four: **`TryFreeze` has no material veto** (audit §4), so Ice Lance can freeze stone. SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: `SkillLine.Collect(...)` + `line[0]` exactly as `Cryomancy_RimeGrip.cs:57-69`; for the freeze itself, prefer `ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply(new FrozenEffect(...), target, actor, zone)` over the thermal-crossing route if you want the material gate — but note that changes the mechanic (you lose the post-cool brittle-shatter ordering the docstring exists to preserve). Recommendation: keep the -300J thermal route (it is the mechanic) AND add the material veto in `ThermalPart.TryFreeze` as a separate P0 fix, so the two authorities agree.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS (base, DisplayName="Ice Lance", verb="impales"):
- hit: `"<caster> impales <target> for <N> damage!"` (base:111-113)
- blocked: `"<caster>'s Ice Lance splashes against an obstacle!"` (base:156)
- nothing hit: `"<caster>'s Ice Lance dissipates harmlessly."` (base:160)
- downstream: `"<target> is frozen!"` (FrozenEffect.cs:60), `"<target> thaws."` (FrozenEffect.cs:85)
- grimoire learn: `"Crystalline glyphs bite your fingertips. The ice lance rite chills into your mind."` / `"You already know the ice lance rite."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: `Cryomancy_RimeGrip` (Cryomancy_RimeGrip.cs) — a near twin. Deltas: damage 1d6 (avg 3.5) vs flat `GRIP_DAMAGE = 4` → **−0.5 avg**; range 6 vs `GRIP_RANGE = 5` → **+1**; cooldown 8 vs `COOLDOWN = 35` → **−27 turns**; freeze path −300J thermal → `FrozenEffect(1.0)` from `ThermalPart.TryFreeze` vs RimeGrip's direct `target.ApplyEffect(new FrozenEffect(GRIP_COLD = 0.5f), ...)` (RimeGrip.cs:86) → **Ice Lance freezes twice as hard AND at 1/4 the cooldown**. `FrozenEffect.AllowAction` blocks all action at any positive Cold, so both are full lockouts; the difference is thaw duration.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Cryomancy** (matches plan D3). Class `Cryomancy_IceLance`, command `"CommandIceLance"`. Recommended SP cost: **2**, and **raise the cooldown to ~20 on port**. Reasoning: at CD 8 with a full action-lockout it invalidates `Cryomancy_RimeGrip` (CD 35, a `StartingSpellKit` grant, StartingSpellKit.cs:53) outright — an 8-turn perma-freeze is the strongest single-target effect in the game per cooldown-second. Either raise the CD or reduce the delivered cold (e.g. cap the crossing so `FrozenEffect(0.5)` results). Flag the exact number for user review; the port itself is mechanically trivial.


## RimeNovaMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/RimeNovaMutation.cs (168 lines).

BASE CLASS: `BaseMutation` directly (line 14).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandRimeNova" (16). RADIUS = **2** (17). COOLDOWN = **15** (18). FX constants `ChargeDuration = 0.12f` (19), `RingStepDuration = 0.08f` (20). Registered `AddMyActivatedAbility(DisplayName, COMMAND, "Grimoire Spells", AbilityTargetingMode.SelfCentered, RADIUS)` (29-34). Damage dice **"1d6"** inline (95). Creature freeze **`FrozenEffect(cold: 0.6f)`** (107). Object cooling **−200f** (153).

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. `HandleEvent` (44-59): reads `Zone` (49), `SourceCell` (50), `RNG` (51). Sets `BlocksTurnAdvance = true` (56), `e.Handled = true` (57).
2. `Cast` (61-166): guards (63-64).
3. FX: `EmitChargeOrbit(zone, ParentEntity, 1, 0.12f, Ice, true)` (67-68); `EmitRingWave(zone, sourceCell.X, sourceCell.Y, 2, 0.08f, Ice, true, delay:0.12f)` (69-71).
4. `SpellTargeting.GetCreaturesInRadius(zone, sourceCell.X, sourceCell.Y, 2, exclude: ParentEntity)` (84-85). Empty → `"...unleashes a wave of rime into empty space."` (87-88); cast continues, cooldown still charged.
5. Per creature (90-118): `DiceRoller.Roll("1d6", rng)` (95); if >0 → message (98-100) + `CombatSystem.ApplyDamage(target, damage, "Cold", ParentEntity, zone)` (102) — **not** `ApplySpellDamage` (no Cryomancy/Spellcraft bonus), **not** `RouteDamage`.
6. Survivor gate (105) → `target.ApplyEffect(new FrozenEffect(cold: 0.6f), ParentEntity, zone)` (107). The 73-83 comment is load-bearing design: pre-applying preserves 0.6 because the follow-up cooling's `TryFreeze` short-circuits on `HasEffect<FrozenEffect>()` (ThermalPart.cs:~168). At Cold 0.6 with ~0.025/turn thaw this is **~24 turns of action lockout** from one cast — the comment explicitly flags this as the tuning dial (0.3 → ~12 turns, 0.15 → ~6).
7. Per-target burst FX with radius-staggered delay (110-117).
8. Radius pass over ALL entities (126-162): bounds clamp (126-129); chebyshev skip (135-136); reverse iterate with the **`if (i >= cell.Objects.Count) continue;` re-guard** (145) — present here, absent in Conflagration; skip `ParentEntity` (147-148); `HasPart<ThermalPart>()` (150) → `ApplyHeat` **−200f** (153). Comment 120-125 documents why props are NOT pre-frozen: letting `ThermalPart.TryFreeze` handle them means `FrozenEffect.OnApply`'s brittle-shatter runs post-cool, and burning props cross `FlameTemperature` downward and extinguish.
9. `CooldownMyActivatedAbility(ActivatedAbilityID, 15)` (164).

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `AsciiFxBus.EmitChargeOrbit`/`EmitRingWave`/`EmitBurst` (67,69,114); `SpellTargeting.GetCreaturesInRadius` (84); `DiceRoller.Roll` (95); `CombatSystem.ApplyDamage` int+string overload (102); `Entity.ApplyEffect(FrozenEffect)` (107) — **direct, bypassing `ObjectStatusMatrix`**; `GameEvent "ApplyHeat"` → ThermalPart → TryFreeze/TryExtinguish (152-158).

DIAG: **none.**

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: **SpellTargeting creature-only** (`GetCreaturesInRadius`, SpellTargeting.cs:109) — props get the −200J cooling but never the 1d6 Cold damage. This matters less here than for Conflagration because the cooling IS the point for props (extinguish + shatter). SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: same as Conflagration — add a `GetElementalTargetsInRadius` filtered on `AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget` and route damage through `DestructionSystem.RouteDamage`; keep the two-pass structure (pre-freeze creatures at 0.6, let props freeze via the thermal crossing) because the asymmetry is deliberate and documented.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS:
- empty radius: `"<caster> unleashes a wave of rime into empty space."` (88)
- per creature: `"<caster> rimes <target> for <N> damage!"` (98-100)
- downstream: `"<target> is frozen!"` / `"<target> thaws."` (FrozenEffect.cs:60, 85)
- grimoire learn: `"Frost lances spiral from the margins. The rime nova rite buries itself in your will."` / `"You already know the rime nova rite."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: `Cryomancy_ColdSnap` (Cryomancy_ColdSnap.cs) — same self-centred radius-2 shape, same "buy turns" role. Deltas: damage 1d6 (avg 3.5) vs **0** (ColdSnap deals none by design, ColdSnap.cs:12-15); crowd control `FrozenEffect(0.6)` ≈ 24 turns of TOTAL action lockout vs `HobbledEffect(SNAP_DURATION = 6)` — a movement slow, explicitly "not a stun" (ColdSnap.cs:27-29); cooldown 15 vs `COOLDOWN = 30` → **−15 turns**. Rime Nova is, at half the cooldown, roughly four times the crowd control plus damage.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Cryomancy** (matches plan D3). Class `Cryomancy_RimeNova`, command `"CommandRimeNova"`. Recommended SP cost: **3**, with `Requires: "CryomancySkill"`, and **raise the cooldown to 40 and drop the Cold to 0.3** on port. Reasoning: a 24-turn full lockout on every creature within 2 cells for a 15-turn cooldown is a fight-ender, not a tempo tool, and it makes `Cryomancy_ColdSnap` and `Cryomancy_Frostbind` (CD 35, single target, movement only) both pointless. The mutation's own comment (RimeNova.cs:80-83) names 0.3 as the sanctioned dial and forbids touching `AllowAction`'s threshold — follow that instruction exactly.


## ChillDraftMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/ChillDraftMutation.cs (85 lines).

BASE CLASS: `BaseMutation` directly (line 5).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandChillDraft" (7). COOLDOWN = **5** (8). RADIUS = **1** (9). Registered `AddMyActivatedAbility(DisplayName, COMMAND, "Grimoire Spells", AbilityTargetingMode.SelfCentered, RADIUS)` (18-19). No damage, no dice, no RNG, no FX, no element attribute. Cooling dose **−100f** (71).

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. `HandleEvent` (29-41): reads `Zone` (34) and `SourceCell` (35) — no RNG. On `Cast` false returns true → `"The rite fails to resolve."`. On true sets `e.Handled = true` (39); **never sets `BlocksTurnAdvance`**.
2. `Cast` (43-83): guards (45-46).
3. Bounds clamp to `Zone.Width/Height` around the source (48-51).
4. Double loop y,x (53-79) with `Math.Max(Math.Abs(x - sourceCell.X), Math.Abs(y - sourceCell.Y)) > RADIUS` skip (57-58) — Chebyshev radius 1, i.e. the caster's own cell plus 8 neighbours, **9 cells**.
5. Reverse-iterate `cell.Objects` (64) with **no `i >= Count` re-guard** and **no `ParentEntity` exclusion** — the caster cools itself if it has a ThermalPart (creatures inherit `FlameTemperature 500` from the `Creature` blueprint, so the Player does have one). This is a real self-hit that no other cold power has.
6. Gate: `if (!entity.HasPart<ThermalPart>()) continue;` (67-68).
7. `GameEvent.New("ApplyHeat")` with `Joules = -100f` (71), `Radiant = false` (72), `Source = ParentEntity` (73), `Zone = zone` (74), fire (75), release (76).
8. `CooldownMyActivatedAbility(ActivatedAbilityID, 5)` (81). Returns true unconditionally.
9. **No message log at all** — a successful cast is completely silent.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `GameEvent "ApplyHeat"` → `ThermalPart.HandleApplyHeat` only (70-76). Nothing else — no damage, no matrix, no FX, no tile writes.

DIAG: **none.**

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: none of the four named classes (it never targets creatures for damage and never traces a line). Real defects: (a) **self-cooling** — no `exclude: ParentEntity`, so the caster receives −100J every cast, and repeated casting can cross the caster's own `FreezeTemperature` and self-apply `FrozenEffect` via `ThermalPart.TryFreeze`; (b) totally silent + no `BlocksTurnAdvance` + no FX, so the player has no feedback; (c) no diag. SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: exclude the actor (every skill-side radius helper takes `exclude:` — `SpellTargeting.GetCreaturesInRadius(zone, x, y, r, exclude: actor)`, SpellTargeting.cs:78-118, and `Cryomancy_ColdSnap.cs:55-56` does exactly that with the explicit rationale "a slow that also slowed you would be strictly bad to cast"), add a message and an `EmitSkillRejectedDiag(ctx, "no_target")` for the empty case. Since it needs objects not creatures, use the new `GetElementalTargetsInRadius` helper the other novas need, or an inline `AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget` scan.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS: **none authored.** Downstream only: `"<target> is frozen!"` (FrozenEffect.cs:60) if the cooling crosses a freeze point; `"<target> is no longer burning."` (BurningEffect.cs:88) when it extinguishes something. Grimoire learn: `"A biting draft slips from the pages. You learn chill draft."` / `"You already know chill draft."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: `Cryomancy_ColdSnap` for the self-centred shape. Deltas: radius 1 (9 cells) vs `SNAP_RADIUS = 2` (25 cells) → **−16 cells**; cooldown 5 vs 30 → **−25 turns**; effect −100J thermal on every ThermalPart holder (props included) vs `HobbledEffect(6)` on creatures only; damage 0 vs 0 (match). ChillDraft is the props-facing counterpart ColdSnap deliberately isn't. Also compare `Quench` (−150J single target) and `RimeNova` (−200J radius 2): ChillDraft's −100J is the family's weakest cooling, consistent with its 5-turn cooldown.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Cryomancy** (matches plan D3). Class `Cryomancy_ChillDraft`, command `"CommandChillDraft"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**. Reasoning: it is the tree's cantrip — short cooldown, no damage, no creature effect, pure environmental cooling (put out the fire you're standing next to, chill a burning barrel, cool a room). At Cost 1 it complements ColdSnap (creature tempo) rather than competing. Its whole value depends on the port adding player feedback; ship it with a success line such as `"<caster> exhales a chill draft."` mirroring DryingBreeze's existing `"<caster> exhales a drying breeze."` (DryingBreezeMutation.cs:73) so the two sibling cantrips read as a matched pair.


## ArcBoltMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/ArcBoltMutation.cs (37 lines).

BASE CLASS: `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase` (line 12). Full base machinery as in the KindleMutation dossier (DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:30-167).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandArcBolt" (14). FxTheme = `AsciiFxTheme.Lightning` (17). CooldownTurns = **7** (18). AbilityRange = **5** (19). DamageDice = **"1d8"** (20) — the highest single-target dice of the six projectile mutations. AbilityClass = "Grimoire Spells" (21). ImpactVerb = "jolts" (22). ElementAttribute = **"Electric"** (23) → `DamageAttributeFlags.Electric`, satisfies `GalvanismSkill.OnGetSpellDamageModifier` (GalvanismSkill.cs:35).

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST:
1-2. As Kindle: `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact` at range 5, lightning projectile FX with trail (base:87), roll 1d8, `ApplySpellDamage` → `"Spell"`+`"Electric"` → `RouteDamage`, damage message, `MutationDamage` diag.
3. `ApplyOnHitEffect` (29-35) is one line: **`ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply(new ElectrifiedEffect(charge: 1.0f), target, ParentEntity, zone)`** (34). This is the only one of the 18 that uses the matrix for lightning, and it is the CORRECT door — `ObjectStatusMatrix.Table[typeof(ElectrifiedEffect)]` requires a `Conductor`/`Metal`/`Water` material tag on non-creatures (ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:86, 61-62), creatures pass through unchanged (ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:110), and refusals emit an `effect/ObjectEffectRefused` diag (ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:131-140).
4. Downstream, `ElectrifiedEffect.OnApply` does the amplification: doubled charge and +1 duration on targets with `WetEffect.Moisture > 0.2`, plus a one-turn creature stun (ArcBolt docstring 5-11; ElectrifiedEffect.cs:24-32, Duration default 2). The conductor chain resolves on the struck target's next `EndTurn` via `ElectrifiedEffect.OnTurnEnd` → `MaterialPart.HandleTryChainElectricity` (MaterialPart.cs:182+), which gates on `IsConductiveMaterial` (Conductivity ≥ **50** on the 0-100 scale, or Metal/Conductor tag — MaterialPart.cs:173-180).
5. Base applies the 7-turn cooldown.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact` (base:75); `AsciiFxBus.EmitProjectile` (base:87); `DiceRoller.Roll` (base:92); `MutationDamageHelpers.ApplySpellDamage` (base:104) → `GetSpellDamageModifier` → `RouteDamage`; **`ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply`** (ArcBoltMutation.cs:34); then `ElectrifiedEffect` → `MaterialPart.HandleTryChainElectricity` on the target's EndTurn.

DIAG: `damage/MutationDamage` from the base (122-137), PLUS `effect/ObjectEffectRefused` from `ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply` when the target's material doesn't conduct (ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:131-140). It is the best-instrumented of the 18.

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: **LineTargeting Wall/Terrain skip** (LineTargeting.cs:171). This is the most damaging instance of the four in the whole set: **`WaterPuddle` and `BrinePool` inherit `Terrain` and therefore carry the `Terrain` tag**, so an Arc Bolt aimed at the puddle the enemy is standing in flies straight over it. The entire water-conducts-shock grammar the `ObjectStatusMatrix` `Conductive` list exists to encode ("Water is in the list because ... shock the puddle the player is standing in", ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:52-59) is unreachable from Arc Bolt. Plus the corpse/loot priority at LineTargeting.cs:174. SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: `SkillLine.Collect(...)` + `line[0]` — `AbilityTargeting.IsElementalTarget` accepts a `MaterialPart`-only entity like `BrinePool` explicitly (AbilityTargeting.cs:14-23, 50-51), so the port fixes this by construction. `Galvanism_Overload.cs:76-91` shows the exact creature-first-then-scenery scan to imitate.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS (base, DisplayName="Arc Bolt", verb="jolts"):
- hit: `"<caster> jolts <target> for <N> damage!"` (base:111-113)
- blocked: `"<caster>'s Arc Bolt splashes against an obstacle!"` (base:156)
- nothing hit: `"<caster>'s Arc Bolt dissipates harmlessly."` (base:160)
- downstream: `"<target> is electrified!"` (ElectrifiedEffect.cs:32), `"<target> is no longer electrified."` (ElectrifiedEffect.cs:116)
- grimoire learn: `"Static crackles across the vellum. The arc bolt rite sparks into your memory."` / `"You already know the arc bolt rite."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: `Galvanism_RailSpike` (Galvanism_RailSpike.cs). Deltas: damage 1d8 (avg 4.5) vs flat `SPIKE_DAMAGE = 5` → **−0.5 avg**; range 5 vs `SPIKE_RANGE = 6` → **−1**; cooldown 7 vs `COOLDOWN = 45` → **−38 turns**; charge `ElectrifiedEffect(1.0)` **through the matrix** vs RailSpike's direct `ground.ApplyEffect(new ElectrifiedEffect(charge: SPIKE_CHARGE = 1.5f), ...)` (RailSpike.cs:112-113) — RailSpike charges harder but bypasses the material gate; shape: single first body vs pierce-all-then-charge-the-last.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Galvanism** (matches plan D3). Class `Galvanism_ArcBolt`, command `"CommandArcBolt"`. Recommended SP cost: **2**. Reasoning: Galvanism currently has no cheap repeatable — its actives are Overload (CD 50), Ground Surge (CD 30), Backlash Coil, Rail Spike (CD 45). Arc Bolt at CD 7 fills the EmberSpit-shaped hole in the tree, which is a genuine gap. But CD 7 for 1d8 + a matrix-gated Electrified is stronger than Pyromancy's CD-8 filler, so **raise the cooldown to 10-12 on port** and price at 2, or leave CD 7 and price at 3. Keep the `ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply` call verbatim — it is the correct pattern and the other Galvanism actives should be migrated TO it, not away from it.

BROKEN PATHS — this mutation carries **two** of the four named classes:
(1) **Stale conductivity threshold.** Line 155 tests `mat.Conductivity > 0.5f`. The canonical authority is `MaterialPart.IsConductiveMaterial` (MaterialPart.cs:172-180), which uses `mat.Conductivity >= ConductiveThreshold` where `ConductiveThreshold = 50f` (MaterialPart.cs:181), explicitly documented as a fix for exactly this bug: "The old test here was `Conductivity > 0.5f`, which on a 0-100 scale means 'anything above half a percent' — a rubber mat at 5 counted as a conductor. Two systems disagreeing about what conducts is worse than either answer" (MaterialPart.cs:164-170). Thunderclap is the one caller that still holds the pre-fix number. Consequence: it electrifies essentially every material-bearing prop in radius (Combustibility/Conductivity are authored 0-100 in most content), while `Galvanism_Overload`'s chain, the tile propagation, and `ObjectStatusMatrix` all refuse them. Additionally it bypasses `ObjectStatusMatrix`, so it charges `MetalGrate` (Conductivity 100, no tags — audit F13) where the matrix refuses it, deepening the five-authority split.
(2) **SpellTargeting creature-only** (`GetCreaturesInRadius`, SpellTargeting.cs:109) — props take the charge but never the 2d6 Electric damage.
SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: replace lines 151-161 with `ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply(new ElectrifiedEffect(0.8f), entity, actor, zone)` — the matrix already encodes the conductor rule (ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:61-62, 86) AND emits a refusal diag, which is exactly the collapse-to-one-authority move recommended by audit §6 P4. For damage, use a `GetElementalTargetsInRadius` + `DestructionSystem.RouteDamage`. Model the conductivity query on `Galvanism_Overload.cs:106-110`, which asks the matrix rather than restating the tag list, with the rationale spelled out at 100-105.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS:
- empty radius: `"<caster> unleashes a clap of thunder into empty space."` (82)
- per creature: `"<caster> jolts <target> for <N> damage!"` (96-98) — note the verb collides with Arc Bolt's `"jolts"` (ArcBoltMutation.cs:22); differentiate on port.
- downstream: `"<target> is electrified!"` (ElectrifiedEffect.cs:32)
- grimoire learn: `"Thunder rolls from the open pages. The thunderclap rite booms itself into your will."` / `"You already know the thunderclap rite."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: `Galvanism_Overload` for the damage tier, `Galvanism_GroundSurge` for the shape. Deltas vs Overload: shape self-radius-2 (25 cells, no aim) vs line-8 chain that breaks on the first non-conductor; damage 2d6 (avg 7, **14 on wet**) vs flat `OVERLOAD_DAMAGE = 8`; cooldown 18 vs `COOLDOWN = 50` → **−32 turns**; Overload requires the target to already be Wet or Electrified to hit at all (Overload.cs:106-111), Thunderclap requires nothing. Deltas vs GroundSurge: cooldown 18 vs 30 → **−12**; GroundSurge electrifies on a 40% roll, Thunderclap at 100%.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Galvanism** (matches plan D3). Class `Galvanism_Thunderclap`, command `"CommandThunderclap"`. Recommended SP cost: **3**, with `Requires: "GalvanismSkill"`, and **raise the cooldown to 45** on port. Reasoning: no-aim, 25-cell, guaranteed-Electrified, doubled-on-wet 2d6 at CD 18 dominates every other Galvanism active including Overload at CD 50. The wet-doubling also stacks with `HydromancySkill.MOISTURE_BONUS` and with `ElectrifiedEffect`'s own wet amplification, so a Hydromancer/Galvanist pairing gets a triple multiplier from one cast — the strongest combo in the game. The conductivity-threshold fix (155 → matrix) is non-negotiable and should ship in the same commit.


## AcidSprayMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/AcidSprayMutation.cs (36 lines).

BASE CLASS: `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase` (line 10). Full base machinery as in the KindleMutation dossier (DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:30-167).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandAcidSpray" (12). FxTheme = `AsciiFxTheme.Poison` (15). CooldownTurns = **10** (16) — the longest of the six projectile mutations bar Calm. AbilityRange = **4** (17) — the shortest. DamageDice = **"1d4"** (18). AbilityClass = "Grimoire Spells" (19). ImpactVerb = "douses" (20). ElementAttribute = **"Acid"** (21) → `DamageAttributeFlags.Acid`, satisfies `CorrosionSkill.OnGetSpellDamageModifier` (CorrosionSkill.cs:32).

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST:
1-2. As Kindle: trace at range 4, poison-theme projectile FX, roll 1d4, `ApplySpellDamage` → `"Spell"`+`"Acid"` → `RouteDamage`, damage message, `MutationDamage` diag.
3. `ApplyOnHitEffect` (27-34) is one line: **`ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply(new AcidicEffect(corrosion: 0.8f), target, ParentEntity, zone)`** (33). The matrix row for `AcidicEffect` is unconditional (`e => true`, ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:93 — "anything can be etched"), so this always lands on any target, but it still routes through the single door and gets the refusal-diag plumbing for free.
4. Downstream, `AcidicEffect` (Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Effects/Concrete/AcidicEffect.cs): `Corrosion = 0.8f` (19/21), `Duration = DURATION_INDEFINITE` (24); `OnTurnStart` degrades organic `MaterialPart.Combustibility` and deals flat damage (docstring 5-8; effect body ~55-75); `OnTurnEnd` decays the corrosion (75). The `acid_plus_organic` reaction layers fuel-consumption acceleration on burning acidic organics (docstring 30-33).
5. Base applies the 10-turn cooldown.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact` (base:75); `AsciiFxBus.EmitProjectile` (base:87); `DiceRoller.Roll` (base:92); `MutationDamageHelpers.ApplySpellDamage` (base:104) → `GetSpellDamageModifier` → `RouteDamage`; **`ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply`** (AcidSprayMutation.cs:33); downstream `MaterialReactionResolver` via `acid_plus_organic`.

DIAG: `damage/MutationDamage` from the base (122-137); `effect/ObjectEffectRefused` from the matrix is theoretically reachable but never fires because the AcidicEffect gate is `e => true`.

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: **LineTargeting Wall/Terrain skip** (LineTargeting.cs:171) — the Terrain-tagged blueprints (Bush, Campfire, WaterPuddle, OilSlick, DryBrush, TarSeep) and Wall-tagged breakables are unhittable, plus the corpse/loot priority at LineTargeting.cs:174. Less thematically damaging than for Kindle/ArcBolt (acid on a puddle means little) but the same class of bug. SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: `SkillLine.Collect(...)` + `line[0]`, per `Cryomancy_RimeGrip.cs:57-69` / `Pyromancy_EmberSpit.cs:67-80`.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS (base, DisplayName="Acid Spray", verb="douses"):
- hit: `"<caster> douses <target> for <N> damage!"` (base:111-113)
- blocked: `"<caster>'s Acid Spray splashes against an obstacle!"` (base:156)
- nothing hit: `"<caster>'s Acid Spray dissipates harmlessly."` (base:160)
- downstream: `"<target> is coated in acid."` (AcidicEffect.cs:29), `"<target> takes <N> acid damage."` (AcidicEffect.cs:63), `"<target> is no longer corroding."` (AcidicEffect.cs:34)
- grimoire learn: `"Corrosive ink hisses as you read. The acid spray rite eats its way into your mind."` / `"You already know the acid spray rite."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: **none — the Corrosion tree has zero actives.** Its entire content is `CorrosionSkill` (root, +25% Acid on Acidic targets) and two passives, `Corrosion_Etch` (+50% melee on Acidic, Corrosion_Etch.cs:26) and `Corrosion_AcidRetort` (3 Acid on a miss) — see Corrosion.json, which is the smallest tree in the game at 3 rows. So Acid Spray would be **the tree's first and only way to actually apply `AcidicEffect`**, which today has no player-reachable source in the skill system at all: `CorrosionSkill`'s own description tells the player to "Open with AcidSpray or PoisonSpit" (Corrosion.json:7) — i.e. it already documents a dependency on a mutation. Shape-wise the nearest analogue is `Pyromancy_EmberSpit` (line, single target, cheap): deltas — damage 1d4 (avg 2.5) vs flat 3 → **−0.5 avg**; range 4 vs 4 → **match**; cooldown 10 vs 8 → **+2**; effect matrix-gated `AcidicEffect(0.8)` vs `PyroIgnition.TryIgnite(0.6)`.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Corrosion** (matches plan D3). Class `Corrosion_AcidSpray`, command `"CommandAcidSpray"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**. Reasoning: this is the highest-value single port in the whole set — it takes a tree whose three existing rows are ALL conditional on a status nothing in the tree can apply, and makes the tree self-sufficient. Price it at the floor so a player buying into Corrosion gets the enabler first. The numbers need no change (1d4 / range 4 / CD 10 sits comfortably below EmberSpit's power budget). Consider making it the tree root's implicit companion via `Requires: "CorrosionSkill"` so the +25%-on-Acidic root and the applier are bought as a pair.


## QuenchMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/QuenchMutation.cs (42 lines).

BASE CLASS: `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase` (line 9). Full base machinery as in the KindleMutation dossier (DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:30-167).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandQuench" (11). FxTheme = `AsciiFxTheme.Water` (14). CooldownTurns = **6** (15). AbilityRange = **5** (16). DamageDice = **"1d3"** (17) — the lowest damage of any damaging power in the set. AbilityClass = "Grimoire Spells" (18). ImpactVerb = "drenches" (19). **ElementAttribute is NOT overridden** — it inherits the base default `""` (DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:28), so the damage is tagged `"Spell"` only and is **untyped**: no resistance applies and no elemental tree's `OnGetSpellDamageModifier` fires (only the universal `SpellcraftSkill` +1 and `Spellcraft_Empower` +2 do). This is deliberate and matches `Hydromancy_JetBlast`'s note that water damage maps to `DamageAttributeFlags.None` (Hydromancy_JetBlast.cs:98-103).

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST:
1-2. As Kindle: trace at range 5, water-theme projectile FX, roll 1d3, `ApplySpellDamage(target, damage, "", ParentEntity, zone)` → `Damage` with `"Spell"` only → `RouteDamage`, damage message, `MutationDamage` diag with `elementAttribute = ""`.
3. `ApplyOnHitEffect` (25-40), two steps:
   a. **`ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply(new WetEffect(moisture: 0.8f), target, ParentEntity, zone)`** (28). Matrix row for `WetEffect` is `e => true` (ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:92 — "anything can get wet"), so it always lands.
   b. `GameEvent.New("ApplyHeat")` with `Joules = -150f` (34), `Radiant = false` (35), `Source = ParentEntity` (36), `Zone = zone` (37), fire (38), release (39).
4. The docstring (5-8, 27-32) states the intent: Moisture 0.8 is above `WetEffect`'s two thresholds — 0.2 for `ElectrifiedEffect`'s charge doubling and **0.35 for ignition suppression** (`ThermalPart.TryIgnite`, ThermalPart.cs:~117; mirrored at `PyroIgnition.MoistureSuppressionThreshold`, PyroIgnition.cs:39) — and the −150J cooling can drop temperature below `FlameTemperature` so `ThermalPart` extinguishes `BurningEffect` on its next `EndTurn`.
5. Base applies the 6-turn cooldown.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact` (base:75); `AsciiFxBus.EmitProjectile` (base:87); `DiceRoller.Roll` (base:92); `MutationDamageHelpers.ApplySpellDamage` (base:104) → `GetSpellDamageModifier` → `RouteDamage`; **`ObjectStatusMatrix.TryApply`** (QuenchMutation.cs:28); `GameEvent "ApplyHeat"` → `ThermalPart.HandleApplyHeat` (34-39). NOT called: `HydromancySkill.ApplyMoistureBonus` — this is the one water power in the game that ignores the Hydromancy tree bonus.

DIAG: `damage/MutationDamage` from the base (122-137).

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: **LineTargeting Wall/Terrain skip** (LineTargeting.cs:171) — same as Kindle. Acutely painful here: a burning `Bush`, `Campfire`, `DryBrush` or `TarSeep` all carry the `Terrain` tag and are therefore unhittable by the game's dedicated fire-extinguishing spell. Plus the corpse/loot priority at 174. SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: `SkillLine.Collect(...)` + `line[0]`. Additionally the port MUST route the moisture through `HydromancySkill.ApplyMoistureBonus(actor, 0.8f)` (HydromancySkill.cs:49-60) — its docstring states "Any water power calls this rather than using its base value directly, so the tree bonus can never be forgotten by a future power," and `Hydromancy_JetBlast.cs:90` and `Hydromancy_DrenchLob.cs:114` both do. Note 0.8 + 0.25 clamps to 1.0 (HydromancySkill.cs:56-59).

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS (base, DisplayName="Quench", verb="drenches"):
- hit: `"<caster> drenches <target> for <N> damage!"` (base:111-113)
- blocked: `"<caster>'s Quench splashes against an obstacle!"` (base:156)
- nothing hit: `"<caster>'s Quench dissipates harmlessly."` (base:160)
- downstream: `"<target> is drenched."` (WetEffect.cs:31), `"<target> has dried off."` (WetEffect.cs:36), `"<target> is no longer burning."` (BurningEffect.cs:88)
- grimoire learn: `"Water-words ripple across the vellum. The quench rite flows into your memory."` / `"You already know the quench rite."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: `Hydromancy_JetBlast` (Hydromancy_JetBlast.cs). Deltas: shape line range-5 single target vs cone length-2 multi-target; damage 1d3 (avg 2) vs flat `BLAST_DAMAGE = 2` → **match**, both untyped; moisture **0.8 flat** vs `BLAST_MOISTURE = 0.8f` put through `ApplyMoistureBonus` → **0.8 vs 1.0 for a Hydromancer**; cooldown 6 vs `COOLDOWN = 20` → **−14 turns**; Quench adds −150J cooling (no JetBlast analogue); JetBlast adds a 1-cell push (`SkillCombatHelpers.TryPush`, JetBlast.cs:119) and writes `"water"` coating to the ground for 6 turns (`ZoneTileStateSystem.WriteCoating`, JetBlast.cs:130-131, 157-161) plus `ResolveAfterAbility` (143) — Quench writes nothing to the world. Compare also `Hydromancy_DrenchLob`: range 6 / radius 2 / `LOB_MOISTURE = 0.6f` / CD 30, no damage.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Hydromancy** (matches plan D3). Class `Hydromancy_Quench`, command `"CommandQuench"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**. Reasoning: Hydromancy is the smallest damage tree (3 powers: JetBlast, DrenchLob, Undertow — Hydromancy.json) and has no long-range single-target option; Quench fills that slot as the cheap repeatable, and its −150J cooling is a genuinely distinct capability (nothing else in the tree extinguishes). At CD 6 it is cheaper than JetBlast's 20, but its total output is a fraction of JetBlast's (one target, no push, no ground coating), so CD 6 is defensible — I recommend keeping it. The two required changes on port are `SkillLine` targeting and `ApplyMoistureBonus`.


## ConjureWaterMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/ConjureWaterMutation.cs (116 lines).

BASE CLASS: `BaseMutation` directly (line 5).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandConjureWater" (7). COOLDOWN = **4** (8). RANGE = **2** (9). Registered `AddMyActivatedAbility(DisplayName, COMMAND, "Grimoire Spells", AbilityTargetingMode.DirectionLine, RANGE)` (18-23). No damage, no dice, no RNG, no FX. Wet applied **0.5f** (106).

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. `HandleEvent` (33-48): reads `Zone` (38), `SourceCell` (39), then — **the bug** — `int dx = e.GetParameter<int>("DirectionX");` (40), `int dy = e.GetParameter<int>("DirectionY");` (41), `int range = e.GetParameter<int>("Range");` (42). Calls `Cast(zone, sourceCell, dx, dy, range > 0 ? range : RANGE)` (43). Sets `e.Handled = true` (46) on success; **never sets `BlocksTurnAdvance`**.
2. `Cast` (50-114): guards zone/sourceCell/ParentEntity (52-53) and `(dx == 0 && dy == 0) return false` (54-55).
3. Line walk (60-85), steps 1..range: bounds check (64-65); `zone.GetCell` (66-68); **stop at the first cell containing an entity with `BurningEffect`** (71-78, via `goto placeWater`); else if `!candidate.IsPassable()` break WITHOUT landing on it (81-82); else `targetCell = candidate` (84).
4. `placeWater:` label (87). If `targetCell == null` return false (88-89).
5. `var factory = MaterialReactionResolver.Factory;` (91); `Entity puddle = factory?.CreateEntity("WaterPuddle");` (92); on null, `UnityEngine.Debug.LogError("[ConjureWater] Factory returned null for WaterPuddle blueprint.")` (95) and return false.
6. `zone.AddEntity(puddle, targetCell.X, targetCell.Y)` (98).
7. Per other object in the cell, reverse-iterated (100-110): skip the puddle (102-103); `entity.ApplyEffect(new WetEffect(0.5f), ParentEntity, zone)` (106) — **direct, bypassing `ObjectStatusMatrix` and bypassing `HydromancySkill.ApplyMoistureBonus`**; if the entity has `BurningEffect`, `MaterialReactionResolver.EvaluateReactions(entity, zone, entity.GetEffect<BurningEffect>())` (108-109).
8. `CooldownMyActivatedAbility(ActivatedAbilityID, 4)` (112).
9. **No message log at all.**

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `MaterialReactionResolver.Factory` + `EntityFactory.CreateEntity("WaterPuddle")` (91-92); `Zone.AddEntity` (98); `Entity.ApplyEffect(WetEffect)` (106); `MaterialReactionResolver.EvaluateReactions` (109). NOT called: any damage path, `ObjectStatusMatrix`, `ZoneTileStateSystem`, `AsciiFxBus`.

DIAG: **none.**

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: **`GetParameter<int>` wrong dictionary — this is audit F17 and it makes the spell completely dead from the keybind.** `GameEvent.SetParameter(string, int)` writes to `IntParameters` (GameEvent.cs, the `int` overload), while `GetParameter<T>` reads ONLY `Parameters`, the object dictionary (GameEvent.cs `public T GetParameter<T>(string name)` → `if (Parameters.TryGetValue(name, out object value) && value is T typed) return typed; return default;`). `InputHandler.ResolveAbilityCommand` writes `cmd.SetParameter("DirectionX", dx)` / `("DirectionY", dy)` / `("Range", ability.Range)` — all through the int overload (InputHandler.cs:3140-3142). So ConjureWater always reads dx=0, dy=0 → `Cast` returns false at line 54-55 → `HandleEvent` returns true (unhandled) → InputHandler prints `"The rite fails to resolve."` (InputHandler.cs:3159). **Every keybound cast of Conjure Water has always failed.** The `range` read fails identically but is masked by the `range > 0 ? range : RANGE` fallback (43). AI casts are broken the same way (`AIHelpers.cs:471` also uses the int overload). The sibling `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:56` and `EmberVeinMutation.cs:53` use the correct `GetIntParameter`. SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: the bug cannot occur — `SkillsPart.HandleEvent` lifts direction with `e.GetIntParameter("DirectionX")` / `("DirectionY")` (SkillsPart.cs:404-405), which falls back through all three dictionaries (`GameEvent.GetIntParameter` checks IntParameters, then Parameters, then StringParameters), and hands them to the skill as `ctx.DirectionX`/`ctx.DirectionY` (SkillsPart.cs:276). Read `ctx.DirectionX/Y` and the spell works for the first time.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS: **none authored.** Downstream: `"<target> is drenched."` (WetEffect.cs:31), `"<target> is no longer burning."` (BurningEffect.cs:88). Grimoire learn: `"Condensation beads across the page. You learn conjure water."` / `"You already know conjure water."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: `Cryomancy_GlacialWall` (Cryomancy_GlacialWall.cs) — the only other power that spawns blueprint entities into the zone. Deltas: spawns 1 `WaterPuddle` vs up to `WALL_LENGTH = 3` `IceWall`s; range 2 vs 3; cooldown 4 vs `COOLDOWN = 40` → **−36 turns**; factory access `MaterialReactionResolver.Factory` (ConjureWater:91) vs GlacialWall's own injected `public static EntityFactory Factory` with a `no_factory` reject diag (GlacialWall.cs:38-40, 64-71) — **use GlacialWall's pattern on port**; GlacialWall skips occupied and solid cells (GlacialWall.cs:90-100), ConjureWater deliberately lands ON the burning cell. Also compare `Hydromancy_JetBlast`, which writes water to the ground via `ZoneTileStateSystem.WriteCoating(zone, x, y, "water", 6, actor, Name)` (JetBlast.cs:130-131) rather than spawning an entity — the plan's D1 correctly identifies ConjureWater as "water creation, not projectile," and the entity-spawn is what makes it distinct from the coating write.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Hydromancy** (matches plan D3). Class `Hydromancy_ConjureWater`, command `"CommandConjureWater"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**. Reasoning: no damage, 4-turn cooldown, range 2 — it is a utility cantrip whose value is entirely environmental (make a conductive puddle for a lightning follow-up; drop water on a fire). Because the port is the first time it will ever function, treat its balance as untested: ship at Cost 1, CD 4, and flag for playtest. Two required changes: read `ctx.DirectionX/Y`, and route the 0.5 moisture through `HydromancySkill.ApplyMoistureBonus`. Strongly recommended third change: add a success message and a `no_direction` / `no_room` reject diag, since today the spell is silent in both success and failure.


## ConjureRainMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/ConjureRainMutation.cs (140 lines). Taught by `WateringGrimoire` (Objects.json). Living doc: `Docs/CROPS-WATERING-GRIMOIRE.md §2.4` (cited at line 14).

BASE CLASS: `BaseMutation` directly (line 21).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandConjureRain" (23). COOLDOWN = **5** (24). RADIUS = **3** (25) — the largest radius of the 18. `MOISTURE_TICKS = 40` (29), documented as top-up semantics via `CropPart.Water`, never additive (27-28). Registered `AddMyActivatedAbility(DisplayName, COMMAND, "Grimoire Spells", AbilityTargetingMode.SelfCentered, RADIUS)` (38-39). No damage, no dice, no RNG.

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. `HandleEvent` (49-61): reads `Zone` (54) and `SourceCell` (55). Sets `e.Handled = true` (59); **never sets `BlocksTurnAdvance`** — deliberate, the rain FX is non-blocking (line 137 passes `blocksTurnAdvance: false`).
2. `Cast` (63-109): guards (65-66); bounds clamp to `Zone.Width/Height` (68-71).
3. Double loop with Chebyshev skip (74-79) over 49 cells max.
4. Per cell, forward-iterate objects (85): `var crop = cell.Objects[i].GetPart<CropPart>(); if (crop == null) continue;` (87-89).
5. `crop.Water(MOISTURE_TICKS)` (91) → `CropPart.Water` (CropPart.cs:80-96): `if (ticks > MoistureTicks) MoistureTicks = ticks;` (83-84) — **top-up, not additive**; sets `RenderPart.BackgroundColor = WET_SOIL_BG` and `MarkOwnCellDirty("CropWatered")` (86-91); emits `crop/CropWatered` diag (93-95).
6. `EmitRainFx(zone, x, y)` (92); `cropsWatered++`; `break` — **one crop per cell by the planting gate** (94).
7. `MessageLog.Add(cropsWatered > 0 ? "Rain patters down over the crops." : "The conjured rain finds no crops to nourish.")` (99-101).
8. `Diag.Record("crop", "RainConjured", actor: ParentEntity, payload: new { cropsWatered, radius = RADIUS })` gated on `Diag.IsChannelEnabled("crop")` (103-105).
9. `CooldownMyActivatedAbility(ActivatedAbilityID, 5)` (107). **Always returns true** — the cast succeeds and charges its cooldown even with zero crops nearby; the docstring names this the "DryingBreeze convention" (13-17).

THE FX (`EmitRainFx`, 126-138): three staggered `AsciiFxBus.EmitParticle` calls — `(x, y-2, '|', "&B", lifetime:0.25f, dy:1, moveInterval:0.1f, delay:0f)` (129-130); `(x, y-1, '\'', "&b", lifetime:0.15f, dy:1, moveInterval:0.1f, delay:0.12f)` (132-133); `(x, y-2, '.', "&B", lifetime:0.25f, dy:1, moveInterval:0.1f, delay:0.24f)` (134-135); then `AsciiFxBus.EmitBurst(zone, x, y, AsciiFxTheme.Water, blocksTurnAdvance: false, delay: 0.3f)` (136-137). The lifetime-as-travel-budget rationale is documented at 116-124: the renderer moves a particle `floor(lifetime / moveInterval)` cells (`AsciiFxRenderer.UpdateParticles`), so each drop's lifetime is sized to die ON the crop row; the original 0.45f made drops rain 2-3 tiles through the soil (SM7d farming-audit note).

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `CropPart.Water` (91); `AsciiFxBus.EmitParticle` ×3 + `EmitBurst` (129-137); `Diagnostics.Diag.Record` (103-105).

DIAG: **`crop/RainConjured`** with `{cropsWatered, radius}` (103-105) — one of only two of the 18 with a bespoke diag record. Plus `crop/CropWatered` per crop from `CropPart.Water` (CropPart.cs:93-95).

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: **none of the four.** This is the second-cleanest of the 18 after FlamingHands: it never traces a line, never uses `SpellTargeting`, reads no direction params, and touches no conductivity. Minor gaps: no `exclude` for the caster (harmless — the player has no CropPart) and no `BlocksTurnAdvance` (deliberate). SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: port the radius scan verbatim; the only mechanical change is that `SkillsPart` applies the cooldown unconditionally after `OnCommand` (SkillsPart.cs:284), which matches this mutation's existing always-succeed convention exactly — a rare zero-delta port.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS:
- success: `"Rain patters down over the crops."` (100)
- no crops: `"The conjured rain finds no crops to nourish."` (101)
- grimoire learn: `"Cool mist rises from the pages. You learn Conjure Rain."` / already-known `"The rite of rain is already yours."` (WateringGrimoire — note this is the only grimoire whose already-known line is not of the form "You already know X")

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: **none.** No skill in any tree touches farming, and no skill emits particle FX at all (verified: zero `AsciiFx` references anywhere under Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Skills/). The nearest by shape is `Cryomancy_ColdSnap` (self-centred radius scan, no damage, no aim): deltas — radius 3 (49 cells) vs `SNAP_RADIUS = 2` (25 cells) → **+24 cells**; cooldown 5 vs 30 → **−25 turns**; effect: crop moisture top-up vs `HobbledEffect(6)` on creatures.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Hydromancy** (matches plan D3). Class `Hydromancy_ConjureRain`, command `"CommandConjureRain"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**, and I recommend making it **teach-only** (the `Persuasion` tree already establishes the sentinel-cost convention at `Cost: 100` for narrative-gated nodes — Persuasion.json:6,14 — against a +1 SP/level grant at LevelingSystem.cs:71-73, i.e. effectively unbuyable). Reasoning: it is a pure farming utility with zero combat application, discovered via a specific grimoire tied to the crops feature. Putting it in the SP economy at Cost 1 would have every Hydromancer buy it by accident; leaving it grimoire-only preserves the discovery framing the plan's D2 calls "the mutation system's one genuinely good idea." This is the clearest teach-only candidate in the set and a good place to prototype the mechanism for the user's open question §5.2. Also note: the port must add a row to whatever replaces `GrimoireTooltipData` — Conjure Rain's row (GrimoireTooltipData.cs:125-131) exists precisely because it shipped without one and became invisible in the grimoire picker (SM7d, farming audit F3, documented at GrimoireTooltipData.cs:48-55).


## DryingBreezeMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/DryingBreezeMutation.cs (78 lines).

BASE CLASS: `BaseMutation` directly (line 5).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandDryingBreeze" (7). COOLDOWN = **3** (8) — the second-shortest of the 18. RADIUS = **1** (9). Registered `AddMyActivatedAbility(DisplayName, COMMAND, "Grimoire Spells", AbilityTargetingMode.SelfCentered, RADIUS)` (18-19). No damage, no dice, no RNG, no FX, no element attribute.

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. `HandleEvent` (29-41): reads `Zone` (34), `SourceCell` (35). On `Cast` false returns true → `"The rite fails to resolve."`. On true sets `e.Handled = true` (39); **never sets `BlocksTurnAdvance`**.
2. `Cast` (43-76): guards (45-46); bounds clamp (48-51).
3. Double loop with `Math.Max(Math.Abs(x - sourceCell.X), Math.Abs(y - sourceCell.Y)) > RADIUS` skip (57-58) — Chebyshev radius 1 = the caster's cell + 8 neighbours, **9 cells**.
4. Reverse-iterate `cell.Objects` (64) with **no `i >= Count` re-guard** and **no `ParentEntity` exclusion**.
5. `if (entity.HasEffect<WetEffect>()) entity.RemoveEffect<WetEffect>();` (67-68) — a hard removal, not a moisture decrement. There is no partial-dry path and no threshold.
6. `MessageLog.Add(ParentEntity.GetDisplayName() + " exhales a drying breeze.")` (73).
7. `CooldownMyActivatedAbility(ActivatedAbilityID, 3)` (74). Returns true unconditionally — the cast always succeeds, even over 9 bone-dry cells. The `ConjureRainMutation` docstring names this the "DryingBreeze convention" (ConjureRainMutation.cs:13-17).

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `Entity.HasEffect<WetEffect>` / `Entity.RemoveEffect<WetEffect>` (67-68) and `MessageLog.Add` (73). **That is the entire external surface** — the simplest of the 18.

DIAG: **none.**

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: **none of the four.** Real defects: (a) **self-drying** — no exclusion, so the caster strips its own `WetEffect`; this is arguably intended (dry yourself off to stop conducting) but it is undocumented and interacts badly with the Hydromancy tree, whose entire identity is keeping moisture above 0.2/0.35 (HydromancySkill.cs:21-29); (b) it bypasses `WetEffect`'s own decay model entirely — `WetEffect.OnTurnEnd` evaporates gradually and sets `Duration = 0` when spent (WetEffect.cs:~55-62), whereas this removes the whole effect at once; (c) no diag, so a player asking "why is my target still wet?" has no query. SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: exclude the actor (or make self-drying an explicit, documented feature), and emit `EmitSkillRejectedDiag(ctx, "no_wet_targets")` when nothing was dried. Consider also honouring `ObjectStatusMatrix` symmetry — there is no removal-side matrix, so a direct `RemoveEffect` is acceptable here.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS:
- always: `"<caster> exhales a drying breeze."` (73)
- downstream: `"<target> has dried off."` is NOT emitted — `RemoveEffect<WetEffect>` bypasses the effect's `OnRemove` message path in this flow; the only line the player sees is the caster's own.
- grimoire learn: `"The page exhales a dry wind. You learn drying breeze."` / `"You already know drying breeze."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: **none** — no skill removes a status effect from anything. The closest by intent is `WardGleamMutation` (the other cleanser in the set, but it strips Acidic/Charred from equipped items rather than Wet from a radius). By shape, `Cryomancy_ColdSnap` (self-centred, no damage, no aim): deltas — radius 1 (9 cells) vs `SNAP_RADIUS = 2` (25 cells); cooldown 3 vs 30 → **−27 turns**; adds an effect vs removes one; ColdSnap excludes the caster and rejects with a diag + message when the radius is empty (ColdSnap.cs:55-63), which is the exact shape DryingBreeze lacks.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Hydromancy** (matches plan D3), though the fit is inverted — this is the tree's *anti*-power, and there is a real design argument for **Pyromancy** instead (drying a target is fire's setup, exactly as soaking is lightning's). Recommend Hydromancy for cohesion with the plan and because "a hydromancer controls where the water is, including removing it" is coherent; flag the alternative for the user. Class `Hydromancy_DryingBreeze`, command `"CommandDryingBreeze"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**. Reasoning: 3-turn cooldown, no damage, 9 cells, one status removed — it is a cantrip. Its real value is as the counter-pick against `PyroIgnition`'s wet-suppression (PyroIgnition.cs:56-63) and against `ElectrifiedEffect`'s wet amplification, which makes it a genuinely interesting Cost-1 tech card rather than filler.


## WardGleamMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/WardGleamMutation.cs (85 lines).

BASE CLASS: `BaseMutation` directly (line 5).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandWardGleam" (7). COOLDOWN = **15** (8). Registered `AddMyActivatedAbility(DisplayName, COMMAND, "Grimoire Spells", AbilityTargetingMode.SelfCentered, **0**)` (17-22) — range 0, which `ActivatedAbilitiesPart.AddAbility` silently clamps to 1 (`Range = range < 1 ? 1 : range`, ActivatedAbilitiesPart.cs:61). No damage, no dice, no RNG, no FX, no zone, no cell. It is the only one of the 18 whose `Cast()` takes **no arguments at all**.

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. `HandleEvent` (32-42): early-out unless `e.ID == COMMAND`; reads **no parameters whatsoever**; calls `Cast()` (37). On false returns true (unhandled) → InputHandler prints `"The rite fails to resolve."` (InputHandler.cs:3159) — **on top of** the mutation's own `"Nothing to cleanse."` line, so the player sees two failure messages. On true sets `e.Handled = true` (40); never sets `BlocksTurnAdvance`.
2. `Cast` (44-83): guards `ParentEntity` (46-47).
3. `InventoryPart inventory = ParentEntity.GetPart<InventoryPart>(); if (inventory == null) return false;` (49-51) — silent failure path with no message at all.
4. `var equipped = inventory.GetAllEquipped();` (54) → `InventoryPart.GetAllEquipped` (InventoryPart.cs:270-280) returns the de-duplicated values of `EquippedItems`. **Equipped only — carried-but-unequipped items are untouched** (pinned by the shipped test `WardGleam_SkipsUnequippedItems`, GrimoireSpellTests).
5. Per equipped item (55-73): `if (item.HasEffect<AcidicEffect>()) { item.RemoveEffect<AcidicEffect>(); removedFromItem = true; }` (60-64); `if (item.HasEffect<CharredEffect>()) { item.RemoveEffect<CharredEffect>(); removedFromItem = true; }` (66-70); `removedAny |= removedFromItem` (72). Exactly two effect types, hardcoded.
6. `if (!removedAny) { MessageLog.Add("Nothing to cleanse."); return false; }` (75-79) — **and no cooldown is charged.**
7. `CooldownMyActivatedAbility(ActivatedAbilityID, 15)` (81); return true.
8. **No success message.** A working cast produces nothing but the absence of two status effects on gear the player probably wasn't watching.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `Entity.GetPart<InventoryPart>` (49); `InventoryPart.GetAllEquipped` (54); `Entity.HasEffect`/`RemoveEffect` for `AcidicEffect` and `CharredEffect` (60-70); `MessageLog.Add` (77). Nothing else.

DIAG: **none.**

LEVEL: **not read.**

BROKEN PATH: **none of the four.** Real defects: (a) the double-failure message (own `"Nothing to cleanse."` plus the InputHandler's `"The rite fails to resolve."`); (b) **no success message** — the single worst feedback gap in the set; (c) the `inventory == null` path (49-51) is completely silent; (d) equipped-only scope is undocumented in any player-facing text; (e) the effect list is a hardcoded pair with no extension point, so a future item status (e.g. `BrokenEffect`, which the matrix already permits on objects — ObjectStatusMatrix.cs:96) will silently not be cleansed. SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: the port gains a behaviour change for free and must be handled deliberately — `SkillsPart.TryRouteSkillCommand` applies the cooldown **unconditionally** after `OnCommand` returns (SkillsPart.cs:284, `ability.CooldownRemaining = ability.MaxCooldown;`), so the "no cooldown on a wasted cast" mercy at line 75-79 disappears unless the skill explicitly zeroes it (the mechanism is documented at SkillsPart.cs:281-283: "If OnCommand wants to suppress the cooldown ... it can manually set ability.CooldownRemaining = 0 before returning"). Decide which semantic you want and write the test either way. Also add `EmitSkillRejectedDiag(ctx, "nothing_to_cleanse")` and `(ctx, "no_inventory")`.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS:
- failure: `"Nothing to cleanse."` (77) — note this is the only message in the set with no `<caster>` prefix.
- success: **none.**
- grimoire learn: `"The ink brightens to a cleansing sheen. You learn ward gleam."` / `"You already know ward gleam."`

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: **none.** No skill in any tree touches inventory or equipment status. The closest by shape is `Spellcraft_ArcaneSurge` (Spellcraft_ArcaneSurge.cs) — self-targeted, no zone interaction, pure state-cleanup on the caster: deltas — cooldown 15 vs Arcane Surge's **250** (Spellcraft.json:23) → **−235 turns**; scope: two effects on equipped items vs every activated ability's cooldown reset.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Spellcraft** (matches plan D3). Class `Spellcraft_WardGleam`, command `"CommandWardGleam"`. Recommended SP cost: **1**. Reasoning: Spellcraft is the universal/meta tree (root +1 spell damage, Empower +2, Arcane Surge, Ley Tap — Spellcraft.json) and a gear-cleansing ward is a coherent meta-utility that belongs to no element. There is a competing case for **Corrosion**, since `AcidicEffect` is Corrosion's signature status and Ward Gleam is its literal counter — but putting the counter inside the tree that applies it is self-defeating. Keep Spellcraft. At Cost 1 with CD 15 and a strictly defensive effect it will not distort the economy; the port's real work is the three missing messages and two missing diags, not the numbers.


## CalmMutation

FILE Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Mutations/CalmMutation.cs (72 lines). **One of the two mutations on the Player blueprint** (`StartingMutations = "FlamingHandsMutation:1,CalmMutation:1"`, Objects.json:872) — no grimoire teaches it.

BASE CLASS: `DirectionalProjectileMutationBase` (line 27). Full base machinery as in the KindleMutation dossier (DirectionalProjectileMutationBase.cs:30-167).

DECLARATION: COMMAND = "CommandCalm" (29). FxTheme = `AsciiFxTheme.Arcane` (33) — the docstring notes a dedicated "Mental" theme would fit better and is deferred (16-18). CooldownTurns = **20** (34) — the longest of the 18. AbilityRange = **6** (35). DamageDice = **"0"** (36) — `DiceRoller` returns 0 via its invalid-pattern fallthrough, and the base gates all damage handling on `damage > 0` (base:93), so **no damage is applied, no damage message is logged, and no `MutationDamage` diag is emitted** (this is pinned by the shipped test `CalmMutation_Cast_ZeroDamage_NoDamageLogLine_NoMutationDamageDiag_HpUnchanged`, CalmMutationTests). AbilityClass = **"Mental Mutations"** (37) — not "Grimoire Spells". ImpactVerb = "calms" (38, never used because damage is always 0). `ElementAttribute` not overridden → `""`. Public field `BaseDuration = 40` (49).

BEHAVIOUR ON CAST, STEP BY STEP:
1. Base `HandleEvent` reads params and traces; `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact` at range 6 (base:75-82); `AsciiFxBus.EmitProjectile(..., AsciiFxTheme.Arcane, trail: true, blocksTurnAdvance: true)` (base:87); `BlocksTurnAdvance = true` (base:65).
2. Damage block skipped entirely (roll = 0).
3. On-hit gate (base:147-152) — a creature with Hitpoints > 0 passes.
4. `ApplyOnHitEffect` (51-70): null-guard target (53); `var brain = target.GetPart<BrainPart>(); if (brain == null) return;` (54-55) — **silent bail on anything without a brain**, which is every object and every mindless entity.
5. Idempotence gate (61-65): `if (brain.HasGoal<NoFightGoal>()) { MessageLog.Add(target.GetDisplayName() + " is already at peace."); return; }`. Documented at 19-21: the cast is a no-op and does NOT extend the window, so chained casts cannot stack pacification. The cooldown is still consumed (the base applies it unconditionally at base:163) — the message exists so the player isn't confused by an apparently-silent cast (58-60).
6. `int duration = BaseDuration + (Level * 10);` (67) → **50 turns at Level 1**.
7. `brain.PushGoal(new NoFightGoal(duration, wander: false))` (68) → `NoFightGoal` ctor at NoFightGoal.cs:40-44; `CanFight() => false` (46); `Finished() => Duration > 0 && Age >= Duration` (49-52).
8. `MessageLog.Add(target.GetDisplayName() + " becomes peaceful.")` (69).
9. Base applies the 20-turn cooldown (base:163).

DOCUMENTED SIDE-EFFECT (22-25): `NoFightGoal` suppresses `AIBoredEvent` while active, so a pacified target's own `AISelfPreservationPart` won't fire to retreat even at low HP. Preserve this note in the ported class's docstring.

EXTERNAL SYSTEMS: `LineTargeting.TraceFirstImpact` (base:75); `AsciiFxBus.EmitProjectile` (base:87); `DiceRoller.Roll("0", rng)` (base:92, returns 0); `Entity.GetPart<BrainPart>` (54); `BrainPart.HasGoal<NoFightGoal>` (61); `BrainPart.PushGoal` (68). NOT called (because damage is 0): `MutationDamageHelpers.ApplySpellDamage`, `RouteDamage`. NOT called at all: `ObjectStatusMatrix`, `ZoneTileStateSystem`, `MaterialSimSystem`, any heat path.

DIAG: **none** on any path — the base's `MutationDamage` record is unreachable at 0 damage. There is no record for "pacified", "already at peace", or "target has no brain". The sibling dialogue trigger lives in `ConversationActions` (docstring 8-9).

LEVEL: **READ — 3 references** (6, 47, 67). One of only two of the 18. In live play `Level` is always 1 (see the FlamingHands dossier for the `BaseMutation.CalcLevel` walkthrough), so flatten to a **constant 50-turn duration** with zero balance drift. Note the shipped test `CalmMutation_LevelScalesDuration` (CalmMutationTests) asserts the scaling explicitly and must be rewritten or deleted, not silently dropped.

BROKEN PATH: **LineTargeting Wall/Terrain skip** (LineTargeting.cs:171) plus the corpse/loot priority (LineTargeting.cs:174). For Calm the Terrain-skip is nearly harmless (only creatures have brains) but the **corpse/loot priority is severe**: `GetFirstTargetableObject` accepts any `PhysicsPart` holder, so a dropped item or a corpse between the caster and the target ends the trace, `HitEntity` is the corpse, `GetPart<BrainPart>()` returns null, and the cast silently does nothing while consuming a 20-turn cooldown — with no message and no diag. Corpses-after-a-fight is the normal battlefield state (audit F12). SKILL-SIDE EQUIVALENT: `SkillLine.Collect(...)` collects **creatures first within each cell** (SkillLine.cs:110-114) before non-creatures (115-121), so a corpse in the same cell as a monster no longer wins — but a corpse in a NEARER cell still does. For a creature-only power, use `AbilityTargeting.IsCreatureTarget` (AbilityTargeting.cs:60-61 — "the stricter rule the weapon skills keep") to filter `SkillLine.Collect`'s output, or write a creature-only line walk. Then take `line[0]` and reject with `EmitSkillRejectedDiag(ctx, "no_creature_in_line")`.

MESSAGE-LOG STRINGS:
- success: `"<target> becomes peaceful."` (69)
- already pacified: `"<target> is already at peace."` (63)
- from the base, when nothing is hit: `"<caster>'s Calm dissipates harmlessly."` (base:160); when blocked: `"<caster>'s Calm splashes against an obstacle!"` (base:156)
- no message at all when the trace hits a brainless entity (the silent-failure path).
- grimoire: none — Player-blueprint grant only.

CLOSEST EXISTING SKILL: **`Persuasion_Dismiss` / `Persuasion_Recruit`** (Persuasion.json, Persuasion_Recruit.cs 14175 bytes, Persuasion_Dismiss.cs 4254 bytes) — the only social/AI-manipulation powers in the skill system. Deltas vs Recruit: range 6 line vs adjacent; no stat check vs `d20 + Ego mod vs their level` with `Attribute: "Ego", Minimum: "16"` gating (Persuasion.json:15-16); temporary 50-turn pacification vs permanent companion; cooldown 20 vs 25 → **−5 turns**; cost — Persuasion's rows are `Cost: 100` (root), `100` (Recruit), `25` (Dismiss) against a +1 SP/level grant (LevelingSystem.cs:71-73), i.e. deliberately near-unbuyable.

RECOMMENDED TARGET TREE: **Persuasion, NOT Spellcraft** — this is my one substantive disagreement with plan D3, which assigns Calm to Spellcraft. Reasoning: Calm is a non-damaging AI-state manipulation delivered at range; it shares its entire mechanical vocabulary (`BrainPart`, goals, hostility) with `Persuasion_Recruit`/`Persuasion_Dismiss` and shares nothing with Spellcraft's meta-magic identity (+spell damage, cooldown reset, HP-for-damage conversion). Putting it in Spellcraft makes Spellcraft "the tree for things that don't fit." Class `Persuasion_Calm`, command `"CommandCalm"`. Recommended SP cost: **1** — but note the tree's existing cost scale is 25/100, so either price Calm at 25 for consistency or (better) treat it as a Player-blueprint starting grant moved into `StartingSpellKit.SpellClasses` (StartingSpellKit.cs:48-54), which preserves today's behaviour exactly and sidesteps the cost question. If the user prefers to keep plan D3's assignment, `Spellcraft_Calm` at Cost 1 is workable; flag the choice explicitly rather than deciding silently.
